**Fresh protected-main frontier:** `6784e0e85c50fd86e3353b54a8b1964f045b65b1`
**Nominal migration:** `028_youtube_browser_transcript.sql`

## 1. Authority and non-authority

This addendum freezes the physical contract that must precede migration 028. It reconciles the governing goal, D-004 through D-016, the Stage 1 formal focused GO, the current migrations and SQLite runner, and R-032.

After an independent review returns GO, this addendum authorizes only implementation, packaging, and tests for the two named repository-owned source forks in §6.12.1—`@ai-brain/better-sqlite3-authority@11.10.0-brain.1` and source-only `@ai-brain/sqlite-vec-authority@0.1.9-brain.1`—packaged solely as their one attested native SQLite authority artifact. Migration schema/test implementation remains blocked until both forks, that artifact, every target first-layer attestation, and the native capability tests receive their own focused implementation GO. This addendum does **not** authorize:

- browser intent, inspect, grant, commit, or status routes;
- transcript attachment or processing-hold release;
- extension/mobile changes;
- live YouTube access or an isolated lab canary;
- held manual enrichment, provider dispatch, or production enablement;
- applying a migration to any shared, lab, or production database;
- deployment or rollback allowlisting.

Production browser-visible transcript capture and production processing of a held browser transcript remain denied. The external live-lab authorization packet remains absent.

## 3. Frozen Stage 2 decisions

### 3.1 Current single-user owner binding

The repository has local single-user PIN/session authentication, not an account table or stable user subject. The product phrase “Brain account” is implemented for this data foundation as a data-root-local owner scope:

1. A server-only 256-bit random value is created once as the setting `youtube_browser.owner_scope_v1` before the first fixture-only intent can be issued.
2. It is independent of session cookies, PIN signing-key rotation, bearer rotation, extension identity, request data, host/origin headers, and item content.
3. Authority rows persist only `owner_scope_hash = SHA-256("youtube-browser-owner-scope-v1\0" || raw_owner_scope)`.
4. The raw value is never returned, logged, placed in a URL, sent to the extension, or treated as a credential. It rotates only during an explicit data-root reinitialization that first invalidates all active browser authority.
5. Every intent, grant, policy decision, and terminal capture receipt must carry the same server-derived hash. A request cannot supply or select it.

This is deliberately not represented as a multi-account identifier. A future multi-user architecture requires a separate migration and review.

### 3.2 Authority lifetimes and cleanup

| Authority | Fixed lifetime | Consumption/invalidation | Inert-row cleanup |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Capture intent | exactly 30 minutes from `created_at` | terminal on commit, terminal conflict, cancel, revocation, or expiry; single terminal receipt maximum | terminal or expired mutable intent rows may be purged after 7 days when no grant remains in `state='issued'`; immutable receipt survives until item deletion |
| Inspect authorization | 60 seconds | one use; the live hash is cleared immediately on use, cancel, replacement, or terminal intent; a new authorization requires every gate to pass again | the mutable intent retains only content-free issued/consumed clocks, while the permanent issuance table retains the epoch-bound hash, generation, and issue/expiry clocks as inert never-reuse evidence for that live epoch |
| Upload grant | 120 seconds | one use; consumption and terminal receipt occur in the same transaction; revoke on intent cancellation/terminalization | expired, revoked, or consumed grant rows purge after 24 hours; receipt retains the inert grant hash for exact reconciliation |

Cleanup is not authority extension. Comparisons use the database clock supplied by the service transaction, enforce `now < expires_at`, and test the exact millisecond before, at, and after each boundary. No cleanup job may delete an active row or the only terminal receipt.

### 3.3 Secret and token storage

- Raw intent identifiers, inspect authorizations, upload grants, and claim tokens are generated with at least 256 bits of randomness.
- The database stores only lowercase 64-hex, domain-separated SHA-256 values.
- Raw claim tokens live only in the claiming process. Raw inspect/upload grants live only in their exact trusted extension channel.
- No raw token or hash is emitted to diagnostics, UI projections, reports, URLs, provider payloads, or usage rows.
- Every CAS compares the stored hash; the database never gains a raw `claim_token` column.
- Browser intent, inspect, and upload-grant hashes are bound to the current §6.7 authority epoch. Their v2 preimage is `UTF8(exact-v2-domain) || 0x00 || raw32(authority_epoch_id) || raw32(secret)`. The raw token envelope carries only its closed version, nonsecret epoch ID, and raw secret. Every endpoint rejects a wrong or retired epoch before hash lookup, DOM inspection, body read, or mutation.
- The detached backup-scrub attestation uses a separate 256-bit `backup_attestation_key_v1`, never the PIN/session/owner key. Before 028, an exact version is provisioned in two permission-restricted roles: one data-root-local signer and one independently recoverable verifier on the configured recovery surface. Neither is inside SQLite, an ordinary database backup, logs, reports, or the detached attestation. Only `backup_key_id_hash=SHA-256("brain-sensitive-backup-key-id-v1\0" || raw32(key))` and the authenticated provisioning-receipt digest are stored. The recovery role exposes verification and an authenticated install acknowledgement only; it never exports key bytes or exposes an HMAC/signing operation. Rotation is an explicit two-role operation: a version cannot sign until both roles, their store identities, the key-ID hash, a fixed verification challenge, and the append-only recovery-control-plane receipt agree. Both roles retain every prior verification version until every backup under it is explicitly retired. Backup publication requires the active local signer, independent recovery verification, and exact receipt agreement. A normal restore with the local data root present verifies both roles and fails on absence or disagreement.
- A declared data-root-loss restore uses the historical recovery version only to verify its input. Before creating any transformed-candidate scrub-plan row it must complete `declared_root_loss_signer_reprovision_v1`: under offline quarantine the local signer generates a fresh raw32 key/version, sends only a wrapped provisioning payload to the independent store, obtains a non-exportable verify-only install acknowledgement, signs the fixed typed challenge locally, and has recovery verify it. The recovery control plane then appends/fsyncs its authenticated provisioning receipt before the local version may become active. Only that fresh local version signs the output, and recovery independently verifies it. The historical recovery version is never installed locally or exposed through a signing endpoint.
- Provisioning follows the one-way operation-journal states `absent -> local_prepared -> recovery_verified -> receipt_committed -> active`. The content-free fsynced journal binds operation, input-evidence, new-key-ID, and receipt hashes. A pre-receipt failure leaves the version inactive and destroys or quarantines it; a post-receipt failure resumes only the exact recorded version/receipt. Publication before `active`, a second concurrent provisioning, or a mismatched replay fails. The lock subset is `global_maintenance -> backup_publication -> retired_ledger -> backup_signer_provisioning`; provisioning runs with no SQLite transaction open, never acquires an earlier lock while holding the last token, and releases `backup_signer_provisioning` only after receipt fsync and local activation. Loss of the local signer therefore does not make an otherwise valid historical backup unverifiable, but it always blocks a new output until this fresh edge completes.

### 3.4 R-032 closure strategy

Automatic transcript recovery must use one immediate transaction for current-authority validation, body/source apply, revision advance, derived reset, immutable recovery-apply receipt, attempt success, one idempotent provider-outcome/health update, and transcript-job finalization. A committed body can never coexist with a replayable recovery job.

The transaction is supplemented by an immutable `transcript_recovery_apply_receipts` row so restart reconciliation can distinguish “provider result never committed” from “commit succeeded and the process/response was lost.” Deterministic barriers are mandatory before the transaction, after each write with forced rollback, and after commit before process acknowledgement.

R-032 remains open until this schema and the corresponding worker transaction/tests pass. Stage 1’s GO does not waive it.

### 3.5 Filesystem artifact boundary

SQLite cannot atomically commit filesystem artifacts. The 028 automatic-recovery and browser-attachment transactions therefore create no fresh `capture_artifacts` files or rows and do not call `saveCaptureArtifacts`. Required source provenance, accepted text hash, and ordered segments are durable in the database transaction.

This deliberately removes the current automatic-recovery path’s best-effort post-commit artifact write from the success contract. Existing artifacts are preserved. A future artifact feature requires a separately reviewed, deterministic outbox/temp-file/fsync/rename/reconciliation design; it cannot be smuggled back into the R-032 transaction as a best-effort side effect.

### 3.6 Held enrichment generation belongs in 028

The Stage 2 goal requires atomic attachment, while the final manual-enrichment contract requires that attachment to create a unique nonclaimable held job generation. Deferring all generation identity to 029 would make a 028-only attachment internally incomplete. Therefore 028 owns only the minimum cross-stage generation foundation:

- `items.next_enrichment_generation` as a strictly increasing allocator;
- `items.current_applied_enrichment_generation` as the nullable current derived identity;
- one positive generation on every enrichment job;
- the exact held generation on the hold and capture receipt;
- the expected enrichment generation on new embedding jobs.

Migration 029 still owns authorization snapshots, interactive lanes/runs/attempts, provider/context fingerprints, retry semantics, and guarded hold release. No 028 claimant may process a held generation.

## 4. Migration-prefix and application guard

Before reading or applying SQL, a reusable guard in the migration runner must:

1. require every SQL filename to match `^[0-9]{3}_[A-Za-z0-9_]+\.sql$`;
2. group by the three-digit prefix;
3. permit duplicate 017 only for exactly `017_topics.sql` and `017_transcript_recovery.sql`;
4. permit duplicate 018 only for exactly `018_topics.sql` and `018_transcript_policy_sources.sql`;
5. reject every other duplicate, missing grandfathered member, added grandfathered member, malformed prefix, or case-conflicting filename before any migration transaction;
6. retain full-filename plus SHA-256 ledger authority; a prefix alone never identifies an applied migration.

The guard requires focused unit tests plus a clean replay of migrations 001–028. It must not rename either historical pair.

The same runner change must harden application concurrency and pragma restoration:

- each migration re-reads its ledger row inside an immediate transaction instead of trusting a process-start snapshot;
- concurrent migrators serialize before DDL and an already-applied exact row becomes a no-op;
- the prior `foreign_keys` and `legacy_alter_table` values are captured and restored in `finally`, including forced migration failure;
- a leaked `legacy_alter_table=1` is a test failure;
- collision checks cover every 028 table, scratch table, trigger, and index name before destructive DDL.

Migration 028 is a special closed runner edge, not another file passed to the legacy generic executor. After the static prefix/source-hash checks, it must enter the §6.12.1 `schema_027_to_028_transition_capability_attestation_v1` preflight/apply or exact-S28 no-op state machine. No ordinary connection, startup ledger snapshot, generic multi-statement `exec`, or second-layer-only S27 open may prepare the 028 file.

## 5. Required preflight before migration 028

Preflight is a read-only TypeScript step run against the stopped-writer database before backup acceptance or DDL. It emits counts and content-free codes only. Any failure leaves schema and `_migrations` unchanged.

### 5.1 Operational prerequisites

- all content writers and standalone backfills are stopped;
- the exact S27/S28 standalone retention containment package, v2 scrubber, clock-high-water service, artifact/metadata locks, and both supervised units are prepositioned and execution-proven under the migration lock, and a current worst-case `retention-budget-v1` profile fits the absolute ceilings; application DDL is forbidden before this latch;
- no `running` transcript or enrichment job exists, and no nonterminal legacy embedding job exists. Because 028 cannot invent a current applied-enrichment generation for an old embedding request, every legacy pending/retryable/running embedding row must be drained or removed by a separate operator-reviewed cleanup artifact before migration; preserved terminal rows remain inert with null generation;
- no `batched` enrichment item/job, unresolved `opaque-reservation-v1:` marker, or provider batch result remains in flight;
- a fresh backup has been created, opened, integrity-checked, and rehearsed on a disposable production-shaped copy;
- `PRAGMA integrity_check`, the stable pre-existing `foreign_key_check` manifest, table row counts, stable row hashes, indexes, triggers, and migration ledger are captured;
- applied migration names and hashes through 027 match the packaged source set;
- `sqlite_sequence` high-water marks for every rebuilt AUTOINCREMENT table are captured and later preserved even when the current maximum row ID is lower.

### 5.2 Ownership and provenance prerequisites

Preflight must fail, without auto-repair, when any of the following exists:

- any `items.id IS NULL`, duplicate item identity, or row that cannot be copied into `id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL` without invention;
- more than one `transcript_sources.status='active'` row for an item;
- a transcript source whose referenced policy has a null `capture_policy_decisions.item_id`, an item ID different from the source item, or a non-null `blocked_reason` despite being used as positive source authority;
- a transcript segment whose `item_id` differs from its source’s `item_id`;
- a transcript attempt whose `item_id` differs from its job’s `item_id`;
- a non-null `transcript_jobs.last_attempt_id` that is missing or names an attempt for another job or item;
- a transcript job with `attempts < 0`, `max_attempts <= 0`, `state IN ('pending','retryable_error') AND attempts >= max_attempts`, or any other claim/completion/schedule shape that cannot satisfy §6.5 without inventing history;
- any source or segment with a missing/non-lowercase-64-hex required hash, and any active source with provenance that fails its exact legacy/versioned validator or a text hash that cannot be proven to match the current `items.body` under the applicable frozen normalization contract;
- any nonterminal transcript job whose current item does not classify exactly as `weak_metadata_capture` under §5.2.1, or any terminal job shape that falsely claims a current source/body authority the classifier cannot reproduce;
- an unknown method, source-kind, status, timestamp, retention, queue-state, extraction-method, or capture-quality literal;
- a body-derived queue row that cannot be bound to its current item.
- any `chunks_vec.rowid` without exactly one `chunks_rowid` bridge to a canonical chunk, or any bridge/chunk ownership mismatch. Migration 028 never guesses which item owns an orphan vector; a separately reviewed global orphan-purge artifact is required before preflight can pass.
- any wrong SQLite storage class in a copied identity, clock, count, Boolean, hash, enum, FK, or authority field used by the 028 contract, even if SQLite coercion would make a value/range expression truthy.

No source kind, caption class, policy, owner, revision, provider, or provenance is inferred from current configuration. A mismatch requires a separate operator-reviewed cleanup artifact; 028 never silently supersedes, relabels, or repairs it.

Legacy active sources proven to equal the current body bind to the new current revision. Non-active historical sources whose old revision cannot be proven retain a nullable `accepted_content_revision` and are ineligible for current processing.

“Versioned provenance” is not retroactively required of rows written by schema-027 code that never emitted a schema-version field. The preflight has a closed legacy validator registry keyed by the already stored `source_kind` and exact writer shape:

| Legacy kind | Frozen validator authority |
|---|---|
| `user_paste` / `uploaded_file` | `provenanceForPaste` / `provenanceForFile` in `user-provided.ts` SHA-256 `9b41fe6ec7524b6ace6f5155da85c443984418a8bad2576b62979fe975e8db8f` |
| `youtube_official_caption` | `provenanceForOfficialCaption` in `youtube-official.ts` SHA-256 `17dd0568eaa5af9c316bfaf8c16c0af5a3c9e3c7c92838c343f1c9e634b836bf` |
| `owned_media_stt` | `provenanceForOwnedMediaStt` in `owned-media-stt.ts` SHA-256 `6d48f3f7e808345e7032fd9036822cef4d07881ad22eae279be1574e4611a487` |
| `lab_public_caption` | no packaged 027 source writer is authoritative; any retained row blocks 028 pending an explicit operator-reviewed provenance artifact |

Each legacy validator enforces `json_valid`, exact key membership/types from that writer, policy-ID and retention equality, numeric/Boolean bounds, and source-kind/caption/timestamp compatibility without rewriting a byte. For active rows it also requires SHA-256 of the exact current UTF-8 `items.body`, exact segment row count, contiguous ordinals where segments exist, per-segment hashes, the canonical duration/end relation below, and a reproducible `transcript-integrity-v1`; this verification supplies revision/integrity backfill but does not relabel the legacy JSON or invent a normalization version. Copied rows retain `provenance_schema=NULL`.

Every post-028 insert instead selects exactly one closed validator through its non-null immutable `provenance_schema`:

| Source kind | Exact schema token and shape authority |
|---|---|
| `user_paste` | `user-paste-provenance-v1`; exact `provenanceForPaste` key/type shape pinned above, plus canonical fixed-key serialization and current source/policy/count equality |
| `uploaded_file` | `uploaded-file-provenance-v1`; exact `provenanceForFile` key/type shape pinned above, plus canonical fixed-key serialization and current source/policy/count equality |
| `youtube_official_caption` | `youtube-official-caption-provenance-v1`; exact `provenanceForOfficialCaption` key/type shape pinned above, plus canonical fixed-key serialization and current source/policy/video/count equality |
| `owned_media_stt` | `owned-media-stt-provenance-v1`; exact `provenanceForOwnedMediaStt` key/type shape pinned above, plus canonical fixed-key serialization and current source/policy/count equality |
| `browser_visible_transcript` | exact `youtube-browser-provenance-v1` contract in §6.3 |
| `lab_public_caption` automatic recovery | exact `youtube-recovery-provenance-v1` contract in §6.5.1 |

No post-028 row can select a null/unknown token, a token for another source kind, or the looser migration-only legacy validator. Each named insert trigger rejects duplicate/extra/missing keys, wrong JSON types, noncanonical bytes, and disagreement with separately stored source/policy/segment facts.

The four non-browser manual writers also have a closed current-body contract; a policy/source/revision match is never sufficient by itself. For `user_paste`, canonical source text is exactly the UTF-8 output of the pinned `normalizePastedTranscriptText` path above, its second `repairItemWithText` CRLF/trim normalization is required to be byte-idempotent, and the post-028 source has `segment_count=0`. For `uploaded_file` and `youtube_official_caption`, canonical source text is exactly the pinned parser's nonempty ordered segment texts joined by two LF bytes (`\n\n`) with no leading/trailing separator. `owned_media_stt` uses the same two-LF ordered-segment rendering from its pinned validated transcript. In all four cases the committed `items.body` is exactly that canonical source text, and `transcript_sources.text_sha256` is `sha256_utf8(items.body)` over those exact bytes. For the three segmented kinds, the ordered segment assembly must also equal the body byte-for-byte and reproduce `segment_count`, every segment hash, and `integrity_sha256`. The named source-insert and source-resolution guards use the deterministic SQL digest contract in §6.12; a same-revision body/source/hash/segment disagreement aborts the complete writer transaction rather than merely refusing the optional job update.

#### 5.2.1 Total current-body authority classifier

The informal terms “weak”, “recovery candidate”, “transcript-bearing”, “legacy”, and “source-less” are not independent predicates. Migration 028 creates one versioned, total classifier named `youtube-body-authority-classifier-v1`; preflight, enqueue/backfill, claim, dispatch, retry/rearm, generic user-text replacement, browser commit, provider finalization, startup sweep, and tests must all call the same implementation. No caller may reproduce only its marker test.

The classifier is exposed to SQLite through the exact schema-owned view `youtube_body_authority_v1(item_id,content_revision,authority_class)`. Its view SQL uses one closed `CASE` in the precedence below plus the deterministic scalar `youtube_legacy_body_class_v1(title,author,body,source_url,duration_seconds,published_at,description,capture_quality,extraction_method,extraction_warning,extraction_version)`. The scalar returns exactly `weak_metadata_placeholder|transcript_bearing|ambiguous`, never null and never throws content into an error message. B28 registers it on every application, migration, preflight, backup, restore, maintenance, and script connection before schema-028 SQL can be prepared; migration 028 probes golden vectors before DDL. The packaged TypeScript classifier queries this view rather than carrying a second branch table. Exact normalized view SQL, the scalar registration/version, and all golden vectors are part of the schema capability descriptor.

`youtube_legacy_body_class_v1` is pinned to `src/lib/capture/youtube-body.ts` SHA-256 `b68ab865d208abe50a35c103cbb8d449a23f44c38450199ed807ce9aa78984bc` and reproduces `buildYoutubeBody` byte-for-byte from only the supplied stored fields. It returns `weak_metadata_placeholder` only when all supplied storage classes/ranges are canonical, `capture_quality='metadata_only'`, and one of these exact legacy/current writer shapes rerenders to the supplied body byte-for-byte:

| Extraction method | Warning | Extraction version | Exact transcript argument used for rerender |
|---|---|---|---|
| `youtube_innertube_timedtext` | `no_transcript` | `legacy|capture-v0.7.5` | null, producing exactly `[No transcript available for this video]` |
| `youtube_innertube_timedtext` | `youtube_transcript_fetch_metadata_only` | `capture-v0.7.5` | the one body-extracted placeholder `[Transcript unavailable: R]`, where `R` is 1–256 Unicode scalars, contains no NUL/CR/LF/`[`/`]`, and is exactly `Timed-text returned ` followed by three ASCII digits or begins `Timed-text fetch failed: ` with at least one remaining scalar |
| `youtube_oembed_metadata` | `youtube_antibot_metadata_only` | `legacy|capture-v0.7.5` | exactly `[Transcript unavailable: YouTube blocked AI Memory's server transcript request with an anti-bot sign-in check.]` |

For the middle row, extraction of `R` is only a way to reproduce the historical renderer; the complete rerender must still equal the body bytes, including metadata, spacing, chapter derivation, and the single terminal placeholder. A second `Transcript:` section, extra suffix/prefix, alternate line ending, invalid ISO date, noncanonical duration, noncanonical stored URL, or any metadata disagreement returns `ambiguous`. The stored URL must be exactly `https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V`, where `V` is the one server-parsed eleven-character video ID; the item must have `source_platform IN ('youtube','youtube_short')`, and `source_type='youtube'`. A Shorts-classified item still stores that canonical watch URL. The scalar returns `transcript_bearing` when any exact strong signal is present—`capture_quality IN ('transcript','metadata_plus_transcript','user_provided_full_text')`, `extraction_method IN ('youtube_innertube_timedtext','youtube_user_provided_text','manual_repair_transcript','youtube_official_caption','owned_media_stt','browser_visible_transcript')`, or `extraction_warning='transcript_truncated_2h'`—and the tuple is not one of the weak shapes above. Every other tuple/body combination is `ambiguous`; it is never made weak merely because the old broad predicate would have matched one marker.

The view then selects exactly one class in this order:

1. `not_youtube` when the item does not satisfy `source_type='youtube' OR source_platform IN ('youtube','youtube_short')`.
2. When one or more active sources exist, `active_source_current` only for exactly one active source whose current-revision, policy, provenance, terminal evidence, segment/integrity, hold/retention, and kind-specific body relation all pass their exact validators; otherwise `invalid_current_authority_graph`.
3. With no active source or hold, `current_generic_user_text` only for exactly one current-revision `generic_user_text` transition, its trigger-owned body application, and its immutable generic receipt whose item/revision/body hash/marker/renderer tuple equals the current item, with no current non-generic terminal evidence.
4. With no active source, any current non-generic body transition or content-accepting evidence, malformed/partial current generic graph, or active hold is `invalid_current_authority_graph`.
5. With no active source/hold/current transition/current content-accepting evidence and no source, segment, manual-seal, attached browser-receipt, recovery-receipt, or generic-receipt history, `weak_metadata_capture` only when the scalar returns `weak_metadata_placeholder`.
6. With no active source/current transition, `source_less_strong_content` when the scalar returns `transcript_bearing` or any retained source, segment, manual seal, attached browser receipt, recovery receipt, or generic receipt history exists.
7. Every remaining YouTube row is `source_less_ambiguous`.

These branches are exhaustive and mutually exclusive. A browser `outcome='conflict'` receipt is immutable request evidence but is explicitly not content-accepting evidence: it does not change the classifier, so a note conflict or prior strong/ambiguous conflict cannot relabel a weak/ambiguous body. Preflight evaluates and records content-free counts for every class and fails on `invalid_current_authority_graph`; `source_less_strong_content` and `source_less_ambiguous` remain readable but confer no body-mutation or recovery authority. A nonterminal transcript job is permitted only with `weak_metadata_capture`; cleanup of an old nonterminal job in another class requires a separately reviewed operator artifact and is not invented by migration 028. A new post-028 YouTube item is accepted only through the packaged capture insert that first obtains the same scalar result and supplies the exact canonical item tuple; the insert guard repeats the marker/platform/URL constraints, while repository tests prove no other production insert path can create a weak candidate.

Weak-body authority cannot be invalidated by a same-revision metadata rewrite. A named `BEFORE UPDATE OF title,author,duration_seconds,published_at,description ON items` guard selects the current view row and, while it is `weak_metadata_capture`, aborts any null-safe actual change to any of those five body-renderer inputs. There is no exception flag, transient token, or enrichment-specific bypass. `source_url` and the extraction marker tuple remain covered by the existing identity/body-transition guards, and all four authorized body transitions already preserve these renderer fields byte-for-byte. Therefore a weak body either remains reproducibly weak at its revision or changes only through a complete reviewed body transition.

Every synchronous, direct-route, and batch enrichment finalizer opens `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` and selects the exact classifier row plus its enrichment job generation, expected revision, claim or batch binding, and current item state. For `weak_metadata_capture` it uses a distinct SQL statement that **omits `title` from the update column list**, applies only the permitted summary/quotes/category/tags/topics and generation-bound terminal projection, discards the model-proposed title, returns the stored title, and reselects/asserts the class is still weak before commit. Omitting the column, rather than assigning `OLD.title`, also prevents an `UPDATE OF title` FTS trigger from firing. For a nonweak item the ordinary title update remains available subject to its exact generation/revision/claim/hold CAS. If enrichment serializes first, title/body/revision/class stay unchanged and the transcript job remains valid. If any complete body transition serializes first, its revision/generation/hold changes make the old enrichment result no-effect; only the rearmed generation may apply. A same-revision nonweak nonterminal job is physically unreachable and receives no invented cleanup disposition.

The lifecycle mapping is closed:

| Authority class | Generic user-text service | Browser commit | Automatic recovery / enqueue / claim / dispatch / retry |
|---|---|---|---|
| `weak_metadata_capture` | may enter the exact generic transition | may enter `attached` | eligible, subject to every other policy/runtime/retention gate |
| `active_source_current` | reject | ineligible before body access; a valid post-stream race selects `existing_transcript_conflict`, while same-request reconciliation returns its existing attached receipt | ineligible |
| `current_generic_user_text` | reject | immutable `existing_item_text_conflict` receipt, no content/note mutation | ineligible, including stale rearm/backfill |
| `source_less_strong_content` | reject | immutable `legacy_provenance_conflict` receipt, no content/note mutation | ineligible |
| `source_less_ambiguous` | reject | immutable `ambiguous_provenance_conflict` receipt, no content/note mutation | ineligible |
| `invalid_current_authority_graph` | containment error `youtube_authority_graph_invalid`, no receipt or authority consumption | same containment error before body selection/consumption | fenced |
| `not_youtube` | reject | reject before intent authority | ineligible |

For browser commit, the three source-less conflict results are stable terminal no-mutation outcomes after an otherwise valid grant; they precede note inspection and therefore always use `note_action='none'`. No source-less class can fall through to `attached` because a marker happens to equal `metadata_only`. Golden matrices cross every class with source count, current/historical transition and evidence presence, all marker tuples, canonical/noncanonical body and URL, job state, hold state, browser note choice, and the exact revision boundaries; the TypeScript/view result and every lifecycle disposition must agree.

### 5.3 Post-migration equivalence

After applying 028 on a disposable snapshot:

- the pre-existing `foreign_key_check` manifest is exactly preserved;
- affected-table counts and stable legacy hashes are preserved;
- every new cross-item substitution fails;
- `integrity_check` is `ok`;
- applying 028 again is a no-op with the same ledger hash;
- a fault after every DDL/data-copy step rolls back the complete migration and ledger row.

## 6. Exact physical object contract

All epoch values are integer milliseconds. Every stored hash below is lowercase 64-hex. IDs are opaque random UUID/ULID-style values unless the field is explicitly a hash. Within every new-table column list or table below, a field is physically `NOT NULL` unless it is explicitly marked nullable; every text primary key is declared `PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL`. Every new ordinary 028 table is `STRICT`; inability of a supported runtime to create, migrate, back up, or restore that exact shape is a migration blocker, not permission to weaken it. Rebuilt legacy tables may remain non-`STRICT` only to preserve historical storage, but every new or authority-bearing text/integer/Boolean field has an explicit stored-class check (`typeof(x)='text'`, `typeof(x)='integer'`, or the same under `x IS NULL OR ...`) in addition to its value/range check. SQLite applies column affinity before `CHECK` and `BEFORE` triggers, so the database contract is deliberately about the resulting stored class, not an unobservable pre-affinity bind type: a coercible `'1'` bound to an `INTEGER` column may become canonical integer `1`, while a value that does not become the required stored class/shape aborts. Every packaged repository method additionally validates the JavaScript/JSON input type before binding and rejects coercible wrong-type caller input; raw SQL with semantically equivalent coercion is outside the packaged caller boundary but still cannot persist a noncanonical class or value. Every trigger repeats stored-class validation for copied legacy fields that participate in identity, equality, clock, count, Boolean, hash, FK, or authority decisions. Preflight rejects a historical wrong-storage-class value rather than relying on SQLite coercion. Rebuilt-table legacy exceptions remain nullable only where this addendum says so explicitly.

### 6.1 Item revision and generation columns

Add:

| Column | Contract |
|---|---|
| `content_revision` | `INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1 CHECK(content_revision > 0)` |
| `next_enrichment_generation` | `INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1 CHECK(next_enrichment_generation > 0)` |
| `current_applied_enrichment_generation` | nullable positive integer and always lower than `next_enrichment_generation` |

Triggers must provide these exact semantics:

- every `UPDATE OF body` advances the revision exactly once, including a byte-identical assignment;
- if the writer leaves the revision unchanged, an after-update trigger sets it to `OLD.content_revision + 1`;
- if a transactional writer explicitly supplies the revision, only `OLD.content_revision + 1` is accepted and the after-update trigger does not increment again;
- decreases, jumps, nulls, zero, overflow, and two increments in one statement abort;
- updates that do not include `body` do not advance the revision;
- inserts begin at revision 1. A named `BEFORE INSERT` guard rejects every explicit value other than 1; the default is not the only enforcement.
Every item rebuild after 028 must explicitly preserve these columns and both revision guards. Trigger tests cover ordinary update, byte-identical update, explicit `OLD+1`, invalid jump, rollback, FTS interaction, concurrent writers, and every dependent FK/trigger.

Migration 028 rebuilds the item root with `id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL`. After the data copy and before application writers resume, a named `BEFORE INSERT ON items` guard aborts whenever `NEW.id` already exists, before SQLite resolves `OR REPLACE`, `ON CONFLICT`, or any other conflict algorithm; a named `BEFORE UPDATE OF id` guard rejects every identity change. Application code that needs idempotent create must select and classify the existing root before inserting rather than using a root-table conflict algorithm. These guards are tested with `recursive_triggers` both off and on, and must preserve the complete old item/evidence/retention/FTS graph after an attempted same-ID `INSERT OR REPLACE`.

For this contract, a row is YouTube-classified exactly when `source_type='youtube' OR source_platform IN ('youtube','youtube_short')`. Every item body, identity, and marker trigger applies when **either** `OLD` or `NEW` satisfies that predicate; a writer cannot escape a guard by reclassifying in the same statement. Once a migrated or newly inserted row is YouTube-classified, `id`, `source_type`, `source_platform`, and `source_url` are byte-immutable for its lifetime. A named `BEFORE UPDATE OF source_type,source_platform,source_url` guard rejects a one- or multi-statement transition into, out of, or between YouTube identities whenever any of those three values changes. The dedicated generic service may validate and derive the canonical video identity server-side, but it must preserve these stored identity bytes; a noncanonical or ambiguous stored URL is a no-mutation identity-repair error, not permission to rewrite identity alongside content.

For an existing YouTube/YouTube Short body, the authorization guard runs as a named `BEFORE UPDATE OF body` trigger. It requires the selected transition's expected/applied revisions to equal `OLD.content_revision` and `OLD.content_revision+1`, and permits only `NEW.content_revision=OLD.content_revision` for the common implicit-increment path or `NEW.content_revision=OLD.content_revision+1` for the explicit transactional path. The common revision machinery then leaves the stored row at exactly the transition's applied revision: the after-update increment owns only the first shape and is a no-op for the second. Terminal evidence is inserted after the update statement and rechecks that stored revision. No relative ordering between multiple `AFTER` triggers is used as authority.

The authority-bearing marker tuple is `extraction_method`, `capture_quality`, `extraction_warning`, and `extraction_version`. A named `BEFORE UPDATE OF` those fields applies under the same `OLD OR NEW` classifier and rejects every actual marker change unless the transaction has the one fresh same-item body transition for `OLD.content_revision -> OLD.content_revision+1`, the transition's complete applied marker tuple equals `NEW`, and its body digest equals `sha256_utf8(NEW.body)`. Every successful transition in the closed registry requires `applied_extraction_warning IS NULL`; metadata-only/recovery warnings cannot survive or be introduced by a successful body transition. The transition's deferred body-application and terminal-evidence relations then require an actual `UPDATE OF body` plus the applied item revision, so a marker-only statement, a marker change followed by an unrelated revision edit, or a pair-only backfill cannot commit. A marker update cannot use a historical transition or receipt. Enqueue, claim, dispatch, retry, and apply guards independently exclude a current-revision `generic_user_text` transition plus exact generic receipt; they do not infer safety only from the mutable marker tuple.

Generation rules:

- every post-028 item insert begins at `content_revision=1`, `next_enrichment_generation=1`, and null applied generation; the recreated `AFTER INSERT` enqueue trigger first inserts the one enrichment singleton at generation 1/expected revision 1 and then advances the stored allocator to 2 inside that same trigger program. A named allocator guard admits this bootstrap edge only for the just-inserted item with that exact job and otherwise rejects it; application inserts cannot supply a different allocator or generation;
- every one of the four authorized existing-item body-transition families takes `OLD.next_enrichment_generation` as a fresh generation, increments the allocator exactly once, clears `current_applied_enrichment_generation`, and rearms the singleton enrichment job for that exact applied revision/generation in the same transaction;
- a browser attachment binds that newly allocated generation to its hold and terminal receipt; the other three families leave the rearmed generation pending and claimable only after all ordinary gates pass;
- body replacement never resets or decrements the allocator, even after rollback/retry reconciliation; only a rolled-back transaction may leave the candidate number unconsumed because it created no durable transition;
- a successful current enrichment sets the applied identity to its exact job generation;
- a stale/failed job cannot change it and a generation is never reused;
- legacy jobs receive deterministic positive generations during migration, `next_enrichment_generation` seeds above every retained job value, and existing derived output remains legacy/unknown rather than receiving invented current-generation provenance.

Named guards permit `next_enrichment_generation` to advance only by the exact insert bootstrap above or by one in a complete body-transition transaction, and permit `current_applied_enrichment_generation` to change only to null in that reset or from null to the exact current successful enrichment generation. Each terminal body-evidence trigger verifies the resulting allocator, applied-generation nullness, and exact singleton job generation/revision; no family may implement a different allocation rule.

#### 6.1.1 Immutable `youtube_item_body_transitions`

Every post-028 body update of an **existing** YouTube/YouTube Short item requires one preinserted, durable, exact transition row; absence of a source/hold is not write authority. The exact physical column order is:

```text
transition_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL, fresh bounded opaque ID
item_id TEXT NOT NULL
expected_content_revision INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK > 0
applied_content_revision INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK = expected_content_revision + 1
transition_kind TEXT NOT NULL exact generic_user_text|manual_source|browser_attachment|automatic_recovery
applied_extraction_method TEXT NOT NULL bounded reviewed token
applied_capture_quality TEXT NOT NULL bounded reviewed token
applied_extraction_warning TEXT nullable; exact null for every permitted transition
applied_extraction_version TEXT NOT NULL bounded reviewed token
body_sha256 TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
body_utf16_code_units INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
reset_body_chunk_count INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
reset_body_vector_count INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
reset_item_topic_count INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
reset_auto_tag_count INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
reset_semantic_purge_count INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
prior_taxonomy_epoch INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
manual_evidence_seal_id TEXT nullable
browser_receipt_id TEXT nullable
recovery_receipt_id TEXT nullable
generic_receipt_id TEXT nullable
created_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
```

Add `UNIQUE(item_id,applied_content_revision)`, an immediate item-root FK, and one unique partial index on each terminal-evidence ID where that ID is non-null. Separately add the four **non-partial** composite `UNIQUE` parent keys enumerated in §6.11; a partial unique index is never used as a SQLite FK parent. A named shape guard supplies `MATCH FULL` behavior and requires exactly one evidence ID: `manual_source` selects only a manual seal; `browser_attachment` only an attached browser receipt; `automatic_recovery` only a recovery-apply receipt; `generic_user_text` only a generic body-update receipt. Each selected ID participates in the exact deferred terminal-evidence FK in §6.11; the three unselected IDs are null. All columns are immutable, direct deletion while the item exists aborts, and insert guards reject ID/revision/evidence collisions before `REPLACE` conflict handling.

The owning service inserts this row before superseding any old source or changing the body. Its insert guard samples/advances the retention clock; requires an existing YouTube/YouTube Short item, `expected_content_revision=items.content_revision`, `applied_content_revision=items.content_revision+1`, and `created_at` equal to that clock; snapshots the exact body-dependent chunk/vector, item-topic, auto-tag, missing semantic-purge, and current taxonomy-epoch counts; and rejects any supplied count mismatch. It also rejects the transition when any retained source-history `retention_cleanup_start_by <= created_at`, any same-item obligation has entered a purge phase, or any retention error/clock latch exists. The item-wide retention window cannot be bypassed by changing source kind or attaching evidence after a previous update. The source-status guard permits supersession only when a fresh same-item `manual_source` transition exists and the old source is the exact current unheld source; the other three transition kinds require no active source and cannot supersede one. The body-update guard then requires exactly one transition whose expected/applied revisions have the two allowed shapes above, whose body hash equals `sha256_utf8(NEW.body)`, whose item-body character count equals `utf16_code_units(NEW.body)`, whose complete extraction method/quality/warning/version tuple equals `NEW`, and whose explicitly preserved item fields equal `OLD`; it also requires the exact kind-specific current item/source/hold/job predicates.

The applied marker registry is closed: every branch has null warning and a nonempty bounded reviewed extraction version; `generic_user_text` is exactly `youtube_user_provided_text`/`user_provided_full_text`; `manual_source` is `manual_repair_transcript`/`user_provided_full_text` for `user_paste|uploaded_file`, `youtube_official_caption`/`metadata_plus_transcript` for that source kind, or `owned_media_stt`/`metadata_plus_transcript` for that source kind; `browser_attachment` is exactly `browser_visible_transcript`/`transcript`; and `automatic_recovery` is exactly `youtube_innertube_timedtext`/`metadata_plus_transcript`. The kind-specific terminal evidence guard proves the applicable extraction version from the exact renderer/parser/extractor provenance rather than trusting the transition alone. The terminal manual seal, browser receipt, recovery receipt, or generic receipt is inserted only after the body/evidence writes and must point back through `body_transition_id=transition_id`. If the body never changes, its terminal evidence trigger cannot satisfy the future applied revision; if the body changes without terminal evidence, the transition's deferred FK fails at commit. Thus a body-only update, marker-only update, evidence-only insert, abandoned preauthorization, wrong authority kind/marker tuple, due retained deadline, and cross-transaction replay all fail closed.

Historical transitions are immutable audit evidence until item-root deletion. Their `applied_content_revision` makes them unusable for a later body update, and terminal evidence IDs are never reused. This contract authenticates packaged application transitions and is paired with repository scans; arbitrary operator control of the database/schema remains outside the application caller boundary.

#### Immutable body-statement application proof

Marker and revision guards must distinguish a real `UPDATE OF body` statement from a marker-only statement that explicitly increments `content_revision`. Create `youtube_item_body_transition_applications` with the following exact all-non-null immutable physical column order:

```text
transition_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL
item_id TEXT NOT NULL
expected_content_revision INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK > 0
applied_content_revision INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK = expected_content_revision + 1
body_sha256 TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
body_utf16_code_units INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
extraction_method TEXT NOT NULL
capture_quality TEXT NOT NULL
extraction_version TEXT NOT NULL
observed_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
```

The one named `AFTER UPDATE OF body` authority trigger first inserts this row from the exact selected transition/`NEW` tuple and then, only for the implicit shape, advances `content_revision` to the transition's applied revision. For the explicit shape the row is already at that revision and no second increment runs. These two statements are ordered inside one trigger program; authority does not depend on relative order among separate `AFTER` triggers. Add an immediate exact application→transition FK and a deferred exact transition→application reverse FK, both over non-partial parent keys. The application insert guard requires the same item, expected/applied revisions, body hash/count, complete marker tuple with null item warning, and transition clock; all fields are immutable, ID/natural-key `REPLACE` collisions abort before conflict handling, direct deletion while the item exists is denied, and item-root cascade removes both sides.

Every manual seal, attached browser receipt, recovery receipt, and generic receipt requires the exact application row in addition to its transition. A non-body update cannot create the trigger-owned row, so a marker-only update, a direct explicit revision increment, or a two-statement marker/revision sequence leaves the transition's deferred application relation unsatisfied and cannot commit even if it fabricates otherwise matching terminal evidence. Production repository scans permit inserts into this table only in the migration-owned body trigger; direct insert APIs or raw statements are rejected. Arbitrary schema-level operator control remains outside the application caller boundary.

#### 6.1.2 One common successful derived reset

Every successful `generic_user_text|manual_source|browser_attachment|automatic_recovery` body transition executes this one closed reset under the owning `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; a family-specific approximation is forbidden. Before the first destructive reset write, the service requires `items.batch_id IS NULL`, no `enrichment_jobs.state='batched'`, and no unresolved Stage 1 `opaque-reservation-v1:` reservation or provider batch result. A normal token/lease-bound enrichment or embedding worker may be preempted only by the exact revision/generation CAS below; its late result is then no-effect. An opaque external batch reservation cannot be preempted and rejects the body transition.

For the transitioning item, the reset is exactly:

1. Enumerate every body-dependent `item_semantic_events` indexed tuple whose `source_kind IN ('legacy_item_context','original_content','ai_summary')` and that lacks a matching `purged` action. Before deleting any chunk, insert exactly one content-free purge event for each tuple at the sampled transition clock, with its ID deterministically derived by the §6.12 typed framing of domain `youtube-body-reset-semantic-purge-v1`, transition ID, source kind, epoch, and version. Preserve the immutable event history and every `manual_note` semantic event.
2. Delete `chunks_vec` rows selected through `chunks_rowid` only for those three body-dependent source kinds, verify no selected vector remains, then delete those `chunks` rows so their `chunks_rowid` bridges cascade. Preserve `manual_note` chunks/bridges/vectors, `vector_rowid_sequence`, and every canonical item-note table because they derive from user-authored note state, not the replaced body.
3. Delete every `item_topics` link for the item and every `item_tags` link whose joined `tags.kind='auto'`. Preserve the shared topic/tag rows, every manual tag link, every collection link, cards, chat history, canonical `item_note_state|item_notes|item_note_revisions|item_note_mutations|note_ai_provider_consents|item_notes_fts|note_index_jobs`, capture artifacts, Recall sync rows, NotebookLM request snapshots/events, workflow projection/events/receipts/undo/enrollment/preferences state, and unrelated usage/audit history byte-for-byte. This preservation list does not include the body-derived `items_fts`, `enrichment_jobs`, or `embedding_jobs`, whose exact reset behavior is separately specified in steps 4, 6, and 7. The existing taxonomy triggers advance `processing_runtime_state.taxonomy_epoch` exactly once per deleted link and set its `updated_at` from the final such delete; when no link is deleted both remain unchanged. `workflow_epoch`, schema/readiness/failure state, deep-audit timestamps/hashes, and every other runtime-state field remain byte-identical.
4. Delete the item's singleton `embedding_jobs` row, including a stale claim. No replacement embedding job exists at body-transition commit; only a later successful enrichment of the exact new generation may enqueue one with `expected_content_revision=transition.applied_content_revision` and `expected_enrichment_generation` equal to that generation. A late old embedding result has no row/revision/generation CAS and is no-effect.
5. In the one authorized item body update, set `summary=quotes=category=enriched_at=batch_id=current_applied_enrichment_generation=NULL`, set `enrichment_state='pending'`, set the exact closed marker tuple and new body, set `total_chars=utf16_code_units(NEW.body)`, and advance `next_enrichment_generation` from `G` to `G+1`, where `G=OLD.next_enrichment_generation`. The statement may either omit/leave `content_revision` at `OLD.content_revision` so the body trigger performs the single implicit increment, or set it explicitly to `transition.applied_content_revision`; no third shape is valid, and after the trigger program both shapes store the same applied revision before terminal evidence. This preserves the product's existing JavaScript `String.length` convention—UTF-16 code units—for `items.total_chars`; it is deliberately distinct from a transcript source's Unicode-scalar count. Because no pre-enrichment title provenance exists, Stage 2 explicitly preserves `title` byte-for-byte rather than guessing whether it was AI-derived. It also preserves `author`, `duration_seconds`, `published_at`, `thumbnail_url`, `description`, `capture_source`, `captured_at`, `total_pages`, every workflow field, and all other non-reset item columns byte-for-byte.
6. Rearm exactly one `enrichment_jobs` singleton for `(item_id, generation=G, expected_content_revision=transition.applied_content_revision)`. Preserve its primary-key ID when it already exists; otherwise insert one. Its complete current-generation shape is `state='pending'`, `attempts=0`, `last_error=NULL`, `claimed_at=completed_at=claim_token_hash=lease_expires_at=NULL`, and `created_at=transition.created_at`; no old batch, claim, completion, error, revision, or generation field survives. Browser attachment then inserts the hold for this exact generation before terminal evidence, making the pending row physically unclaimable.
7. Let the existing items-FTS triggers project the exact new title/body once. The terminal check requires one current FTS projection and no stale duplicate row; the reset never writes FTS through a second service path.

The insert trigger for each of the four terminal evidence types—manual evidence seal, generic body-update receipt, attached browser receipt, and recovery-apply receipt—independently verifies the complete common end state before it can close the deferred transition cycle: the item null/pending fields, exact `total_chars`, and `G -> G+1` allocator edge; the exact pending enrichment singleton at the applied revision/generation; no embedding job; no body-dependent chunk/vector bridge; no unpurged body-dependent semantic tuple; no item topic; no auto-tag link; exactly the snapshotted semantic-purge count; `processing_runtime_state.taxonomy_epoch=prior_taxonomy_epoch+reset_item_topic_count+reset_auto_tag_count`; and the family-specific hold/source/job facts. Thus even a valid source, segment set, or receipt cannot commit with stale derived output. Seeded-success tests place a distinct sentinel in every cleared field/table and a distinct manual/workflow/note/collection/Recall/NotebookLM sentinel in every preserved family for all four transitions; failure injection before and after each reset write proves complete rollback.

Vector-first deletion is a schema-wide invariant, not only a reset convention. Named `BEFORE DELETE` guards on `chunks` and `chunks_rowid` require the corresponding `chunks_vec` row to be absent before a bridge can cascade or be removed. Every item-root deletion path, chunk replacement/repair, backfill, retention purge, and offline backup scrub therefore selects and deletes vectors through the exact bridge first; direct root/chunk/bridge deletion with a live vector aborts. Every canonical `chunks` column and both `chunks_rowid` columns are immutable after insert; changing item/source ownership, kind, ordinal, body/text, hash, epoch/version, timestamps, or any later-added field is forbidden even when the update would not collide. Replacement/repair is only vector-first delete followed by a fresh guard-authorized insert. `BEFORE INSERT` pre-conflict guards reject collisions on `chunks.id`, `(item_id,source_kind,idx)`, `chunks_rowid.chunk_id`, or `chunks_rowid.rowid` before `REPLACE`/UPSERT conflict handling. Ordinary insert/update, UPSERT, `INSERT OR REPLACE`, and `UPDATE OR REPLACE` tests with recursive triggers off/on prove the old chunk/bridge/vector graph remains exact. Combined with the preflight orphan rejection, this guarantees every post-028 vector remains attributable until it is explicitly removed and prevents mutation, a cascade, or a conflict algorithm from manufacturing/relabeling an unowned vec0 row.

#### 6.1.3 Immutable semantic-event history

Migration 028 preserves existing `item_semantic_events` rows but makes their audit role physical. Every column has an explicit storage-class/value guard; all updates are forbidden; direct deletion while the item exists is forbidden while the tested item-root cascade remains allowed. A `BEFORE INSERT` pre-conflict guard rejects an existing primary ID or natural `(item_id,source_kind,source_epoch,source_version,action)` tuple before any conflict algorithm. The current `INSERT OR IGNORE` helper is replaced by select-and-exact-reconcile followed by ordinary insert, and no production writer uses `OR REPLACE`, UPSERT, or a caller-supplied event ID.

Post-028 body-dependent `indexed` inserts for `legacy_item_context|original_content|ai_summary` are denied until a separately reviewed current-revision/generation indexer contract exists; copied historical indexed rows remain eligible only for reset purge. A body-dependent `purged` insert is accepted only from the common-reset service with one fresh same-item body transition, while that transition still owns the current expected revision. It must name an existing indexed tuple with no prior purge, use the deterministic typed ID for domain `youtube-body-reset-semantic-purge-v1` plus transition ID/source kind/epoch/version, and set `created_at=transition.created_at`; the transition's snapshotted count and terminal guard prove exactly all and only the then-missing body-dependent purge tuples were inserted. `manual_note` indexed/purged events remain on the note-index worker's exact note epoch/generation path, which is likewise converted to select-and-reconcile and cannot select a body transition. Post-commit UPDATE, direct DELETE, primary/natural-key `INSERT OR REPLACE`, wrong-transition purge, extra purge, and historical-purge mutation tests preserve the immutable event graph.

### 6.2 Rebuilt `capture_policy_decisions`

Preserve every legacy column and row. Extend the existing checks and add:

| Column | Contract |
|---|---|
| `processing_mode` | `TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'not_applicable'`, enum `not_applicable|hold` |
| `owner_scope_hash` | nullable 64-hex; required for browser method |
| `policy_manifest_hash` | nullable 64-hex; required for browser method |
| `policy_decision_hash` | nullable 64-hex; required for browser method |
| `policy_expires_at` | nullable nonnegative epoch; required for browser and every post-028 `lab_public_caption` restricted method |
| `source_delete_by` | nullable nonnegative epoch; required for browser and every post-028 `lab_public_caption` restricted method |
| `retention_cleanup_budget_ms` | nullable positive integer; required for every post-028 restricted method and at least 300,000 |
| `retention_cleanup_start_by` | nullable nonnegative epoch; required for every post-028 restricted method and exactly `source_delete_by-retention_cleanup_budget_ms` |
| `retention_budget_profile_hash` | nullable lowercase 64-hex; required for every post-028 restricted method |

Extend `method` with `browser_visible_transcript`. Add these exact parent keys:

- `UNIQUE(id,item_id)` for base ownership;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,owner_scope_hash)` for browser owner binding;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,source_delete_by)` for a restricted source deadline even when owner is null;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,owner_scope_hash,source_delete_by)` for browser source owner/deadline binding;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,policy_expires_at,source_delete_by)` for owner-independent restricted policy timing; and
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,owner_scope_hash,policy_expires_at,source_delete_by)` for the browser grant authority window;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,policy_expires_at,source_delete_by,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_cleanup_start_by,retention_budget_profile_hash)` for the complete owner-independent restricted timing/profile; and
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,owner_scope_hash,policy_expires_at,source_delete_by,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_cleanup_start_by,retention_budget_profile_hash)` for the complete browser owner/timing/profile binding.

A `browser_visible_transcript` row is valid only when:

- `platform='youtube'`;
- `environment IN ('lab','test','development')` and never `production`; `lab` remains the only externally authorized live-lab class, while `test` and `development` exist solely for synthetic fixture/local verification and confer no live-access authority;
- `rights_basis IN ('owned_youtube_channel','authorized_youtube_video')`;
- `retention_class='full_text_allowed'`;
- `processing_mode='hold'`;
- `production_allowed=0`;
- `item_id`, all three binding hashes, `policy_expires_at`, `source_delete_by`, cleanup budget/start, and budget-profile hash are non-null;
- `created_at < policy_expires_at <= retention_cleanup_start_by = source_delete_by-retention_cleanup_budget_ms`, `retention_cleanup_budget_ms >= 300000`, and `source_delete_by <= created_at + 604800000`; checked subtraction/ordering rejects overflow. The profile and budget come from the exact feasibility contract in §6.3.1, so five minutes is a floor rather than an unsupported claim that all cleanup always finishes in five minutes.

A post-028 `lab_public_caption` policy used by automatic recovery is separately valid only with `environment='lab'`, `rights_basis='public_lab_only'`, `processing_mode='not_applicable'`, `production_allowed=0`, non-null legal approval, null browser owner/manifest/decision hashes, and the same non-null cleanup budget/start/profile, policy-before-start ordering, and seven-day ceiling. It never inherits `youtube_official_caption` production or owned-video authority.

Every policy used as positive authority—manual source insertion/resolution, transcript job authorization/claim/dispatch/finalization, browser intent/inspect/grant/commit, hold, source, or receipt—requires `blocked_reason IS NULL` under its named insert/transition guard. A post-028 denied decision requires a nonempty bounded stable `blocked_reason` and cannot be referenced by any positive-authority row even when every method, rights, retention, owner, hash, and clock field otherwise matches. Legacy denied/content-free decisions remain immutable evidence but confer no authority. The `blocked_unknown_rights` basis is always denied and can never coexist with null `blocked_reason`; an allowed rights basis used positively requires null `blocked_reason`. FK identity alone never proves an allowed decision.

The browser policy decision is inserted and frozen in the content-free intent-creation transaction, before any inspect authorization can exist. PRD2-F15's atomic policy requirement is therefore reconciled as atomic **acceptance and exact binding** of that already-immutable decision during attachment, not insertion of a new or relabelled decision after body read. Denied, cancelled, expired, or abandoned intent policies remain content-free immutable evidence until item-root deletion; no commit may swap them for a later same-item decision.

Legacy rows receive `processing_mode='not_applicable'` and null new binding fields. They are preserved exactly and cannot be treated as browser authority.

Policy decisions are immutable after insert. Every direct update aborts, and direct deletion while the parent item exists aborts; item-root cascade remains permitted. This freezes the composite parent key and prevents a receipt/source from inheriting changed policy semantics.

### 6.3 Rebuilt `transcript_sources`

Preserve all legacy fields and add:

| Column | Contract |

| `retention_obligation_id` | nullable source ID; exactly equal to `id` for browser and post-028 `lab_public_caption`, null for every unrestricted/manual/copied row |
