Extend `source_kind` with `browser_visible_transcript`. Add:

- `UNIQUE(id,item_id)`;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,accepted_content_revision)`;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,policy_decision_id,owner_scope_hash)` for exact held authority;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,policy_decision_id,source_delete_by)` for an exact restricted deadline even when owner is null;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,policy_decision_id,source_delete_by,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_cleanup_start_by,retention_budget_profile_hash)` for the exact restricted admission timing/profile;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,policy_decision_id,owner_scope_hash,source_delete_by)` for exact browser owner/deadline binding;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,policy_decision_id,text_sha256,integrity_sha256,segment_count)` for source evidence regardless of nullable owner;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,policy_decision_id,source_kind,text_sha256,integrity_sha256,segment_count)` for exact manual evidence sealing;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,policy_decision_id,source_delete_by,text_sha256,integrity_sha256,segment_count)` for deadline-bound source evidence regardless of nullable owner;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,policy_decision_id,owner_scope_hash,source_delete_by,text_sha256,integrity_sha256,segment_count)` for exact browser receipt/hold evidence;
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,policy_decision_id,source_delete_by,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_cleanup_start_by,retention_budget_profile_hash,text_sha256,integrity_sha256,segment_count)` for owner-independent restricted receipt/obligation evidence; and
- `UNIQUE(id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,policy_decision_id,owner_scope_hash,source_delete_by,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_cleanup_start_by,retention_budget_profile_hash,text_sha256,integrity_sha256,segment_count)` for exact browser owner/timing/profile receipt/obligation evidence;
- composite FK `(policy_decision_id,item_id)` → `capture_policy_decisions(id,item_id)` using the exact action in §6.11;
- for rows with non-null owner, composite FK `(policy_decision_id,item_id,owner_scope_hash)` → `capture_policy_decisions(id,item_id,owner_scope_hash)` in addition to the base policy/item FK;
- composite FK `(policy_decision_id,item_id,source_delete_by)` → `capture_policy_decisions(id,item_id,source_delete_by)`; legacy/unrestricted null deadlines skip only this supplemental FK;
- for browser rows, composite FK `(policy_decision_id,item_id,owner_scope_hash,source_delete_by)` → `capture_policy_decisions(id,item_id,owner_scope_hash,source_delete_by)` in addition to the non-owner deadline FK;
- for post-028 manual rows, the deferred exact reverse seal FK in §6.4.1; null legacy/browser/recovery seal IDs skip only that supplemental relation and are constrained by their own legacy/receipt shape;
- for restricted rows, deferred `(retention_obligation_id,item_id,source_delete_by)` → `youtube_source_retention_obligations(obligation_id,item_id,source_delete_by)`; unrestricted null tuples skip only this supplemental relation and are rejected from selecting an obligation;
- unique partial index `transcript_sources_one_active_per_item` on `(item_id)` where `status='active'`;
- cleanup index `(status,source_delete_by)` where `source_delete_by IS NOT NULL`.

Every post-028 source insert passes a named method-specific compatibility guard that joins its immutable policy: item, source kind/method, retention class, processing mode, owner/null-owner shape, deadline, environment/production denial, legal/rights basis, and `blocked_reason IS NULL` must agree exactly. Before SQLite can resolve any conflict algorithm, that `BEFORE INSERT` guard also aborts if `NEW.id` already exists or, for `NEW.status='active'`, if any active source already exists for the item. Thus `INSERT OR REPLACE` cannot silently delete an old source through either the primary key or the one-active partial index when `recursive_triggers` is off. The same guard requires the exact body/source SHA-256 equality frozen above for `user_paste`, `uploaded_file`, `youtube_official_caption`, and `owned_media_stt`; their terminal evidence seal additionally proves the exact ordered segment assembly where segments are required. For restricted browser/lab-public rows, the insertion clock must satisfy `now < policy_expires_at <= retention_cleanup_start_by < source_delete_by`, all cleanup/profile values must equal the policy, and the item must carry the exact `no_durable_client_copy_v1` contract. The source is inserted first with a deferred exact source→obligation FK and a preselected obligation ID equal to its source ID. The obligation insert then must observe that exact fresh source/transition/policy/client-contract tuple and terminal-evidence selection, while the deferred FK prevents source-without-obligation commit. Before terminal evidence, the same transaction next inserts the first exact schedule reservation or select-and-exact-reconciles the immutable existing same-item reservation; the obligation's deferred reservation FK prevents omission. A browser source additionally requires the fresh `browser_attachment` transition/application at its applied revision with the absent preselected attached-receipt ID; a lab-public source requires the corresponding exact `automatic_recovery` graph and absent recovery-receipt ID. Those transitions' deferred terminal-evidence relations force the corresponding receipt in the same transaction, so an obligation-only row, source-only row, reservation-only row, or standalone policy+obligation+source+segments graph cannot commit.

For `browser_visible_transcript`, `provenance_schema` is exactly `youtube-browser-provenance-v1`; status is initially `active`; accepted revision must equal the item's current revision; owner/delete-by must equal the policy/receipt binding; timestamp mode is exactly `timestamped`; and caption source class is exactly `unknown`. The canonical source text and resulting `items.body` are exactly the server-normalized cue texts joined in observed order by one `\n`, with no leading/trailing separator; `text_sha256` hashes those exact UTF-8 bytes. The attached terminal-receipt guard compares the current item body byte-for-byte with that ordered segment assembly and its recomputed hash before commit. Client page title or metadata never establishes this body. Its `provenance_json` is canonical UTF-8 JSON with no duplicate keys and exactly this `youtube-browser-provenance-v1` object shape; additional keys fail:

```text
schema_version: exact "youtube-browser-provenance-v1"
observation_kind: exact "browser_visible_transcript"
extractor_version: 1..128-byte reviewed ASCII version token
extractor_contract_version: 1..128-byte reviewed ASCII version token
algorithm_version: 1..128-byte reviewed ASCII version token
renderer_family: 1..64-byte reviewed ASCII token
renderer_version: 1..128-byte reviewed ASCII version token
visible_track_label: null or 1..200 Unicode scalar values with no control characters
visible_language_code: null or 1..32 ASCII BCP-47-shaped token
snapshots: integer 1..151
scroll_iterations: integer 0..150
stable_bottom_checks: exact integer 3
reached_top: exact JSON true
reached_bottom: exact JSON true
overlap_failures: exact integer 0
elapsed_ms: integer 0..15000
cue_count: integer 1..7200
nonempty_cue_count: exact cue_count
total_chars: integer 1..500000
server_normalization_version: exact "youtube-transcript-normalization-v1"
received_at: nonnegative epoch equal to source.created_at and the database transaction time
```

Version tokens use only `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]`; `renderer_family` uses `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`; language uses `[A-Za-z0-9-]`. A named insert trigger validates `json_valid`, JSON types, exact key membership/count, duplicate-key absence, numeric bounds, fixed-key canonical serialization, and equality to the separately bound grant/source facts. Provenance may not contain account, cookie, signed URL, player, destination, origin, authorization, raw request, arbitrary error, or official/manual/ASR claims.

`text_sha256` is the SHA-256 of the canonical source text. `integrity_sha256` is the governing `transcript-integrity-v1`: it uses the typed field framing in §6.12 with domain bytes `UTF8("transcript-integrity-v1") || 0x00` and this exact order—decoded 32-byte `text_sha256`, segment count, then for each stored segment in ascending `idx`: `idx`, nullable `start_ms`, nullable `end_ms`, and the exact normalized stored text UTF-8 bytes. Every value retains its type/null marker and length; repeated equal cues remain repeated framed tuples. `duration_ms` is excluded only because §6.4 makes it an immutable deterministic derivative of start/end. Migration 028 computes the digest only where legacy body/segments are provable; copied inactive history that cannot be reconstructed remains null and cannot become current authority without explicit rebuild.

All source identity and evidence fields are immutable after insert: `id`, `item_id`, `policy_decision_id`, `source_kind`, language/caption/timestamp fields, `provenance_schema`, `provenance_json`, retention, both hashes, `segment_count`, accepted revision, manual evidence-seal ID, retention-obligation ID, owner, delete-by, cleanup budget/start/profile, and `created_at`. The only permitted post-028 application update is one monotonic status edge `active -> superseded|blocked`; neither state exits. Copied legacy `deleted` rows are preserved as terminal history, but no new insert or update may create `deleted`—item-root physical deletion removes rows instead. A named active-source insert guard requires `accepted_content_revision=items.content_revision`. A held source cannot take any status edge in schema 028. A named `BEFORE UPDATE OF body` guard rejects the update whenever that item still has an active source **or held row**, so every permitted non-held replacement transaction must first insert its §6.1.1 transition, supersede the exact old source under that transition, execute the common reset/body update once, and insert a newly revision-bound active source plus terminal evidence before commit. Those checks are additive: absence of an active source or hold is never by itself body-write authority. Direct source, segment, or evidence-seal deletion is forbidden while its parent item exists. Parent-item cascade is the only source/segment/seal-row deletion path, while the independent content-free retention obligation survives it.

For a browser or post-028 `lab_public_caption` source, `source_delete_by` schedules authoritative **item-root deletion**, not source-only redaction. The earliest stored cleanup start/deadline across every retained source-history row for the item wins; supersession, blocking, a later policy/request/profile, a later receipt, or source status change never cancels or extends it. The synchronous retention service begins no later than that `retention_cleanup_start_by` by atomically fencing the complete item before any path-bearing database row can disappear. It removes and proves filesystem artifacts first while their rows still supply deletion authority, closes the enumerated non-cascading surfaces below, deletes vectors before the item root, checkpoints away restricted WAL bytes, and completes immutable content-free evidence before the deadline. These Stage 2 success paths create no fresh filesystem artifact, but cleanup still verifies no transcript sentinel remains in pre-existing artifacts and every storage surface. Merely marking the source deleted, deleting segments while retaining `items.body`, or allowing an item cascade to erase the cleanup obligation/path manifest is forbidden and does not satisfy retention.

#### 6.3.1 Durable retention obligation and purge receipt

Create `youtube_source_retention_obligations` as content-free control state that deliberately has no cascading FK to `items`, so item-root deletion cannot erase unfinished filesystem/backup work. Each restricted browser or post-028 lab-public transaction preselects one source/obligation identity, inserts the source first, inserts its exact guarded obligation, inserts or exact-reconciles its immutable schedule reservation, and only then inserts terminal evidence; the deferred source/obligation/reservation/evidence relations close together at commit. The exact physical column order is:

```text
obligation_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL, exactly the restricted source ID
item_id TEXT NOT NULL
policy_decision_id TEXT NOT NULL
accepted_content_revision INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK > 0
source_delete_by INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
source_kind TEXT NOT NULL exact browser_visible_transcript|lab_public_caption
retention_cleanup_budget_ms INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 300000
retention_cleanup_start_by INTEGER NOT NULL exact source_delete_by-retention_cleanup_budget_ms
retention_budget_profile_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
metadata_cache_scope_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
client_storage_contract_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
admission_evidence_kind TEXT NOT NULL exact browser_receipt|recovery_receipt
admission_evidence_id TEXT NOT NULL bounded opaque ID
live_source_id TEXT nullable; exactly obligation_id while pending
live_browser_receipt_id TEXT nullable
live_recovery_receipt_id TEXT nullable
admission_released_at INTEGER nullable
state TEXT NOT NULL exact pending|artifacts_purging|artifacts_purged|db_root_purged|complete
purge_receipt_id TEXT nullable
created_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
artifact_manifest_hash TEXT nullable lowercase 64-hex
artifact_manifest_count INTEGER nullable CHECK >= 0
survivor_manifest_hash TEXT nullable lowercase 64-hex
survivor_manifest_count INTEGER nullable CHECK >= 0
artifacts_purge_started_at INTEGER nullable
db_root_purged_at INTEGER nullable
wal_truncated_at INTEGER nullable
artifacts_purged_at INTEGER nullable
verified_at INTEGER nullable
completion_earliest_source_delete_by INTEGER nullable
completion_earliest_cleanup_start_by INTEGER nullable
completion_obligation_set_hash TEXT nullable lowercase 64-hex
completion_obligation_count INTEGER nullable CHECK > 0
root_purge_application_id TEXT nullable
root_purge_seal_id TEXT nullable
root_obligation_set_hash TEXT nullable lowercase 64-hex
root_obligation_count INTEGER nullable CHECK > 0
last_error_code TEXT nullable bounded content-free allowlist
updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
```

Add `UNIQUE(obligation_id,item_id,source_delete_by)`, an item/deadline scan index, and ordinary non-unique indexes for purge receipt, root application, and root seal; many obligations for one item must be able to share the same terminal facts. Identity, item, policy/revision/admission evidence, original deadline/budget/start/profile, metadata-cache scope, client-storage contract, source kind, and creation time are immutable; a deadline/start never recomputes from restore time and never increases. The insert guard requires the exact just-inserted restricted source, its policy, body transition, selected not-yet-present terminal receipt ID, same transaction clock, exact `no_durable_client_copy_v1` item contract, and all copied facts. `live_source_id` has an exact deferred source FK; exactly one live browser/recovery receipt field has the corresponding deferred FK and equals immutable `admission_evidence_id`. Thus the source requires the obligation, the obligation requires the source, and terminal evidence closes the creation transaction.

The one-way shape is exact. `pending` has all manifest/phase/root/root-set/completion/receipt fields null, `live_source_id=obligation_id`, exactly one live terminal-evidence ID, and null admission-release clock. In one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, every same-item pending obligation moves together to `artifacts_purging`, records the same content-free artifact and survivor manifest hashes/counts plus start clock, verifies the exact live source and terminal evidence are represented, then clears only the three live reference fields and stamps `admission_released_at=artifacts_purge_started_at`; immutable admission IDs/facts remain forever. No other edge may release those FKs. `artifacts_purged` adds only the artifact completion clock after the exact manifest is durably absent. `db_root_purged` adds the root-delete clock, exact root application/seal, and common `root_obligation_set_hash|count` while the four completion fields remain null. `complete` additionally has WAL/verification clocks, copies the root set exactly into the four completion-set fields, names its immutable receipt, and has `last_error_code=NULL`. Direct deletion is always forbidden on the live database. Item-ID reuse is rejected by both this history and the immutable retired-ID/external-ledger union below, including after restore.

`last_error_code` is closed and content-free. `pending` alone permits `retention_clock_rollback|cleanup_budget_exceeded|retention_capability_unavailable|manifest_build_failed`; `artifacts_purging` alone permits `artifact_manifest_mismatch|artifact_identity_changed|artifact_delete_failed|artifact_fsync_failed|survivor_manifest_mismatch|metadata_cache_scope_mismatch`; `artifacts_purged` alone permits `root_precondition_failed|root_transaction_failed`; and `db_root_purged` alone permits `vacuum_failed|checkpoint_busy|verification_failed`. `complete` permits none. A failure retains its current state, stores only one exact code and `updated_at`, and emits path/raw OS details only to bounded ephemeral operator handling that is excluded from database/log/report payloads; a later successful owning phase edge clears the code. No path, title, URL, body, source text, filename, token, exception message, errno string, or arbitrary error is stored.

Schema 028 adds the `STRICT` singleton `youtube_retention_clock_state` with this exact physical column order:

```text
id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK(id=1)
max_observed_wall_ms INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
trusted_wall_anchor_ms INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
trusted_monotonic_anchor_ms INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
trusted_raw_wall_observed_ms INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
trusted_boot_id_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
trusted_reference_lower_ms INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
trusted_reference_upper_ms INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= trusted_reference_lower_ms
trusted_reference_provider_set_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
trusted_reference_proof_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
trusted_proof_max_error_ms INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK BETWEEN 0 AND 1000
trusted_proof_kind TEXT NOT NULL CHECK IN ('automatic_same_boot','automatic_new_boot','operator_reanchor_v1')
trusted_operator_artifact_hash TEXT nullable lowercase 64-hex
trusted_reanchor_counter INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
trusted_proof_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
rollback_detected_at INTEGER nullable CHECK >= 0
forward_jump_detected_at INTEGER nullable CHECK >= 0
updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= trusted_proof_at
CHECK trusted_reference_upper_ms-trusted_reference_lower_ms <= 2000 under checked arithmetic
CHECK trusted_wall_anchor_ms=trusted_reference_lower_ms+
      ((trusted_reference_upper_ms-trusted_reference_lower_ms)/2)
CHECK trusted_proof_max_error_ms=
      MAX(trusted_wall_anchor_ms-trusted_reference_lower_ms,
          trusted_reference_upper_ms-trusted_wall_anchor_ms)
CHECK ((trusted_proof_kind='operator_reanchor_v1' AND trusted_operator_artifact_hash IS NOT NULL)
    OR (trusted_proof_kind IN ('automatic_same_boot','automatic_new_boot')
        AND trusted_operator_artifact_hash IS NULL))
```

Exactly one row must exist before 028 DDL can commit. Its named insert guard accepts only migration bootstrap under the already execution-proven clock service and rejects a second ID before any conflict algorithm; update/delete/`REPLACE` is unavailable outside the closed clock-service edges below, and direct deletion is always denied. `trusted_reference_provider_set_hash` identifies the exact packaged one-provider automatic set or the two-independent-provider operator set without storing endpoints. `trusted_reference_proof_hash` is recomputed by the exact deterministic `trusted_time_proof_v1` scalar from §6.12 as the typed digest with domain `trusted-time-proof-v1` over, in order, proof kind, reanchor counter, boot-ID hash, trusted monotonic anchor, raw wall observation, independently synchronized reference lower/upper bounds, maximum error, provider-set hash, signed operator-artifact hash or null, and proof clock. Those inputs are all physically present in the row, and the insert/update guard rejects any hash or automatic/operator null-shape mismatch. No raw provider response, endpoint, or operator artifact is stored.

A raw OS wall value is **never** allowed to advance the high-water by itself. The only monotonic source is Linux `clock_gettime(CLOCK_BOOTTIME)` through the packaged clock helper, truncated from nanoseconds to whole milliseconds; it includes suspend. The boot identity is the exact lowercase canonical `/proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id` hashed under domain `trusted-time-boot-id-v1`. There is no `CLOCK_MONOTONIC`, `process.hrtime`, uptime, Date, performance-clock, or platform fallback in lab/production; a runtime without the exact source is incompatible. Tests may inject only the packaged deterministic fake implementing those same semantics.

At each process boot, after a boot-ID change, and no more than 60,000 suspend-aware milliseconds before source admission or any destructive retention edge, the separately supervised service produces a proof with reference interval `[L,U]`, `0 <= U-L <= 2000`, anchor `A=L+floor((U-L)/2)`, error `MAX(A-L,U-A) <= 1000`, current `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` value `M`, raw wall `R`, and provider-set hash. It stores `trusted_wall_anchor_ms=A`, `trusted_monotonic_anchor_ms=M`, `trusted_raw_wall_observed_ms=R`, `trusted_proof_at=A`, and the complete proof tuple under the writer lock. On that boot, checked `candidate_now=A+(current_boottime_ms-M)`, then `effective_now=MAX(old.max_observed_wall_ms,candidate_now)`. Only that nondecreasing `effective_now` drives expiry/deadline comparisons, and every accepted clock edge sets `max_observed_wall_ms=effective_now`; an accepted reference anchor below the prior high-water can therefore never reopen expired authority. Proof age is `current_boottime_ms-M`, never a wall-clock subtraction. A high-water-only edge may change only `max_observed_wall_ms|updated_at`; a latch edge may additionally set exactly its owning null latch to the same `effective_now`.

Automatic re-anchor acceptance is numerical and closed. On the same boot, compute checked `prior_candidate_now=old.trusted_wall_anchor_ms+(M-old.trusted_monotonic_anchor_ms)` before replacing the anchor; `[L,U]` must intersect `[prior_candidate_now-5000,prior_candidate_now+5000]` and `R` must lie in `[L-5000,U+5000]`. The proof kind is `automatic_same_boot` and the reanchor counter is unchanged. On a changed boot, `[L,U]` must intersect `[old.max_observed_wall_ms-5000,old.max_observed_wall_ms+300000]` and the same raw-wall condition must hold; the proof kind is `automatic_new_boot` and the counter is unchanged. Any checked-arithmetic failure or interval outside those bounds sets/retains the applicable latch and cannot reset an anchor or high-water.

A larger legitimate correction requires `operator_reanchor_v1`: one permission-restricted, single-use signed artifact bound to the old/new boot hashes, prior complete clock-state hash, next reanchor counter, two independent reference-provider proofs and provider-set hash, `[L,U]`, raw wall, reason enum, and expiry. Its interval still has width at most 2,000 ms, must have `L >= old.max_observed_wall_ms-5000`, and its counter must equal old plus one; there is deliberately no automatic forward bound. Signature/key/artifact verification occurs in the hash-attested clock supervisor before the guarded transaction, and the artifact hash is stored and enters the proof hash. The writer lock plus the committed `trusted_reanchor_counter` is the durable single-use record: a failed/rolled-back transaction may reverify and retry the same artifact, but after one commit its prior-state hash and old-plus-one counter can never pass again. Reuse, one-provider input, backward interval, expired signature, wrong state hash, or counter skip fails. This is the only path that may accept a new-boot interval more than 300,000 ms beyond the high-water.

Every policy/source admission, reader/writer/claimant, scheduler, startup, backup, restore, activation, and phase edge compares raw wall with the monotonic-derived effective value. `R < effective_now-5000` latches `rollback_detected_at`; `R > effective_now+5000` latches `forward_jump_detected_at`. Either latch denies new restricted authority, every restricted-item read/readiness, and every irreversible phase edge; it starts fenced containment but cannot classify an item as due or delete it from suspect raw time. An accepted automatic proof may clear only the latch(es) whose exact interval/raw-wall predicates above now pass; an accepted operator proof may clear either/both while atomically incrementing the counter. The accepted proof replaces every anchor/reference/proof field, including the raw observation and nullable operator-artifact hash, sets `max_observed_wall_ms=effective_now`, sets `updated_at=effective_now`, and changes no other field. No high-water-only/latch edge may clear a latch, no proof may clear only part of its own stored tuple, and a failed proof changes no anchor/latch except setting a previously null detection latch. Restart with no current-boot proof remains fenced. Immediately before `pending -> artifacts_purging`, root deletion, WAL verification/receipt completion, backup publication, restore publication, and activation, the transaction requires a latch-free proof no older than 60,000 suspend-aware milliseconds and recomputes effective time. Raw request/client time is never accepted. Profile admission includes the supervisor's automatic and operator-recovery bounds. Tests cover suspend, forward/backward jumps, both proof kinds, boot changes, counter/signature/provider substitutions, the exact ±5,000/300,000/60,000/1,000-ms boundaries, a legal lower-edge anchor below the old high-water, and every field transition; a far-future raw timestamp produces zero purge mutation and never poisons the high-water.

Deadline feasibility is a source-admission invariant, not an optimistic scheduler promise. The packaged `retention-budget-v1` profile freezes absolute maxima of 2 GiB combined live main/WAL bytes, 256 MiB and 2,048 filesystem entries for one item artifact root, 50,000 survivor-action entries per item, 64 same-item obligations, one matching metadata-cache row, and at most eight simultaneously retained restricted item roots. Installation separately benchmarks (a) the complete worst-case live-retention sequence—including fd-safe artifact deletion/fsync, survivor transforms, vector-first root transaction through the already direct-initialized embedded restricted vec0 runtime, full 2-GiB `secure_delete` VACUUM, checkpoint, and verification; (b) the complete worst-case native SQLite online-backup sequence through the exact 128-page/no-busy-handler/private-VFS state machine in §6.12.1, from immediately before the first destination byte through actual-handle and companion checks, bounded semantic WAL-read transcript capture, separately implemented semantic replay, sole finish, destination/source close, v3 VFS-audit finalization, file/directory fsync, and installed supervisor copy-success transition for a full 2-GiB raw clone on the actual source and staging devices, using the maximum supported WAL-index region/probe fixture and the exact preallocated transcript capacity; and (c) the complete worst-case backup-sanitize sequence from that raw-clone closure through clone scrub, full VACUUM, scrub-stage and pre-finalization full/quick/FK/phase-bound zero checks, ledger append/replication, required live reconciliation/purge, clone finalization, second immutable post-finalization checks, and sidecar/fsync, ending when the raw clone is ready for bounded destruction. Each runs three consecutive times on the actual database/artifact/staging/recovery devices with both retention units isolated. `retention_cleanup_budget_ms=MAX(300000,4*max_live_cycle_ms+120000)`, `backup_copy_budget_ms=MAX(300000,4*max_copy_cycle_ms+120000)`, and `backup_sanitize_budget_ms=MAX(300000,4*max_backup_sanitize_cycle_ms+120000)` under checked arithmetic.

Installation separately measures (d) `max_reservation_watchdog_takeover_ms` from watchdog arming through progress-failure detection, old-supervisor kill/reap, OFD-lease acquisition, complete canonical reconciliation, successor reconstruction/write/fsync/revalidation, and a replacement ready to take the final deadline sample; (e) `max_reservation_validation_to_rename_ms` from that final sample through completed same-directory rename; (f) `max_raw_destroy_physical_closure_ms` from the authoritative `fencing` rename through broker revocation, cgroup freeze/kill/reap, both stable scans, raw-clone unlink, parent-directory fsync, and complete evidence ready for `destroy_v1`; and (g) `max_raw_destroy_sequence_ms` from the last pre-fence effective-time sample through completed `destroy_v1` rename. Measurement (g) includes the `fence_v1` mutation, physical closure, and the `destroy_v1` mutation; it injects a stopped or failed supervisor at every supported point in each mutation and every Cartesian pair of such points, so the two transitions may each independently consume a watchdog takeover and a rename interval. All four measurements run three consecutive times on the actual staging/registry devices under injected scheduler/cgroup contention and every supported takeover boundary.

The profile sets `reservation_watchdog_takeover_headroom_ms=MAX(1000,4*max_reservation_watchdog_takeover_ms+250)` and `reservation_rename_headroom_ms=MAX(1000,4*max_reservation_validation_to_rename_ms+250)` under checked arithmetic. Let `W` and `R` be those two values. It then computes `raw_destroy_composed_bound_ms=2*W+max_raw_destroy_physical_closure_ms+2*R` and `backup_raw_destroy_margin_ms=MAX(60000,4*max_raw_destroy_sequence_ms+1000,raw_destroy_composed_bound_ms+1000)` under checked nonwrapping signed-int64 arithmetic. Thus the margin explicitly reserves one independent `W+R` for `fence_v1`, the measured physical closure, and another independent `W+R` for `destroy_v1`; the end-to-end measurement is a second, non-substitutable bound. Installation rejects the profile before any reservation or destination creation unless every term is present, the complete sequence finishes within both the measured maximum and the derived margin in all runs, and the checked composed inequality holds. The packaged target capability must enforce both independent headrooms and the composed destruction bound with the sole-supervisor lease, external watchdog, scheduler, and killability protocol below; a target that cannot bound any interval is unsupported. The profile hash covers all seven named measurements, every formula, both headrooms, `raw_destroy_composed_bound_ms`, the derived destruction margin, all ceilings, fixture hashes, devices, SQLite/sqlite-vec/tool/supervisor/broker hashes, semantic-transcript schema/producer/independent-validator hashes, exact transcript capacity and overflow policy, LSM/cgroup policy and kernel-capability hashes, multipliers, and reserves.

Create `youtube_retention_schedule_reservations` as a `STRICT`, immutable, content-free history table with this exact column order:

```text
item_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL
retention_cleanup_budget_ms INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 300000
retention_cleanup_start_by INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
source_delete_by INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK > retention_cleanup_start_by
retention_budget_profile_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
created_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
CHECK retention_cleanup_start_by=source_delete_by-retention_cleanup_budget_ms under checked arithmetic
UNIQUE(item_id,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_cleanup_start_by,source_delete_by,retention_budget_profile_hash)
```

It deliberately has no FK to `items`, so a completed live purge or sanitized-copy scrub preserves the schedule as retired content-free history. The first source transaction inserts source, guarded obligation, then this reservation under the obligation's deferred exact reference; the reservation insert guard requires that fresh pending obligation and current item. A later same-item restricted source performs select-and-exact-reconcile against the existing row and must reuse the exact deadline, budget, start, and profile—never update, extend, or create a second slot. For a first item, source commit requires fewer than eight **live** restricted roots and its closed interval `[cleanup_start_by,source_delete_by]` not to overlap any other live reservation. “Live” is exactly a reservation whose `item_id` still exists as a restricted item root; a reservation whose item is absent must have a matching immutable retired-ID proof and is excluded from scheduling but never deleted. Serialized commits make one racing overlap lose. Thus global-lock/VACUUM cycles are schedulable without assuming parallel destructive work. Unused policy rows reserve nothing and source commit rechecks the slot. Every update/delete/`REPLACE` is denied on the live database; backup/restore validates every rootless reservation against the retired-ID union.

Source admission also requires current database/item/survivor surfaces below every ceiling, a current execution-proven profile containing the live, raw-copy, sanitize, and destruction-margin budgets, and `created_at < policy_expires_at <= cleanup_start_by=source_delete_by-budget`. Profile/device/runtime drift denies new authority and starts containment rather than creating a mixed set. While any obligation exists, the connection registry enforces the profile's page-size-derived `max_page_count`, WAL auto-checkpoint/journal-size limit, and a fatal effective-main+WAL byte monitor; every artifact/survivor/obligation writer has the corresponding count/byte guard. An attempted overage is rejected, while external/unregistered growth latches `cleanup_budget_exceeded`, blocks readiness, and starts containment immediately. The scheduler begins no later than the stored reservation start; neither a later/faster profile nor restore can move that clock later. A raw backup may start only when its derived copy/sanitize/destruction deadline is strictly before every included root's cleanup start, so a slow but valid profile never conflicts with an unmeasured snapshot interval.

Client persistence is part of source admission, not something a later server receipt can erase remotely. Create `youtube_item_client_storage_contracts` as a `STRICT` item-cascading table with this exact physical column order:

```text
item_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL
state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(state IN ('legacy_unproven','no_durable_client_copy_v1'))
server_read_contract_hash TEXT nullable lowercase 64-hex
service_worker_sha256 TEXT nullable lowercase 64-hex
note_journal_contract_hash TEXT nullable lowercase 64-hex
extension_storage_contract_hash TEXT nullable lowercase 64-hex
established_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
contract_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
```

`contract_hash` is the §6.12 typed digest with domain `youtube-client-storage-contract-v1` followed by the preceding seven columns in their listed order. The exact shape check requires all four component hashes null for `legacy_unproven` and all four non-null for `no_durable_client_copy_v1`. Migration 028 inserts immutable `legacy_unproven` rows for every existing YouTube/YouTube Short item at its one migration clock. Exactly one authority-sensitive `AFTER INSERT ON items` trigger named `items_bootstrap_youtube_client_contract_and_transcript_v1` handles every post-028 item insert. In one trigger program and this exact statement order it (1) conditionally inserts the exact `no_durable_client_copy_v1` row for a newly inserted YouTube/YouTube Short item using the trigger transaction clock and the packaged B28 server/read, service-worker, journal, and extension-memory-only hashes; (2) asserts that row's exact same-item shape and recomputed contract hash; (3) selects the postinsert `youtube_body_authority_v1` row; (4) conditionally inserts the exact revision-1 transcript job only when the complete closed enqueue predicate below passes; and (5) asserts the expected contract and transcript-job cardinalities before returning. Failure of any statement aborts the original item insert. No other trigger inserts either row or reads the client contract to decide initial transcript enqueue. FTS and enrichment-generation bootstrap may remain separate only because neither supplies authority to these steps; their relative trigger order is irrelevant and is never used as authority. The contract insert guard recomputes the digest, requires the same item and transaction clock, and rejects an existing `item_id` before conflict handling; every column is immutable, direct delete while the item exists is denied, and only the tested item-root cascade removes it. UPSERT, `INSERT OR REPLACE`, and `UPDATE OR REPLACE` cannot relabel or remove a contract under either recursive-trigger setting. No update may promote a legacy row; a separately reviewed data-migration/client-inventory design would be required. Browser or automatic restricted-source admission requires the exact v1 row and copies `contract_hash` into its obligation. This intentionally makes legacy/unprovable items ineligible for restricted recovery rather than pretending an unreachable offline client was erased.

`no_durable_client_copy_v1` is a closed browser contract:

- the server-rendered `/items/:id` response for such an item is a content-free update-required/bootstrap shell and never embeds title, body, summary, notes, citations, transcript, or other item fields in HTML;
- every content-bearing item/note/chat/RAG API requires an authenticated, short-lived `restricted-read-v1` proof held only in process memory. The current bootstrap may obtain it only after proving the exact client bundle and service-worker hashes, deleting every legacy Brain page/API CacheStorage entry, opening the exact current cache namespace with zero matching item entries, and disabling the local note journal for this item. Requests without the proof—including navigation through an old service worker—receive only the content-free shell/error with `Cache-Control: no-store`;
- the same classification/read lock covers the closed aggregate inventory: library/grid/list and React Server Component/flight projections; search/FTS, Ask/RAG scope and citations; collection, tag, topic, workflow/review/needs-upgrade and related counts; item-picker/autocomplete/recent/history surfaces; note/chat/card projections; JSON/list/bulk APIs; feeds; and every server action that returns item fields. Without the in-memory proof, a v1 item is omitted before any title/snippet/body/note/citation/thumbnail/metadata field is selected or serialized, and aggregate counts/facets are recomputed from the filtered set. A direct item lookup returns only the shell/stable content-free denial. Repository scans over server components, route handlers, server actions, database row serializers, and RSC payloads make this inventory closed; an unclassified item reader is a capability failure;
- app-provided durable-copy features are categorically unavailable for a v1 item even when an interactive read proof exists: library ZIP/JSON/Markdown export, single-item download, print/PDF, share payload, clipboard/copy-all, offline pin/preload, NotebookLM/Recall/Telegram or other outbound export, browser-extension/local file save, and future download/feed routes must reject before content selection. A bulk scope containing any v1 row fails atomically with stable content-free `restricted_item_export_denied` rather than silently exporting it or a partial transcript. This ban exists from the item's first response, so later restricted-source admission never relies on erasing a prior app-created durable copy;
- the B28 service worker never `put`s `/items/:id`, item/note/chat/RAG APIs, redirects to them, or any response bearing `X-Brain-Restricted-Content: 1`; it honors `no-store`, deletes all legacy matching entries on activation before claiming clients, and treats an offline restricted request as unavailable rather than replaying HTML. Cache names, match options, redirects, opaque responses, navigation preload, and response-clone paths are in the closed repository scan;
- `brain-manual-notes-v2` and every future IndexedDB/localStorage/CacheStorage/file-system journal reject reads/writes for a v1 item. The editor uses memory plus the online guarded server save only; success deletes every same-item legacy record instead of writing an `acknowledged` full-content record. The exact current journal states are `dirty|in_flight|conflict|copy_only|acknowledged`. Eligibility cleanup is state-independent: it enumerates the `itemId` index and deletes every matching composite key across every editor instance without reading or allowlisting `state`. A historical `pending`, malformed, unknown, or future state with a valid same-item key is deleted identically. Failure or blocking of open, enumeration, delete, or commit denies the v1 proof/save completion. No rejected path may first read `contentMarkdown`, and no successful read/save may recreate a same-item journal row. Extension inspect/upload similarly keeps cue/body data in bounded memory only and its exact version is denied from authority if it exposes a durable store;
- every server response repeats the v1 classification under its writer/read lock, uses `private, no-store, max-age=0`, and is absent from service-worker precache/page caches. A version/hash drift revokes the memory proof and returns to the shell before content bytes are read.

Because a v1 item has been protected from its first response and old clients cannot obtain content, root purge proves non-persistence rather than claiming to contact offline devices. Admission and purge fixtures seed old `PAGES_CACHE` entries, CacheStorage aliases, navigation responses, IndexedDB note records covering each current state `dirty|in_flight|conflict|copy_only|acknowledged` with at least two editor instances per state plus historical `pending` and unknown-state canaries, local/session storage, extension storage, and an old service worker. Cleanup deletes every same-item composite key by the `itemId` index while preserving other-item rows byte-for-byte; blocked/aborted transactions, activation, guarded read/save, retry, restart, and concurrent current-client paths cannot read or recreate a same-item row. Legacy items remain blocked, v1 items disclose zero content to the old client, and current activation removes every canary. The survivor descriptor includes `youtube_item_client_storage_contracts` as an item cascade and the receipt binds its hash.

The v1 row is an **early authority prerequisite**, not merely a source-insert check. Browser intent creation, panel claim, inspect authorization, inspect consumption immediately before isolated execution, review confirmation, upload-grant issuance, and the pre-body upload gate each require the exact same-item `state='no_durable_client_copy_v1'` row, recomputed four-component `contract_hash`, current revision equal to the intent's expected revision, and current §5.2.1 class exactly `weak_metadata_capture`. A known legacy, strong, ambiguous, generic, active-source, invalid-graph, or already-drifted item therefore causes zero DOM execution and zero body transfer. After those gates authorize and the body is validly streamed, revision drift alone is not reclassified as an authentication failure: the post-stream writer-lock gate still requires the exact v1 row/hash and every nonrevision binding, then lets the terminal classifier either attach at the unchanged expected revision, record an allowlisted conflict for a valid greater-revision race, or contain an invalid graph/lower revision/weak greater-revision without receipt. Schema 028 creates no separate duplicate browser receipt; same-intent/request/body response loss or a concurrent handler returns the one already-committed attached/conflict receipt.

Automatic enqueue/backfill, pending reauthorization, claim, dispatch immediately before provider contact, explicit retry, stale rearm, returned-result classification, and success apply likewise require the exact v1 row/hash under their writer lock. A copied `legacy_unproven` pending/retryable transcript job remains preserved but permanently inert: it cannot be scheduled, reauthorized, claimed, retried, dispatched, or finalized into a source, and no provider is contacted. Stopped-writer preflight rejects every running transcript job, so no pre-migration request can cross this boundary in flight. A future promotion or legacy-job cleanup requires a separately reviewed migration; 028 does not invent proof that an old client stored no copy.

Before a restricted source may be admitted, the service holds the global artifact namespace plus item metadata/publication locks, waits for every current producer, and validates **both** location columns introduced by migrations 014 and 016: nullable legacy `capture_artifacts.path` and nullable preferred `capture_artifacts.relative_path`. The stored item ID must itself be one canonical nonempty basename component, and the only legal owner directory is the byte-exact canonical `<capture-artifact-root>/<item_id>/`. A non-null relative path must begin with that exact item-ID component and continue as one canonical separator-normalized relative component sequence with no empty/dot/dot-dot/absolute/platform-alias form; a non-null legacy path must be an absolute canonical descendant of that same owner directory. Runtime resolution remains exactly “use `relative_path` when non-null, otherwise `path`.” When both are non-null they must resolve to the identical device/inode target or admission fails. The whole-table namespace scan additionally requires each canonical effective pathname and each `(device,inode)` pair to have exactly one owning item and artifact row; a cross-item or same-item alias, duplicate path, duplicate inode, hardlink, or target outside its owner directory fails even when link count is one. Neither column may point outside the root, traverse a symlink, change device, use an unsafe hardlink, or remain an unclassified legacy value. A row with both locations null is accepted only in the exact `write_status='failed'|'skipped'` no-file shape, while the deterministic whole-item directory is still scanned. It also derives `metadata_cache_scope_hash=SHA-256(typed("youtube-retention-metadata-cache-scope-v1", "youtube_data_api", "video:" || canonical_video_id))` without storing the raw video ID in the obligation. An unprovable path/cache identity or namespace collision blocks source admission for operator-reviewed cleanup.

While an obligation is pending, every new artifact insert anywhere holds that global namespace lock, uses a fresh opaque ID, passes the same owner-directory/two-column/cross-row canonical-path and inode uniqueness validation plus caps, and is immutable afterward. For every preexisting row, admission installs the same immutability fence over `id,item_id,kind,path,relative_path,content_type,sha256,size_bytes,truncated,write_status,error_message,created_at`; update, direct delete, UPSERT, and `REPLACE` of an existing artifact row are forbidden. A matching metadata-cache read/write/delete is fenced from admission through completion, and its insert/update/`REPLACE` guards compare the typed scope hash so another item cannot repopulate the row. At or after the earliest stored `cleanup_start_by`, every artifact producer, item content read/write, chat/RAG retrieval, NotebookLM request/claim/dispatch, Recall/Telegram recapture, enrichment/embedding/transcript claim, metadata-cache access, and source/body mutation is denied. Entering `artifacts_purging` holds the exclusive lock and fences all of them durably; no new same-item obligation or content/path row may appear until completion.

The artifact manifest is the typed hash and count of the raw-UTF-8-ID-sorted immutable tuples `(artifact row ID,item ID,kind,path hash|null,relative_path hash|null,effective canonical root-relative target hash|null,device,inode,type,link count,content_type|null,stored content SHA-256|null,size|null,truncated,write_status,error_message hash|null,created_at)` plus a deterministic whole-item-directory tuple, using domain `youtube-retention-artifact-manifest-v1`. The tuple preserves the distinction between both stored locations while binding the one runtime-effective target; an error string is hashed only for row-integrity comparison and is never copied into an obligation/report. Paths themselves remain in the guarded live artifact rows, not the obligation. Under the exclusive lock the service opens the configured root/item directories by descriptor, rejects symlinks and unsafe hardlinks, and before each operation revalidates `openat`/`fstatat(AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)` device, inode, link count, type, and canonical relative component against the frozen tuple. It unlinks only fd-relative entries with no-follow semantics, fsyncs every affected directory after each batch, removes the empty item directory fd-relatively, and fsyncs every ancestor through the configured artifact-root parent. It then reopens/revalidates the root, recomputes the exact manifest as absent with no replacement, and only then moves all obligations to `artifacts_purged`. Power loss, rename/symlink/hardlink/inode replacement, fsync failure, or a manifest mismatch leaves `artifacts_purging` plus its content-free error. Because guarded database path rows and the frozen manifest remain until the later root transaction, a crash can resume deterministically without guessing a path.

The closed S28 survivor manifest is attested in the schema descriptor and hashed with domain `youtube-retention-survivor-manifest-v1`. After the domain separator it frames the exact descriptor table count. For each canonical ASCII table token in descriptor order, including zero-row tables, it frames `table_token,row_count`; rows then sort lexicographically by raw32 `primary_key_hash` and frame `primary_key_hash,action,preimage_sha256,expected_postimage_sha256`. The primary-key hash is SHA-256 of the same typed table-token/declared-column-token/complete-primary-key preimage used by the backup root descriptor, so the manifest binds every raw typed key without retaining it in the durable action entry. `action` is exactly `delete|retention_tombstone|cascade_verify|must_be_absent`; `preimage_sha256` is the decoded SHA-256 of the §6.12 typed preimage over that table's descriptor-ordered content/linkage columns; expected postimage is nullable. The separately stored `survivor_manifest_count` is the sum of all table row counts and must equal the complete action-entry cardinality. For every formerly linked tag, topic, **and collection** base row, the preimage additionally frames its complete raw-primary-key-sorted live link set. An auto tag, AI topic, or `collections.kind='auto'` row whose only live link is this item has action `delete`. A manual tag, system topic, `collections.kind='manual'`, or any still-shared base row has `cascade_verify` and must survive byte-identically after exactly the restricted item's `item_tags|item_topics|item_collections` link disappears. A kind/source substitution, orphan auto collection, changed shared collection name/description, missing link-set member, extra surviving restricted link, duplicate primary-key hash, or missing/extra/reordered descriptor table fails the manifest. The matching `youtube_data_api/video:<canonical_video_id>` `capture_metadata_cache` row has action `delete`, or its descriptor table is explicitly framed with zero rows when absent.

When obligations enter `artifacts_purging`, the same transaction materializes `youtube_retention_survivor_action_entries` as a `STRICT`, immutable, content-free table with exact columns `item_id TEXT NOT NULL`, `survivor_manifest_hash TEXT NOT NULL`, `ordinal INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(ordinal>=0)`, `table_token TEXT NOT NULL` from the closed descriptor registry, `primary_key_hash TEXT NOT NULL`, `action TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(action IN ('delete','retention_tombstone','cascade_verify','must_be_absent'))`, `preimage_sha256 TEXT NOT NULL`, and nullable `expected_postimage_sha256`; all hashes are lowercase 64-hex. Its primary key is `(item_id,survivor_manifest_hash,ordinal)` and it has non-partial `UNIQUE(item_id,survivor_manifest_hash,table_token,primary_key_hash)`. The entry stores no raw primary key or content; after a crash the service finds a retained row by scanning the descriptor-listed bounded table and comparing the typed primary-key hash. Insert guards allow entries only for that exact all-obligation phase edge, require contiguous ordinals and exact aggregate count/hash, and forbid update/delete/`REPLACE`. These durable entries let `artifacts_purged|db_root_purged` recovery recheck every exact pre/postcondition even after item/link identity has disappeared.

The survivor actions are exhaustive. Every item-scoped chat thread and every library thread containing a strict canonical citation object for the item is `delete`, with messages cascading; malformed/noncanonical citation JSON blocks restricted-source admission. `notebooklm_export_requests` is `must_be_absent`, so any historical/live row denies admission and every later request/enqueue/claim/dispatch remains denied. `recall_sync_items` is rebuilt/guarded so `retention_tombstone` retains only `recall_card_id`, the unchanged `last_seen_at`, and `last_synced_at=root_purge_clock`; it sets `item_id,recall_created_at,recall_source_url,recall_title,recall_image_url,content_hash,imported_at,last_error,metadata_json` to null, `content_fidelity='blocked_unknown'`, `chunk_count=0`, and `sync_status='retention_deleted'`. `telegram_updates` retains only `update_id`, original `created_at`, sets `handled_at=root_purge_clock`, clears `message_id,chat_id,from_id,file_unique_id,item_id,error`, and sets `status='retention_deleted'`. Both tombstone states are immutable/non-resurrecting and have pre-conflict guards. Each `processing_enrollment_job_items` survivor is transformed before root deletion to `item_id=NULL,result='deleted'` with only its content-free job/ordinal/scope hash. Auto/manual/shared collection actions are exactly the link-set rules above. Every other direct or indirect item/video-bearing S28 table—including FTS/vec virtual-table shadows—must be classified in the descriptor as vector-first cascade, one of these exact actions, or explicit zero-row admission; an unknown/no-FK/`SET NULL`/cache/client-persistence surface blocks admission, purge, backup publication, and readiness.

Create `youtube_retention_root_purge_applications` as a `STRICT`, immutable table with this exact column order:

```text
application_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL
seal_id TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE
item_id TEXT NOT NULL
earliest_cleanup_start_by INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
earliest_source_delete_by INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK > earliest_cleanup_start_by
retention_cleanup_budget_ms INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 300000
retention_budget_profile_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
obligation_set_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
obligation_count INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK > 0
artifact_manifest_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
artifact_manifest_count INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
survivor_manifest_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
survivor_manifest_count INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
metadata_cache_scope_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
client_storage_contract_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
created_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
CHECK earliest_cleanup_start_by=earliest_source_delete_by-retention_cleanup_budget_ms
```

Application `created_at` is the irreversible **retirement-authorization** clock sampled when the pre-delete plan commits; it is not a claim that the item root is already absent.

Create `youtube_retention_root_purge_seals` as a `STRICT`, immutable table with this exact column order: `seal_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL`, `application_id TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE`, then the application's columns from `item_id` through `client_storage_contract_hash` in their exact listed order, `retirement_authorized_at INTEGER NOT NULL`, and final `db_root_purged_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(db_root_purged_at>=retirement_authorized_at)`. `retirement_authorized_at` must equal the application's `created_at`; `db_root_purged_at` is independently sampled from the trusted effective clock inside the later root-delete transaction. The seal has no ambiguous second `created_at`. Both tables deliberately omit an item FK so they survive root deletion. Add these two exact non-partial parent keys, with the listed order normative:

```text
root_application_exact_key =
(application_id,seal_id,item_id,earliest_cleanup_start_by,earliest_source_delete_by,
 retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_budget_profile_hash,obligation_set_hash,obligation_count,
 artifact_manifest_hash,artifact_manifest_count,survivor_manifest_hash,survivor_manifest_count,
 metadata_cache_scope_hash,client_storage_contract_hash,created_at)

root_seal_exact_key =
(seal_id,application_id,item_id,earliest_cleanup_start_by,earliest_source_delete_by,
 retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_budget_profile_hash,obligation_set_hash,obligation_count,
 artifact_manifest_hash,artifact_manifest_count,survivor_manifest_hash,survivor_manifest_count,
 metadata_cache_scope_hash,client_storage_contract_hash,retirement_authorized_at,
 db_root_purged_at)
```

The application is an intentionally committable, content-free pre-delete plan: its insert immediately fences the item and readiness while the external retirement is made durable. It therefore has no impossible application-to-future-seal FK. It does have `UNIQUE(application_id,item_id)` plus the deferred reverse plan-outbox FK in §6.11, so the application can commit without a future seal but cannot commit without its exact pending outbox. The outbox points immediately back to that application and its insert trigger recomputes the stable retired tuple/hash from the full application row, using `application.created_at` as stable `retired_at`/retirement authorization. The seal-to-application immediate FK is the fully enumerated ordered application tuple in §6.11, with child `retirement_authorized_at` mapping to application `created_at`; actual `db_root_purged_at` is deliberately outside that preplan FK. The seal insert guard requires `seal_id=application.seal_id`, every copied field, `retirement_authorized_at=application.created_at`, and the exact trusted root-delete clock. An application without a seal is a resumable containment state, never a successful purge or publication state; it cannot be updated, deleted, replaced, bypassed, or used by any content reader/writer except the owning retention service. A seal without its exact application, or an application without its pending outbox, cannot commit.

Create `youtube_retired_item_ids` as a `STRICT`, immutable table with this exact column order:

root_purge_seal_id TEXT nullable

The stable tuple hash is the §6.12 framing of domain `youtube-retired-item-v1` followed **only** by `item_id,earliest_cleanup_start_by,earliest_source_delete_by,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_budget_profile_hash,obligation_set_hash,obligation_count,client_storage_contract_hash,retired_at`; `retired_at` is the immutable external retirement-authorization clock, not a physical-deletion clock. Proof-family IDs/reason and `local_root_absent_at` are excluded so the same external retirement keeps one digest when imported into another sanitized database at a later absence-proof clock. Add `UNIQUE(item_id,retired_tuple_hash)`. `live_retention_purge` requires only `root_purge_seal_id` and its exact non-partial FK is:

```text
(root_purge_seal_id,item_id,earliest_cleanup_start_by,earliest_source_delete_by,

->

```

`backup_scrubbed_restricted_root` requires only the exact copy-application/retired-item relation frozen in §6.3.2 and copies its actual clone-root absence clock. `external_ledger_import` requires only `ledger_sequence|ledger_entry_hash`, with its full ordered stable retirement tuple referencing the verified ledger-entry projection; its insert transaction first deletes/scrubs any candidate root and sets `local_root_absent_at` to that transaction's trusted effective clock, or proves the root was already absent at that clock. A `MATCH FULL` shape guard requires exactly one complete proof family and all fields of the other two null: live has root seal only, backup has copy attestation/application ID only, and import has ledger sequence/hash only. Every column of all three tables is immutable, pre-conflict guards defeat every primary/natural-key `REPLACE`, and the items-insert guard rejects both the database union and the external retired ledger before conflict handling.

Every retention, backup, and restore participant uses one total interprocess lock order over these exact registry tokens: `global_maintenance` → `backup_publication` when applicable → `source_admission_artifact_namespace` → `item_artifact` → `metadata_cache` → `retired_ledger` → `backup_signer_provisioning` when applicable → `sqlite_writer`. A path may omit an irrelevant lock but may never acquire an earlier lock while holding a later one, and no process may wait for another process to acquire a lock it currently holds. Signer provisioning runs with no SQLite transaction open and releases its last token before any later SQLite writer acquisition. Cross-service work persists and fsyncs a content-free handoff checkpoint, releases shared locks in reverse order, lets the next service acquire in canonical order, then reacquires in canonical order and revalidates every database/file/head fact. The §6.3.2 sole reservation supervisor's private per-reservation mutation serializer is state-transition authority, not a canonical/shared lock: workers never hold it, the supervisor never acquires a canonical token, and raw fencing/destruction never waits for canonical reacquisition. The exact token order, allowed subsets, lock implementation/version hashes, supervisor protocol hash, and wait-for graph are part of the capability hash.

Live root retirement is a three-phase protocol under those canonical global maintenance/artifact/metadata/ledger locks and `secure_delete=ON`. First, one immediate database transaction inserts the immutable root application and a matching `pending` ledger outbox while all same-item obligations are exactly `artifacts_purged` with one exact set/manifests/profile. The application `created_at` is the planned external retirement-authorization clock stored as stable `retired_at`; its exact tuple deterministically supplies the stable retired tuple/hash and preselected seal ID. It is never reused as physical-deletion evidence. Committing this plan immediately fences the item, backup, activation, and readiness but leaves the root intact. Second, the ledger service appends, fsyncs, and independently replicates that exact tuple with `origin_evidence_hash` equal to the §6.12 digest of `root_application_exact_key`. A failure before a valid append leaves the application/outbox pending for retry; a valid append is irreversible even if the database later fails.

Only after exact external replication does the final root transaction import the verified ledger entry/projection/head, obtain a fresh latch-free trusted proof, advance the singleton high-water to one transaction clock `T_delete`, require `T_delete>=application.created_at`, apply every durable survivor entry (including cache deletion, collection handling, and exact tombstones), delete all item vectors through bridges, and issue the root `DELETE`. A named `BEFORE DELETE ON items` guard rejects a restricted root unless it sees the exact precommitted application/absent seal, matching externally replicated entry and pending outbox, exact all-obligation set, no vectors, all pre-root survivor actions/postimages, every cascade row matching its frozen preimage, and the sampled `T_delete` in the trusted clock state. A named `AFTER DELETE` proof verifies every descriptor-listed cascade/postimage, updates **all and only** those obligations to `db_root_purged` with `db_root_purged_at=T_delete`, the common application/seal, and exact `root_obligation_set_hash|count`, inserts the immutable live retired-ID tombstone with `retired_at=application.created_at` and `local_root_absent_at=T_delete`, transitions that same outbox `pending -> mirrored` with its exact sequence/hash, and inserts the root seal last with `retirement_authorized_at=application.created_at` and `db_root_purged_at=T_delete`; row-count/hash mismatches abort. Obligation guards admit the `artifacts_purged -> db_root_purged` edge only from that trigger-owned application, and the seal insert guard requires the item already absent, the exact root tombstone/mirrored outbox/ledger tuple, and every exact obligation at `db_root_purged`. Root `DELETE`, UPSERT/`REPLACE`, a seal-only transaction, a root application not accompanied by its exact pending outbox, a partial survivor transform, or a subset/direct obligation edge cannot commit. An application-only planned state is legal solely so the external append can precede deletion, and blocks all non-retention progress until resumed. Ordinary early user deletion invokes this same service; before `artifacts_purged` it aborts.

Create `youtube_retired_item_ledger_outbox` as a `STRICT` immutable-origin table with this exact column order: `item_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL`, `root_purge_application_id TEXT` nullable, `backup_scrub_application_id TEXT` nullable, `retired_item_id TEXT` nullable, `retired_tuple_hash TEXT NOT NULL` lowercase 64-hex, `state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(state IN ('pending','mirrored'))`, `ledger_sequence INTEGER` nullable and positive, `ledger_entry_hash TEXT` nullable lowercase 64-hex, `created_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(created_at>=0)`, and `mirrored_at INTEGER` nullable with `mirrored_at>=created_at`. The exact shape has one origin family. A live `pending` row has non-null `root_purge_application_id`, null `backup_scrub_application_id`, and `retired_item_id|ledger_sequence|ledger_entry_hash|mirrored_at` all null. A live `mirrored` row preserves that root application, keeps the backup application null, sets `retired_item_id=item_id`, and has all three mirror fields non-null. A copy-only `mirrored` row has null root application, non-null `backup_scrub_application_id`, `retired_item_id=item_id`, and all mirror fields non-null; a pending copy row is impossible. The live outbox has the immediate application FK and supplies the deferred reverse parent for the root application; the copy outbox has the immediate scrub-application FK. In both families the insert/transition guard recomputes `retired_tuple_hash` from the complete origin application/per-item plan, so a generic origin ID is not sufficient.

Its deferred FK `(retired_item_id,retired_tuple_hash)` targets `youtube_retired_item_ids(item_id,retired_tuple_hash)` and deliberately skips only while `retired_item_id` is null in the live pre-delete state. Its pre-conflict guard rejects an existing item ID, root application tuple, or non-null terminal tuple before any conflict algorithm. An ordinary live plan inserts only its exact `pending` row; after verified external replication, the sole live edge to `mirrored` fills the four terminal fields in the root-delete transaction and never exits. The copy-only exception lets verified disposable-clone finalization insert an already-`mirrored` row only after the full external entry/projection/state and backup tombstone exist, with `created_at=mirrored_at` equal to that finalization clock. Every completed tuple has the exact ledger-entry FK in §6.11. Update/delete/`REPLACE` outside the live edge is forbidden, and the clone exception is unreachable on live/prior-live/restore-target paths.

Create singleton `youtube_retired_item_ledger_state` as `STRICT` with this exact physical column order: `id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK(id=1)`, `head_sequence INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(head_sequence>=0)`, `head_hash TEXT NOT NULL`, `independently_replicated_sequence INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(independently_replicated_sequence>=0)`, `independently_replicated_hash TEXT NOT NULL`, `verified_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(verified_at>=0)`, and `updated_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(updated_at>=verified_at)`. Both hashes are lowercase 64-hex; sequence zero uses the packaged genesis hash for both and otherwise both sequence/hash pairs must name real projected entries. Readiness/publication requires the independently replicated sequence/hash to equal the local head. The guarded bootstrap inserts only row 1 and rejects collision before conflict handling. Only the ledger reconciler may advance it by the exact next verified entry; rollback, deletion, arbitrary update, and `REPLACE` abort.

Under the same global lock, the separately packaged ledger service drains a committed pending row into permission-restricted, append-only `youtube-retired-item-ledger-v1` outside the database and fsyncs the file plus parent before any root deletion. Each canonical typed ledger entry contains, in order, monotonically contiguous sequence, previous entry hash, every stored field of the stable retirement tuple from `item_id` through `retired_at`, `retired_tuple_hash`, `origin_reason`, `origin_evidence_hash`, and then `entry_hash` and `HMAC-SHA256` under a separate retained `retired_item_ledger_key_v1`; `record_sha256` is the projection's digest of that complete length-prefixed record and is not another wire field. For a live purge, `origin_evidence_hash` is the §6.12 digest of `root_application_exact_key`; for a copy scrub it is the immutable pre-append `scrub_plan_hash` defined in §6.3.2, avoiding a dependency on a future seal/attestation. The ledger head is synchronously replicated to the configured independent recovery location before the root transaction may begin; truncation, fork, sequence gap, bad HMAC, missing remote acknowledgement, a field/digest mismatch, or a database head not contained in the ledger blocks completion, backup publication, restore, item insertion, and readiness. A crash before append leaves the root/application/outbox pending. After a valid append but before root commit, exact full-tuple/hash reconciliation reuses that same external entry without appending twice and resumes the root transaction; it marks the outbox mirrored only in the root-delete transaction after the item is absent and the exact retired row exists. Loss of the live database after append is safe because restore treats the orphan external entry as authoritative and scrubs the item from any candidate. More than one external corroborating entry for an item is legal, but the item-primary-key outbox records exactly the canonical local origin entry; later corroborating entries do not create another local outbox. The purge receipt cannot complete until that one same-item outbox is mirrored.

The ledger wire contract is byte-exact. Let `F(fields)` mean only the concatenated fixed-order typed field frames from §6.12, without a leading domain. For one entry:

```text
P = F(sequence,previous_entry_hash,item_id,earliest_cleanup_start_by,
      earliest_source_delete_by,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,
      retention_budget_profile_hash,obligation_set_hash,obligation_count,
      client_storage_contract_hash,retired_at,retired_tuple_hash,
      origin_reason,origin_evidence_hash)

entry_hash = SHA-256(UTF8("youtube-retired-item-ledger-entry-hash-v1") || 0x00 ||
                    u64be(byte_length(P)) || P)

entry_hmac_sha256 = HMAC-SHA256(retired_item_ledger_key_v1,
                    UTF8("youtube-retired-item-ledger-entry-hmac-v1") || 0x00 ||
                    u64be(byte_length(P)) || P || raw32(entry_hash))

R = u64be(byte_length(P)+64) || P || raw32(entry_hash) || raw32(entry_hmac_sha256)

record_sha256 = SHA-256(R)

genesis_hash = SHA-256(UTF8("youtube-retired-item-ledger-genesis-v1") || 0x00)
```

`previous_entry_hash=genesis_hash` exactly at sequence 1 and otherwise equals the raw-decoded hash of sequence minus one; all stored/rendered hashes are lowercase hex but hash/HMAC preimages use the raw 32 bytes where the formula says `raw32`. The file starts with exact ASCII `youtube-retired-item-ledger-v1\n`. Each following record is exactly `R`, with no padding, JSON, delimiter, or trailing fields. `record_sha256` hashes the length prefix as well as the body and two authenticators, so truncation, length substitution, and record splicing cannot share its evidence binding. `origin_evidence_hash` for live purge is the typed digest with domain `youtube-retention-root-application-v1` over every field of `root_application_exact_key` in its listed order; backup uses the already-defined plan digest. Sequence and every clock/count must fit signed 64-bit SQLite integer range, while record lengths use unsigned 64-bit encoding and are independently bounded by the fixed schema.

Every ledger verifier uses one frozen failure precedence: framing/length, then supplied `record_sha256`, then recomputed `entry_hash`, then `entry_hmac_sha256`, then sequence contiguity, then `previous_entry_hash`, then decoded-field/projection equality. A negative vector recomputes every unrelated authenticator so it reaches only its named invariant. The v1 ledger uses one exact 32-byte `retired_item_ledger_key_v1` whose permission-restricted local and independent-recovery copies are provisioned before 028; the key is never stored in SQLite, ordinary backups, logs, or reports. Schema 028 permits no rotation or key-ID substitution. Loss/disagreement blocks readiness and restore; rotation requires a separately reviewed v2 chain/bridge contract that retains v1 verification. Writer, crash reconciler, projection importer, backup scrubber, independent replica verifier, and restore consume one shared encoder and the content-hashed conformance fixture named in §6.12, covering genesis, two chained entries, non-ASCII opaque item IDs, signed-int64 boundaries, an actually truncated record, a length-prefix substitution, field tampering with recomputed record hash, a wrong signing key, HMAC substitution, entry-hash substitution, record-SHA substitution, a cryptographically valid fork, a cryptographically valid contiguous record with a decoded source-projection substitution, and reversed record order. Every consumer must return the fixture's exact named outcome with no projection, head, root, or outbox mutation. The SQLite projection insert guard consumes only the dedicated verifier context bound to exact `R`, `record_sha256`, and every decoded `NEW` field; caller-supplied hex fields alone never establish verification.

After that committed root transaction and exact external-ledger mirroring, the still-held global lock uses the already direct-initialized embedded restricted vec0 runtime on the attested owning connection, runs a successful full `VACUUM`, and checkpoints with `wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)`. It uses the durable survivor entries/application/seal to prove every postimage after restart, and proves the restricted sentinel absent from main pages, zero freelist, WAL, SHM, rollback journal, VACUUM temp files, artifact roots, metadata cache, and every server/client-persistence canary before receipt completion. Later content-free phase/receipt writes may create a content-free WAL; the impossible invariant “no live sidecar exists” is not required.

A crash in `artifacts_purging|artifacts_purged|db_root_purged` or any retention error blocks startup progress, backup, capture, processing, activation, and readiness until the idempotent service resumes. An ordinary pending obligation strictly before its stored `retention_cleanup_start_by` is not a partial phase and may coexist only with the proven live retention units/capability below.

#### 6.3.2 Backup, restore, startup, and activation retention gate

Stage 2 replaces the NotebookLM-only scrub call at **every** database snapshot producer and restore path with one hash-attested `brain-sensitive-backup-scrub-v2`. Its schema classifier is closed: pre-026 snapshots remain rejected by the protected-main NotebookLM provider-write latch; exact S26 and S27 are accepted only through the current runtime's existing latch plus PR #55 URL-source handling; exact S28 additionally requires this v2 descriptor; partial/lookalike S28, wrong hash, or unknown-newer schema fails closed. For S28, a published backup contains no restricted item root, survivor/cache/client-contract content, or nonterminal retention control, regardless of whether its deadline is future. Already-complete content-free live-purge histories and their root-seal retired IDs remain intact.

Before removing each still-present restricted root from the disposable copy, v2 acquires the canonical `global_maintenance` → `backup_publication` → `source_admission_artifact_namespace` → `retired_ledger` lock subset, verifies the complete current external chain, and freezes its exact head as the plan base head. If the clone projection is behind, it opens the clone once as `ledger_import_v1`, imports only the exact contiguous suffix from the copied head through that already-frozen base head, commits, and closes; a clone head ahead of, forked from, or otherwise inconsistent with the frozen base fails. It then computes the distinct retired-ID union and treats every ledger-retired ID that still has a candidate root as a mandatory planned removal. It computes a **copy-specific** survivor manifest from the current pre-delete rows. This is mandatory even for an ordinary live `pending` obligation whose live artifact/survivor manifest fields are correctly null. If durable live survivor entries already exist, the copy-specific set must equal them exactly; otherwise the copy set is authoritative only for this scrub and cannot mutate the live obligation. Still under that lock subset it closes and hashes the raw clone after any base-ledger projection/state import, preselects one attestation ID, and freezes every root/obligation/action tuple with the unchanged base head. It reopens the same canonical device/inode only as `backup_plan_v1`, starts one immediate plan transaction, arms the one-shot §6.12 external-evidence verifier with kind `backup_plan_input_v1`, subject `attestation_id`, the closed-file SHA-256, transaction nonce, frozen plan facts, and exact prepared application-insert manifest, then inserts every immutable planned-item row and copy-action row under deferred application FKs. It inserts the immutable scrub application last; the application guard consumes that plan-input proof, sees the complete child sets, recomputes both aggregates, verifies every current pre-delete root/action, and closes the deferred relations. No child may be added after the application exists. The transaction commits only that content-free plan and the role closes before `backup_scrub_v1` may open. Its `scrub_plan_hash` is the §6.12 framing of domain `brain-sensitive-backup-scrub-plan-v1` followed by every application field except itself, in the exact order below.
