The scrubber first holds the transition subset `global_maintenance` → `backup_publication` → `source_admission_artifact_namespace`, obtains the exact supervisor snapshot, and requests the sole supervisor to install the complete `backup_scrub_v1` binding using the expected record revision/hash, authenticated writer identity, exact role/command mapping, and transition-lock proof; only the installed successor authorizes work. It then releases the transition locks in reverse order and acquires the separate clone-work subset `global_maintenance` → `backup_publication` → `retired_ledger`; because this subset omits source admission, no later transition lock may be acquired until the entire subset is released. On that unpublished disposable clone only, a second transaction runs even when the planned restricted-root set is empty. It first deletes every `youtube_browser_upload_grants` row, then every `youtube_browser_capture_intents` row, asserts both complete table counts are zero, and rotates the authority-epoch singleton once to the plan's fresh `backup_sanitize_v1` ID under the exact clone-only guard bypass. It then deletes vectors first, applies the frozen copy actions, removes each planned root and other nonterminal control, records each actual `local_root_absent_at` from its trusted transaction clock, and preserves the immutable schedule reservation. Permanent inspect issuances and immutable extension receipts remain byte-identical. It sets `journal_mode=DELETE`, `temp_store=MEMORY`, and `secure_delete=ON`, uses the already direct-initialized embedded restricted vec0 runtime on the attested `backup_scrub_v1` connection, performs full `VACUUM`, then runs full `PRAGMA integrity_check`, `quick_check`, `foreign_key_check`, and the descriptor-complete scrub-stage zero scan for restricted roots/nonterminal obligations/content/cache/client sentinels plus the two mutable browser-authority tables. The scan uses domain `brain-sensitive-backup-scrub-stage-zero-scan-v1`. Only exact single-row lowercase `ok`, exact lowercase `ok`, zero FK rows, and the exact scrub-stage zero-scan digest with zero total and per-surface violation counts may proceed. Only after those checks pass does the held ledger lock append/fsync/independently replicate an exact backup-origin entry with `origin_evidence_hash=scrub_plan_hash` for each planned item absent from the frozen base ledger; an already-ledger-retired planned item reuses its earliest verified canonical entry and is not appended merely to manufacture a new tuple.

For a nonempty required-live-item set, the still-active scrub writer writes/fsyncs a content-free handoff checkpoint containing the clone identity, plan hash, appended head, required live item set, and unchanged sanitize/destroy deadlines. It then releases `retired_ledger`, `backup_publication`, and `global_maintenance` in reverse order, with no SQLite transaction open. Only after the entire clone-work subset is gone does it reacquire `global_maintenance` → `backup_publication` → `source_admission_artifact_namespace`, obtain a current supervisor snapshot, and revalidate the exact active writer generation, checkpoint, clone identity, plan, committed marker, phase, and unchanged deadlines. It requests the supervisor, with expected record revision/hash and transition-lock proof, to install the complete `sanitizing -> handoff` successor without unbinding, then releases that transition subset in reverse order. It waits while that same authenticated writer generation remains active but holds no shared lock or SQLite transaction. The owning live retention service acquires the canonical live-purge lock sequence and drives every corresponding still-present live root through its ordinary exact purge, including its separately corroborating live ledger entry. The scrub writer then reacquires the full canonical subset `global_maintenance` → `backup_publication` → `source_admission_artifact_namespace` → `retired_ledger`; obtains a fresh supervisor snapshot; revalidates the same writer/checkpoint/clone device/inode and plan, authority-epoch rotation, complete monotonic external/projection head, exact live retired proofs, scrub-stage zero-scan facts, and unchanged deadlines; and only then requests the supervisor to install the same active binding's `handoff -> finalizing` successor. It releases that full subset in reverse order after retaining the revalidated facts. Any new ledger entry covered by the frozen plan/final retired set is retained as an exact contiguous suffix for the first statements of the later `backup_attestation_v1` transaction; `backup_scrub_v1` does not import it. A new head that requires an unplanned clone mutation fences/destroys the clone and restarts from a fresh snapshot. Inability to complete or revalidate this handoff requests fencing and keeps the backup unpublished. No nonempty path acquires source admission while retaining `retired_ledger`.

For an empty required-live-item set, no handoff checkpoint, lock-free wait, or `handoff` phase exists. After the same scrub checks it releases the complete clone-work subset in reverse order, reacquires the full canonical `global_maintenance` → `backup_publication` → `source_admission_artifact_namespace` → `retired_ledger` subset, obtains a current supervisor snapshot, revalidates the exact active writer, committed marker, empty plan, clone identity, authority-epoch rotation, scrub-stage facts, ledger head, and deadlines, and only then requests the supervisor to install `sanitizing -> finalizing` from the expected record revision/hash; it releases the full subset in reverse order. In both branches the one scrub generation remains active until the pre-finalization evidence in the next paragraph exists, then closes and requests the supervisor to install the legal idle successor. The sanitize benchmark and budget include the nonempty worst-case release, live purge, contention, reacquisition, revalidation, and phase transitions.

Immediately before clone finalization, the tool repeats full `integrity_check`, `quick_check`, `foreign_key_check`, the descriptor-complete pre-finalization zero scan, page/freelist facts, and current ledger-head facts against the still-unpublished clone. That scan uses domain `brain-sensitive-backup-pre-finalization-zero-scan-v1`; its surface row/byte measurements are not compared with either other scan phase. The tool closes the `backup_scrub_v1` connection and hashes that exact closed file as `pre_finalization_database_sha256`. It computes `pre_finalization_check_evidence_sha256` as the §6.12 typed digest with domain `brain-sensitive-backup-pre-finalization-checks-v1` over this exact order: `attestation_id,scrub_plan_hash,pre_finalization_database_sha256,pre_finalization_database_size_bytes,pre_finalization_page_size,pre_finalization_page_count,pre_finalization_freelist_count,pre_finalization_integrity_check_token,pre_finalization_quick_check_token,pre_finalization_foreign_key_violation_count,pre_finalization_zero_scan_hash,pre_finalization_zero_scan_violation_count,pre_finalization_retired_ledger_head_sequence,pre_finalization_retired_ledger_head_hash`. Integers use signed-int64 frames and every digest uses raw32 framing. It retains those verified facts in host memory, holds the required transition subset, obtains the current supervisor snapshot, and requests the exact `backup_attestation_v1` bind with expected record revision/hash, role/command mapping, writer identity, and lock proof. Only after that successor is installed does it reopen the one owning writer connection on the same canonical device/inode and begin the final immediate transaction; it does not arm the pre-finalization context yet.

One final `backup_attestation_v1` clone transaction uses this only viable insert order: import each missing resulting ledger projection through its own sequentially armed/consumed `retired_ledger_record_verified_v1` context and advance the head; insert each `brain_sensitive_backup_scrub_retired_items` row; insert its exact rootless `youtube_retired_item_ids` backup tombstone; and insert its already-`mirrored` copy outbox only after that tombstone exists. Immediately before the sole final attestation statement, the host arms `external_evidence_verified_v1` with kind `backup_pre_finalization_checks_v1`, subject `attestation_id`, the retained digest/facts, current transaction nonce, same device/inode, and exact prepared attestation manifest; the attestation trigger consumes it once. Projection/head → copy-retired row → tombstone → copy outbox → arm pre-finalization context → attestation is normative. At most one verifier context exists at any point. The transaction's immediate application/ledger FKs and deferred tombstone/outbox relations all resolve in that order. After commit, while publication locks remain held, the tool checkpoints with `TRUNCATE`, closes the attestation connection, proves WAL/SHM/rollback-journal/temp absence, opens the exact final file only as `immutable_verify_v1`, reruns full integrity/quick/FK/page/freelist/head checks and the descriptor-complete `brain-sensitive-backup-post-finalization-zero-scan-v1` scan, closes without creating a sidecar, hashes the closed main file, and only then creates and HMACs the detached attestation. The post-finalization proof has no SQL verifier or in-database field because it cannot exist before the final transaction; any post-check, closure, sidecar-absence, hash, or sidecar-write failure leaves the clone unpublished and destroys it, while already-replicated ledger effects remain authoritative. Thus no in-database attestation claims a `VACUUM` or integrity result that had not occurred, no backup-origin retirement coexists with an exposed live root after the backup lock is released, and no copy can publish between plan, scrub, ledger, live reconciliation, and finalization phases. This conservative contract may delete a restricted live root earlier than its maximum deadline when a backup is requested; source admission and UI disclosure state that behavior, and the deadline remains a hard latest-delete bound.

A failure before the first external append destroys the volatile clone and leaves the live root governed by its existing obligation. An append that reaches the protected ledger is intentionally irreversible: if any later append, live purge, clone transaction, check, or sidecar step fails, every successfully mirrored entry remains authoritative, the partial clone is destroyed, and the live process stays fenced until the retention service reconciles that new head and purges every now-ledger-retired live root. A partial multi-item append retires exactly its valid prefix; it is never truncated or reused. This conservative orphan-entry rule closes the unavoidable cross-filesystem commit gap. A backup-copy scrub treats both artifact location columns only as database content to remove and never follows either into the live artifact root; live files remain governed by the live obligation until the required live purge performs their fd-safe deletion. This contract covers in-process backups, offsite/B2 backups, immutable pre-processing backups, Recall preflight backups, deployment backups, and every future producer found by a repository scan.

Every scrub application binds one independently authenticated key-provisioning receipt. The recovery control plane serializes that external append-only receipt with typed domain `brain-sensitive-backup-key-provisioning-v1` and this exact field order:

```text
receipt_id,operation_id,provisioning_kind,input_evidence_kind,input_evidence_sha256,
prior_backup_key_id_hash,new_backup_key_id_hash,local_signer_store_identity_sha256,
recovery_verifier_store_identity_sha256,recovery_install_ack_sha256,
verification_challenge_sha256,operator_artifact_sha256,scrubber_tool_hash,
retention_capability_hash,retired_ledger_head_sequence,retired_ledger_head_hash,created_at
```

`provisioning_kind` is exactly `prepositioned_dual_copy_v1|declared_root_loss_reprovision_v1`; `prior_backup_key_id_hash` alone may be null for the initial prepositioned provision. All IDs/enums are bounded canonical text, every named digest is decoded raw32, clocks/sequences are nonnegative signed-int64 frames, and `operator_artifact_sha256` is the digest of the permission-restricted approval artifact rather than its contents. The receipt is authenticated by the independent recovery-store control-plane identity, not by the historical or new backup HMAC key. Its typed SHA-256 is stored below. Plan-input verification replays the receipt, checks both immutable store-identity digests, install acknowledgement, local challenge/recovery result, tool/capability hashes, exact external-ledger head, operation/input evidence, prior/new key lineage, and selected local active signer before arming the plan statement.

Sanitized S28 authority is physically frozen in five `STRICT`, copy-only tables. `brain_sensitive_backup_scrub_applications` is immutable and has this exact all-non-null column order:

```text
attestation_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL
  CHECK(length(attestation_id)=26
    AND attestation_id=lower(attestation_id)
    AND attestation_id NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-hjkmnp-tv-z]*')
scrub_plan_hash TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE lowercase 64-hex
input_database_sha256 TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
scrub_contract TEXT NOT NULL exact brain-sensitive-backup-scrub-v2
backup_key_id_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
backup_key_provisioning_kind TEXT NOT NULL exact prepositioned_dual_copy_v1|declared_root_loss_reprovision_v1
backup_key_provisioning_receipt_sha256 TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
migration_ledger_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
schema_descriptor_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
guard_descriptor_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
scrubber_tool_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
sqlite_runtime_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
sqlite_vec_runtime_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
scalar_registry_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
retention_capability_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
removed_item_set_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
removed_item_count INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
removed_obligation_set_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
removed_obligation_count INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
survivor_action_set_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
survivor_action_count INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
retired_ledger_base_sequence INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
retired_ledger_base_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
effective_clock_ms INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
max_observed_wall_ms INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0 AND >= effective_clock_ms
created_at INTEGER NOT NULL exact effective_clock_ms
```

It has one exact non-partial parent key over all columns in that listed order. The provisioning kind and receipt digest enter the exact `brain-sensitive-backup-scrub-plan-v1` preimage in that position. `input_database_sha256` is specifically the closed clone after verified base-ledger projection/state import and before any copy-only plan row is written; the separately verified source backup hash remains in its detached input attestation. The application insert trigger runs last in the plan transaction and before any copy deletion; it requires `external_evidence_verified_v1('backup_plan_input_v1',attestation_id,input_database_sha256)=1`, verifies the current ledger base head, selected active local signer and independently authenticated provisioning receipt, descriptor/runtime/capability hashes, the complete planned-root and action child sets, trusted clock, every aggregate below, and recomputed plan hash. `prepositioned_dual_copy_v1` requires the exact normal two-role receipt; `declared_root_loss_reprovision_v1` requires the current operation's fresh root-loss receipt and new key ID. Update/delete/`REPLACE` abort.

`brain_sensitive_backup_scrub_planned_items` is the durable, pre-delete witness and has these exact all-non-null columns in order: `attestation_id TEXT`, `item_id TEXT`, `earliest_cleanup_start_by INTEGER`, `earliest_source_delete_by INTEGER`, `retention_cleanup_budget_ms INTEGER`, `retention_budget_profile_hash TEXT`, `obligation_set_hash TEXT`, `obligation_count INTEGER`, `survivor_manifest_hash TEXT`, `survivor_manifest_count INTEGER`, `client_storage_contract_hash TEXT`, `retired_at INTEGER`, `retired_tuple_hash TEXT`, and `root_preimage_sha256 TEXT`; types/checks match their live/ledger counterparts, and its primary key is `(attestation_id,item_id)`. It has a deferred FK to the exact application ID because it is inserted before the application, plus this immutable non-partial parent key:

```text
(attestation_id,item_id,earliest_cleanup_start_by,earliest_source_delete_by,
 retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_budget_profile_hash,obligation_set_hash,obligation_count,
 survivor_manifest_hash,survivor_manifest_count,client_storage_contract_hash,retired_at,
 retired_tuple_hash)
```

Each planned row is admitted only while its application is absent and the exact pre-delete root, obligation set, survivor actions, root preimage, trusted clock, and canonical external-ledger state are present. For an item absent from the frozen base ledger, `retired_at` is the preselected application `effective_clock_ms`; for an already-ledger-retired root, the complete stable tuple and earliest canonical `retired_at` copy the verified entry. In both cases the guard recomputes `retired_tuple_hash`. The application inserted last requires exactly `removed_item_count` distinct planned rows, recomputes `removed_item_set_hash` solely from these rows, and denies a missing, extra, substituted, duplicate-key, or post-application row. Update/delete/`REPLACE` always abort.

`brain_sensitive_backup_scrub_survivor_action_entries` has exact columns `attestation_id,item_id,ordinal,table_token,primary_key_hash,action,preimage_sha256,expected_postimage_sha256`, with the same types/checks as the live survivor table, primary key `(attestation_id,item_id,ordinal)`, and `UNIQUE(attestation_id,item_id,table_token,primary_key_hash)`. Each action has an immediate `(attestation_id,item_id)` FK to its already-inserted planned row and a deferred application FK. Its insert guard permits only the verified disposable-clone scrubber before that application exists, requires contiguous ordinals and the current pre-delete copy rows, and forbids update/delete/`REPLACE`. The application inserted last recomputes `survivor_action_set_hash|count` from the complete action set and closes both deferred application relations.

The four backup aggregate hashes are byte-exact. Each uses §6.12 frames, begins with its UTF-8 domain plus `0x00`, then one signed-int64 frame containing the exact stored row count, followed by every sorted row's fields without a row separator. Counts must fit nonnegative signed int64. A duplicate sort key aborts rather than relying on a tie-breaker; the empty set is the domain plus the framed integer zero and no row fields.

- `removed_item_set_hash` uses domain `brain-sensitive-backup-removed-item-set-v1`. Rows are exactly `brain_sensitive_backup_scrub_planned_items`, sort by raw UTF-8 `item_id`, and frame, in order, `item_id,earliest_cleanup_start_by,earliest_source_delete_by,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_budget_profile_hash,obligation_set_hash,obligation_count,survivor_manifest_hash,survivor_manifest_count,client_storage_contract_hash,retired_at,retired_tuple_hash,root_preimage_sha256`. Thus this aggregate is not an IDs-only set and remains independently recomputable after root deletion.
- `removed_obligation_set_hash` uses domain `brain-sensitive-backup-removed-obligation-set-v1`. Rows sort first by raw UTF-8 `item_id`, then raw UTF-8 `obligation_id`, and frame the complete immutable pre-delete obligation tuple in this order: `obligation_id,item_id,policy_decision_id,accepted_content_revision,source_delete_by,source_kind,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_cleanup_start_by,retention_budget_profile_hash,metadata_cache_scope_hash,client_storage_contract_hash,admission_evidence_kind,admission_evidence_id,created_at,artifact_manifest_hash,artifact_manifest_count,survivor_manifest_hash,survivor_manifest_count`. Nullable manifest fields use the null frame.
- `survivor_action_set_hash` uses domain `brain-sensitive-backup-survivor-action-set-v1`. Rows are the retained copy-action entries sorted by raw UTF-8 `item_id`, then numeric `ordinal`, and frame `item_id,ordinal,table_token,primary_key_hash,action,preimage_sha256,expected_postimage_sha256` in that order. Contiguous zero-based ordinals and the table/key unique constraint make another tie impossible.
- `resulting_retired_id_set_hash` uses domain `brain-sensitive-backup-resulting-retired-id-set-v1`. Rows sort by raw UTF-8 `item_id` and frame every physical `youtube_retired_item_ids` column in its exact table order: `item_id,reason,root_purge_seal_id,backup_scrub_attestation_id,ledger_sequence,ledger_entry_hash,earliest_cleanup_start_by,earliest_source_delete_by,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_budget_profile_hash,obligation_set_hash,obligation_count,client_storage_contract_hash,retired_tuple_hash,retired_at,local_root_absent_at`.

Nested root hashes are also exact. For each descriptor-listed table, `PK` is the concatenation of §6.12 frames for its complete primary-key columns in declared order. `primary_key_hash=SHA-256(UTF8("brain-sensitive-backup-root-primary-key-v1") || 0x00 || frame_text(table_token) || frame_int64(primary_key_column_count) ||` each `frame_text(column_token) || frame_value(column_value)` in declared order `)`. `row_preimage_sha256` uses domain `brain-sensitive-backup-root-row-preimage-v1`, then the table token, total stored-column count, and each descriptor-order `column_token,value`, including nulls. Column tokens and counts are framed; no implicit table/column position or JSON is accepted. Duplicate raw32 `primary_key_hash` values abort. `root_preimage_sha256` uses domain `brain-sensitive-backup-root-preimage-v1`, then one framed table count and, for every table in schema-descriptor order including zero-row tables, `table_token,row_count`, followed by its rows sorted lexicographically by raw32 `primary_key_hash`, each as raw32 `primary_key_hash,row_preimage_sha256`. Every count is a nonnegative signed-int64 frame. The descriptor hash freezes table/column tokens, declared order, types, and the exact root/control inventory.

The literal empty aggregate vectors, including the framed zero count, are respectively `398f4b7fb03e0f3c1fe0d9ba2f26895ba42475f10f23e7b037595554ea86b2dd`, `77f9c173f8f95695cc17381301b2a9f4597975ee276171d21db6da3aab5c745a`, `64b7ccf6723f2ffacca86ae033855117aa88fd86adf5eb6d3f4b0107c5af38cb`, and `a6678a8e0a86c41e661533d1ebfdb35200343da6c5f501002746d44a9c073b8f`. The content-hashed conformance fixture in §6.12 independently freezes two-row sorting, nullable fields, nested PK/row/root preimages, all four aggregate field orders, and every digest. Application insert recomputes the first three from the durable plan rows/current clone before deletion; attestation insert retains those immutable application values, recomputes the resulting retired set from final rows, and proves an exact one-to-one planned/retired ID and stable-tuple match rather than pretending the full-set hashes are interchangeable.

`brain_sensitive_backup_scrub_retired_items` has exact all-non-null columns, in order: `attestation_id TEXT`, `item_id TEXT`, `earliest_cleanup_start_by INTEGER`, `earliest_source_delete_by INTEGER`, `retention_cleanup_budget_ms INTEGER`, `retention_budget_profile_hash TEXT`, `obligation_set_hash TEXT`, `obligation_count INTEGER`, `survivor_manifest_hash TEXT`, `survivor_manifest_count INTEGER`, `client_storage_contract_hash TEXT`, `retired_tuple_hash TEXT`, `retired_at INTEGER`, `local_root_absent_at INTEGER`, `ledger_sequence INTEGER`, and `ledger_entry_hash TEXT`; hashes/checks match their live/ledger counterparts, `local_root_absent_at>=retired_at`, its primary key is `(attestation_id,item_id)`, and it adds this exact non-partial parent key:

```text
(attestation_id,item_id,earliest_cleanup_start_by,earliest_source_delete_by,
 retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_budget_profile_hash,obligation_set_hash,obligation_count,
 client_storage_contract_hash,retired_at,local_root_absent_at,retired_tuple_hash)
```

The copy retired-ID row's deferred FK uses exactly that tuple. Each retired entry has an **immediate** application FK and an immediate FK from its exact shared `attestation_id,item_id,deadline,budget,profile,obligation,survivor,client,retired_at,retired_tuple_hash` fields to the planned-item parent key above. Its survivor hash/count must equal the exact copy-action entries for that item, and its full stable retirement tuple plus `ledger_sequence|ledger_entry_hash` has the exact immediate verified ledger-entry FK in §6.11. `local_root_absent_at` is the trusted effective clock sampled in the earlier clone root-delete transaction and is independently bound by the scrub plan/action proof. For an item absent from the base ledger, `retired_at=application.created_at` and the row selects the new backup-origin entry; for an already-ledger-retired planned root, every stable field and authorization clock copies the earliest verified canonical entry while `local_root_absent_at` remains the actual clone absence-proof clock. The row is inserted only after external replication and before its rootless backup tombstone; update/delete/`REPLACE` abort.

`brain_sensitive_backup_scrub_attestations` has these exact all-non-null columns in order: `attestation_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY` with the same 26-character lowercase Crockford-base32 check as the application, `scrub_plan_hash`, `input_database_sha256`, `scrub_contract TEXT` exact `brain-sensitive-backup-scrub-v2`, `backup_key_id_hash`, `backup_key_provisioning_kind`, `backup_key_provisioning_receipt_sha256`, `migration_ledger_hash`, `schema_descriptor_hash`, `guard_descriptor_hash`, `scrubber_tool_hash`, `sqlite_runtime_hash`, `sqlite_vec_runtime_hash`, `scalar_registry_hash`, `retention_capability_hash`, `removed_item_set_hash`, `removed_item_count`, `removed_obligation_set_hash`, `removed_obligation_count`, `resulting_retired_id_set_hash`, `resulting_retired_id_count`, `survivor_action_set_hash`, `survivor_action_count`, `retired_ledger_base_sequence`, `retired_ledger_base_hash`, `retired_ledger_head_sequence`, `retired_ledger_head_hash`, `effective_clock_ms`, `max_observed_wall_ms`, `application_created_at`, `pre_finalization_database_sha256`, `pre_finalization_database_size_bytes`, `pre_finalization_page_size`, `pre_finalization_page_count`, `pre_finalization_freelist_count`, `pre_finalization_integrity_check_token`, `pre_finalization_quick_check_token`, `pre_finalization_foreign_key_violation_count` exact zero, `pre_finalization_zero_scan_hash`, `pre_finalization_zero_scan_violation_count` exact zero, `pre_finalization_retired_ledger_head_sequence`, `pre_finalization_retired_ledger_head_hash`, `pre_finalization_check_evidence_sha256`, and `completed_at`; IDs/hashes/integers use §6 stored-class and bound checks, the provisioning fields use the closed enum/digest checks above, both pre-finalization check tokens are exact lowercase `ok`, `pre_finalization_database_size_bytes=pre_finalization_page_size*pre_finalization_page_count` under checked arithmetic, `application_created_at=effective_clock_ms`, and `completed_at>=application_created_at`. The copied application fields have the exact full ordered application FK, including provisioning kind/receipt equality, while the attestation insert trigger recomputes the frozen aggregates; proves every planned item has exactly one equal stable-tuple retired row and vice versa; proves the resulting retired-ID set is a superset of the input set and exactly equals the external ledger's distinct item-ID set through the recorded final-transaction head; verifies the exact contiguous ledger advance from the frozen base, mirrored outboxes, independently authenticated provisioning receipt, the independently phase-bound pre-finalization zero scan, and already-run full integrity/quick/FK facts; recomputes the complete pre-finalization evidence digest from the persisted fields; and requires `external_evidence_verified_v1('backup_pre_finalization_checks_v1',attestation_id,pre_finalization_check_evidence_sha256)=1` before consuming that one-shot context. The attestation is the final one-to-one row for its application; application/planned/action/retired rows may exist only in volatile unpublished intermediate state, and publication requires every application to have exactly one valid attestation.

All three zero scans use the same exact §6.12 payload `Z` but non-interchangeable domains: scrub-stage `brain-sensitive-backup-scrub-stage-zero-scan-v1`, pre-finalization `brain-sensitive-backup-pre-finalization-zero-scan-v1`, and post-finalization `brain-sensitive-backup-post-finalization-zero-scan-v1`. `Z` begins with one signed-int64 frame for the descriptor's exact surface count. For every surface in schema-descriptor order—including every table, cache, client, artifact, main-page, freelist, WAL, SHM, rollback-journal, and temp token even when absent—it then frames `surface_token,scanned_row_count,scanned_byte_count,surface_violation_count` in that order. A final signed-int64 frame is the phase's summed violation count. All counts are nonnegative and checked; every per-surface violation count and each phase total must be zero. Surface row/byte counts are measured facts and may change between phases, including because finalization inserts projection, retired-item, tombstone, outbox, and attestation rows. The three hashes are never required to equal; phase-domain substitution is invalid even if two payloads happen to match. Surface tokens are unique canonical ASCII text; a duplicate/missing/extra/reordered token, overflow, implicit absent surface, or content/path value aborts. The descriptor hash freezes the complete surface registry and order.

After the final attestation commit, the tool checkpoints/truncates, closes every connection, proves no WAL/SHM/rollback-journal/temp file exists, and performs a second immutable read-only open under the attested SQLite/scalar registry. That pass reruns full `integrity_check`, `quick_check`, `foreign_key_check`, the complete post-finalization zero scan, final ledger-head verification, `page_size`, `page_count`, and `freelist_count`, then closes without creating a sidecar and hashes the closed main file. It writes canonical fixed-key `<backup>.scrub-v2.attestation.json` with the exact in-database column names in their order above followed by these detached-only keys in this exact order:

```text
database_sha256
database_size_bytes
final_page_size
final_page_count
final_freelist_count
final_integrity_check_token
final_quick_check_token
final_foreign_key_violation_count
post_finalization_zero_scan_hash
post_finalization_zero_scan_violation_count
wal_sidecar_state
shm_sidecar_state
rollback_journal_state
temp_surface_state
hmac_sha256
```

`database_size_bytes=final_page_size*final_page_count` under checked arithmetic; page size/count are positive safe integers; `final_freelist_count`, the post-finalization violation count, and every in-database pre-finalization violation count are independently zero; both final check tokens are exact lowercase `ok`; and each of the four sidecar-state values is exact `absent`. The historical pre-finalization and current post-finalization scan hashes are deliberately not compared and are expected to differ whenever finalization changes a measured surface. Integers are canonical base-10 JSON integers with no plus sign or leading zero except `0`; all other values are lowercase ASCII JSON strings. The field-specific attestation-ID check plus the closed enum/hash alphabets guarantee that no string contains a quote, reverse solidus, control character, non-ASCII byte, or uppercase byte, so serialization never escapes or case-folds a value. No value is null, Boolean, array, object, float, or exponent.

Detached serialization is byte-exact, not generic “canonical JSON.” `canonical_utf8_without_hmac` is one UTF-8 JSON object containing every listed key except `hmac_sha256` in the exact listed order: byte `{`, each ASCII key in double quotes, byte `:`, its value, comma between pairs, and final byte `}`. There is no whitespace, BOM, escaping opportunity in the closed ASCII key/string alphabets, slash escaping, duplicate/extra key, or trailing newline in this HMAC preimage. Let `sans_len` be its byte length. The exact final bytes are `canonical_utf8_without_hmac[0:sans_len-1] || UTF8(",\"hmac_sha256\":\"") || ASCII(hmac_hex) || UTF8("\"}\n")`; this removes the sans-HMAC closing brace before appending the final member and ends with exactly one LF. The LF is not HMACed. The HMAC is `HMAC-SHA256(selected backup-attestation key, UTF8("brain-sensitive-backup-attestation-v2") || 0x00 || canonical_utf8_without_hmac)`. The serialization self-vector uses a 32-byte zero test key and sans-HMAC object bytes `7b2261223a302c2262223a2278227d` (`{"a":0,"b":"x"}`), producing `74087051ee7389b74db5d65eafa4c7c8b5affc768d494422d11ce0ba06ffe855`; the content-hashed conformance fixture freezes the complete real key order, sans-HMAC bytes, final bytes, and rejection cases.

Signing selects the exact **active local signer** for which `SHA-256(UTF8("brain-sensitive-backup-key-id-v1") || 0x00 || raw32(key))` equals the row's `backup_key_id_hash`; its authenticated provisioning receipt must hash to the row's digest and have the row's exact kind. Publication sends the canonical sans-HMAC bytes plus HMAC to the recovery verifier, which resolves that receipt/version and returns only a verification result before publication can proceed. The tool fsyncs the database, sidecar, and publication parent directory before atomic publication. The in-database row intentionally omits the self-referential final-file hash/HMAC and detached filesystem-state facts. A count alone, an unattested database, a detached/mismatched sidecar, a sidecar without the database, a post-check file-size/page mismatch, a present sidecar surface, an unavailable receipt/version, a local/recovery disagreement, a recovery signing/export capability, or an attested retired set behind the external ledger is invalid. An unavailable current local signer always blocks **output publication**. Declared data-root loss is permission to verify the historical input through the independent verifier and then execute the fresh reprovisioning edge; it is never permission to sign output with that historical recovery key.

Live delete guards remain absolute. The only scrub exception operates on a fresh verified disposable **clone**: while live release/backup and artifact-publication locks are held, v2 proves the candidate's canonical path is inside its newly created volatile staging directory, its device/inode differs from every live/prior-live/restore-target database, and no hardlink or sidecar aliases an original surface. It drops only the descriptor-listed item/chunk/obligation/application/seal/receipt delete guards plus the one authority-epoch update guard; ordinary mutable-grant/intent cleanup guards remain present and require grant-first deletion. It performs the exact clone-wide grant deletion → intent deletion → empty-count assertions → one-shot epoch rotation before the vector-first S28 root scrub, recreates every dropped guard from packaged SQL, and requires the complete original schema descriptor/hash before `VACUUM` or attestation. Permanent inspect-issuance and immutable receipt guards are never dropped. Any wrong path, nonvolatile/persistent staging location, original inode, hardlink, missing lock, wrong operation/previous/fresh epoch, nonempty mutable authority, wrong runtime/hash, extra/missing guard, or descriptor drift aborts and destroys the clone. The bypass never runs in place on live, prior-live, `.pre-restore`, sideline, or restore-target paths.

An unsanitized raw staging copy is itself a retention channel. Before the first destination byte, the producer acquires the same global maintenance, backup, and source-admission/artifact-namespace locks used by restricted-source admission in the canonical order and holds them through the terminal accepted `SQLITE_DONE` read epoch, the later quiescent source-close attribution cut, independently replayed v3 provisional evidence, and the committed copy-success transition. Under those locks it obtains a fresh `trusted-time-v1` proof, advances the high-water only from its monotonic-derived effective value, runs the fatal live sweep/check, and requires zero due/at-or-past-`cleanup_start_by` pending obligations, zero `artifacts_purging|artifacts_purged|db_root_purged` obligations, and zero retention error/either-direction clock latch. It asks the sealed source descriptor broker to open the configured source parent through the broker's trusted root FD, retain that locked directory authority, and deliver only a no-follow/read-only/CLOEXEC leaf descriptor plus an opaque fixed-leaf capability to the reservation-scoped native object through copy closure; the producer and worker receive no parent dirfd. The source must be a regular file with link count one and its frozen device/inode or platform file ID, owner, and mode, and every broker-relative revalidation must still name that retained inode. Let the trusted effective clock be `T0`, let the same proof's suspend-aware sample be `T0_boottime_ms`, and let `clock_anchor_proof_sha256` bind the complete accepted proof. From the current attested profile it computes under checked addition `copy_finish_by=T0+backup_copy_budget_ms`, `sanitize_finish_by=copy_finish_by+backup_sanitize_budget_ms`, and `raw_destroy_by=sanitize_finish_by+backup_raw_destroy_margin_ms`.

Before creating even an empty destination file, the producer asks the sole reservation supervisor to create `brain-backup-maintenance-reservation-v1` inside its private registry directory on the **same verified volatile mount** as staging. Neither this registry nor any lease, path, basename, nonce, inode, PID, process-group identity, included-root hash, transition request, or destruction evidence may be written to the live database, a persistent filesystem, swap, hibernation image, log, report, ordinary backup, ledger, or detached attestation. The fixed-order reservation record is:

```text
contract,reservation_id,state,phase,copy_transition_state,boot_id_hash,mount_id,mount_device,root_inode,
stage_basename,nonce,source_file_identity_sha256,destination_device,destination_inode,
T0,T0_boottime_ms,clock_anchor_proof_sha256,
copy_finish_by,sanitize_finish_by,raw_destroy_by,retention_budget_profile_hash,
reservation_supervisor_capability_hash,reservation_watchdog_takeover_headroom_ms,reservation_rename_headroom_ms,
included_root_set_hash,copy_vfs_audit_sha256,
copy_freeze_release_receipt_sha256,copy_final_revalidation_sha256,
owner_uid,owner_pid,owner_start_ticks,owner_pgid,owner_pgid_start_ticks,
record_revision,last_transition_id,last_transition_request_sha256,
transition_receipt_count,transition_receipts[transition_receipt_count],
writer_generation,writer_state,writer_role,writer_uid,writer_pid,writer_start_ticks,
writer_pgid,writer_pgid_start_ticks,writer_session_id_hash,writer_cgroup_hash,
writer_command_hash,writer_bound_at,
fence_reason,fence_started_at,fence_started_boottime_ms,
terminal_at,terminal_evidence_sha256,updated_at,record_sha256
```

`contract` is exact `brain-backup-maintenance-reservation-v1`. `reservation_id` and each transition ID are exactly 26 lowercase Crockford-base32 characters under the same alphabet check used for `attestation_id`; `nonce` is exactly 64 lowercase hex. `stage_basename` is byte-exact ASCII `raw-<reservation_id>-<nonce>.sqlite`, the canonical registry basename is `res-<reservation_id>.v1`, and an exclusive successor sibling is `.<reservation_id>.<transition_id>.next`; slash, NUL, alias, alternate case, extra suffix, or another derived name rejects. `state` is exactly `active|fencing|published|destroyed`. While active, `phase` advances monotonically through `reserved_unbound|copying|sanitizing|handoff|finalizing`; `sanitizing -> finalizing` may skip `handoff` only for an empty included-root set. `active -> fencing -> destroyed` is the sole destruction path and fencing is absorbing access denial: it rejects every bind, raw opener, ordinary phase transition, publication, upload, and capability consumption. Fencing and destroyed preserve the phase at which fencing began; published requires and preserves `finalizing`. Only active `finalizing` may become `published`. `copy_transition_state` is exactly `uncommitted|provisional|committed`: it is `uncommitted` through ordinary `copying`, may become `provisional` only while the copy writer remains active after every file/copy/fsync check has passed, and becomes `committed` only in the final copy-success transition to idle `sanitizing`. It never moves backward. Sanitizer or later-role construction requires `committed`; an uncommitted/provisional crash is fence-and-destroy only. Deadline, proof, profile, supervisor capability, both headrooms, source-file identity, boot/mount, nonce, root-set, and bound-destination fields never change. `reservation_watchdog_takeover_headroom_ms` and `reservation_rename_headroom_ms` are positive nonwrapping signed int64 values equal to their distinct profile values; neither may substitute for or absorb the other. `destination_device|destination_inode` are both null only in `reserved_unbound` and both non-null afterward. `copy_vfs_audit_sha256` is null through uncommitted copy, becomes non-null exactly with the provisional edge, is immutable thereafter, and is mandatory for committed/published state; an earlier fence/destroy may retain it null. It is normatively the v3 audit and transitively binds `terminal_copy_snapshot_sha256`; the reservation needs no redundant terminal field, but the supervisor must receive and independently validate the complete v3 audit bytes and terminal preimage before installing that digest. `copy_freeze_release_receipt_sha256|copy_final_revalidation_sha256` are both null in every uncommitted or provisional record, including a fenced one, and become non-null together only in the committed `copy_commit_v1` successor. The first equals the exact ready source-registry release-receipt digest; the second is the §6.12 digest under domain `backup-copy-final-revalidation-v1` over the exact provisional revision/hash, v3 digest, destination identity/absence/fsync facts, exclusive-source capability, canonical-lock proof, cancellation/deadline/two-headroom results. That digest is completed before and becomes an input to the ready release; it does not hash the later release receipt. Both committed fields are immutable through later phases and mandatory in every committed/published record; the fenced release can never populate them.

Each reservation transition receipt has this fixed field order:

```text
transition_id,transition_request_sha256,
predecessor_record_revision,predecessor_record_sha256,
successor_record_revision,successor_core_sha256
```

`successor_core_sha256` is the §6.12 typed digest under domain `brain-backup-maintenance-reservation-successor-core-v1` over the successor's complete fixed-order fields except `transition_receipt_count`, `transition_receipts[...]`, and `record_sha256`. Omitting the receipt collection avoids a self-referential hash while still binding the exact installed semantic successor. The create receipt alone has null predecessor revision/hash; every later receipt has both non-null and equal to the request's expected canonical predecessor. Its successor revision equals the installed record revision, its request digest equals the successor's `last_transition_request_sha256`, and its ID equals `last_transition_id` when it is the final receipt. The initial record has `record_revision=1`, `transition_receipt_count=1`, and exactly the create receipt. Every installed complete-record replacement copies the complete prior receipt prefix byte-for-byte and appends exactly one receipt, so `transition_receipt_count=record_revision`; no receipt can be updated, reordered, removed, or pruned while that detailed record exists. The generated closed state graph supplies a frozen maximum receipt count and encoded-record length; exhaustion or an extra legal-looking edge fences rather than dropping a witness.

`prune_v1` is the sole special terminal replacement. After §6.3.2's complete reconciliation it atomically replaces the detailed canonical basename with a same-boot content-free tombstone whose fixed fields are:

```text
contract,reservation_id,terminal_record_revision,terminal_record_sha256,
prior_transition_receipt_count,prior_transition_receipt_set_sha256,
prune_transition_id,prune_transition_request_sha256,
pruned_boottime_ms,tombstone_sha256
```

The contract is exact `brain-backup-maintenance-reservation-pruned-v1`. `prior_transition_receipt_set_sha256` is the §6.12 digest under domain `brain-backup-maintenance-reservation-transition-receipt-set-v1` over the count and every six-field prior receipt in append order. The prune request CAS names the exact terminal record; its predecessor evidence also binds the canonical source-registry revision/hash and proves that registry is unfrozen and no `postterminal_v1` header names this reservation. A matching installed freeze denies prune even after raw destruction; the detailed destroyed record and its exact fence/destroy receipt prefix remain available until the source-registry recovery actor installs the fenced release. Immediately before the prune sibling rename, the sole supervisor reselects and revalidates that exact source-registry revision/hash and unfrozen/no-reference predicate. `freeze_install_v1` cannot race into the checked interval because its legal predecessor is an active uncommitted copying reservation, never this terminal record. The prune ID/digest and supervisor clock are copied into the tombstone. `tombstone_sha256` uses the same framed-record construction under domain `brain-backup-maintenance-reservation-pruned-record-v1`. The sibling is verified/fsynced and renamed over the canonical basename under the same sole-supervisor rules; the detailed record and staging bytes are then absent, but the tombstone remains until reboot. Retrying the exact prune returns it; the same prune ID with another preimage rejects; every other transition against that reservation returns terminal-pruned without mutation; and `create_v1` may never reuse its reservation ID on the same boot. Thus detailed retirement ends the reservation lifetime without erasing the witness needed for prune response loss or ID nonreuse.

`writer_state` is exactly `idle|active`; fencing is not a writer state. The closed `writer_role` enum is `backup_copy_source_v1|ledger_import_v1|backup_plan_v1|backup_scrub_v1|backup_attestation_v1|immutable_verify_v1|backup_publication_v1`. `backup_publication_v1` is reservation-only and is not a public SQLite constructor role. `backup_copy_destination_v1`, the supervisor, cleanup workers, and a janitor are never reservation writer roles. Idle records have `writer_role|writer_uid|writer_pid|writer_start_ticks|writer_pgid|writer_pgid_start_ticks|writer_session_id_hash|writer_cgroup_hash|writer_command_hash|writer_bound_at` all null. Active records have all ten fields non-null, and the role maps injectively to the attested command hash and legal phase/predecessor. Both identity hashes are lowercase 64-hex digests derived by the supervisor from the kernel-observed session and canonical cgroup-v2 identity; neither is caller-supplied. A process-group label alone is insufficient, and any packaged target without the exact cgroup freeze/no-migration/kill-empty guarantees below is unsupported for this raw-stage capability. A bind copies the exact authenticated peer session/cgroup hashes into the successor. `writer_generation=0` is legal only on the initial idle `reserved_unbound` record; each bind performs `idle,g -> active,g+1`, never wraps or reuses a value, and unbind preserves that positive generation. `record_revision` starts at one and increments exactly once for every installed complete-record replacement, independently of writer generation; signed-int64 exhaustion of either counter installs or resumes fencing and never wraps.

An `active -> fencing` transition freezes its entry phase, copy marker, generation, and writer shape: an idle entry remains idle with null writer fields, while an active entry retains every victim field byte-for-byte until closure evidence exists. `fence_reason` is exactly `writer_failure|writer_exit|copy_deadline|sanitize_deadline|raw_destroy_deadline|clock_untrusted|capability_drift|identity_drift|registry_invariant_failure|operator_abort`; fence fields are null in active/published records and non-null in fencing/destroyed records. `fence_started_at` is the effective-wall `E` and `fence_started_boottime_ms` the `B` from the same checked supervisor sample that authorized the fencing rename; both are immutable. Published terminal rows are idle with null writer/fence fields and non-null terminal clock/evidence. A destroyed successor clears all writer fields only after `terminal_evidence_sha256` binds the complete frozen entry-writer tuple—including session and cgroup—plus exact broker-revocation, kill/reap, both-scan, unlink/parent-fsync, clock-proof, and supervisor-capability facts. Destroyed records preserve the fence facts.

All record clocks and process counters are nonnegative signed int64. The immutable owner fields are kernel-derived from the initial producer peer at creation and never reused as current-writer identity. `writer_bound_at` is the effective-wall sample of the bind transition and remains unchanged while that binding is active/frozen in fencing; it is null when idle. `updated_at` is exactly the effective-wall sample authorizing the installed revision and never decreases. `terminal_at` is null in active/fencing records and equals the terminal transition's `updated_at` in published/destroyed records. Published terminal evidence binds publication inode/hash/HMAC/parent-fsync and the immediately preceding idle finalizing record; destroyed evidence has the closure binding above. An impossible clock/nullness relation rejects the complete record.

Reservation bytes use the §6.12 frame registry, not JSON or native-struct layout. Let `Q` be the concatenation, in the preceding fixed field order, of every field except `record_sha256`: exact enums/IDs are text frames, every named hash is decoded raw32, every integer is a signed-int64 frame, and each nullable field uses the null frame. `transition_receipt_count` is framed exactly once; `transition_receipts[...]` is not itself a frame, and expands immediately into every receipt's six fields in append order. Then:

```text
record_sha256 = SHA-256(
  UTF8("brain-backup-maintenance-reservation-record-v1") || 0x00 ||
  u64be(byte_length(Q)) || Q
)

record_bytes = u64be(byte_length(Q)+32) || Q || raw32(record_sha256)
```

The unsigned length is independently bounded by the frozen record maximum; no padding, delimiter, trailer, alternate field order, unknown enum, duplicate field, or unframed value is valid. `source_file_identity_sha256` is the §6.12 typed digest with domain `ai-brain-file-identity-v1` over the actual-handle adapter's exact ordered fields `adapter_contract,platform_target,filesystem_id,volume_or_device_id,file_id,regular_file_token,link_count,owner_identity,mode_identity`; the target adapter must encode each without truncation or fail unsupported.

Transition requests are equally exact. Their edge enum is `create_v1|copy_bind_v1|writer_bind_v1|writer_unbind_v1|copy_provisional_v1|copy_commit_v1|phase_handoff_v1|phase_finalizing_v1|fence_v1|destroy_v1|publish_v1|prune_v1`; their actor enum is `producer_v1|source_admission_v1|readiness_v1|backup_worker_v1|cleanup_worker_v1|publication_worker_v1|supervisor_timer_v1|supervisor_recovery_v1|operator_control_v1`. Let `U` be §6.12 frames in this fixed order:

```text
request_contract,reservation_id,transition_id,expected_record_revision,expected_record_sha256,
requested_edge,actor_role,writer_role,predecessor_evidence_sha256,
lock_proof_capability_sha256,peer_uid,peer_pid,peer_start_ticks,
peer_pgid,peer_pgid_start_ticks,peer_session_id_hash,peer_cgroup_hash,
peer_command_hash,requested_at_boottime_ms
```

`request_contract` is exact `brain-backup-maintenance-reservation-transition-v1`. The authorization/null-shape matrix is closed:

| Edge | Permitted actor | Required predecessor | Request `writer_role` | `predecessor_evidence_sha256` | `lock_proof_capability_sha256` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `create_v1` | `producer_v1` | no canonical record; exact empty canonical basename and compliant mount | null | exact create/admission/profile/clock evidence | exact transition-subset proof |
| `copy_bind_v1` | `producer_v1` | active `reserved_unbound`, uncommitted, idle generation 0 | `backup_copy_source_v1` | source/destination/broker capability evidence | non-null |
| `copy_provisional_v1` | `producer_v1` | active copying, uncommitted, same active copy generation | `backup_copy_source_v1` | complete independently replayed v3 copy-audit bytes, terminal snapshot preimage, quiescent attribution cuts, and closure evidence | non-null |
| `copy_commit_v1` | `producer_v1` | exact active provisional predecessor | `backup_copy_source_v1` | exact provisional-record/v3-audit/terminal-token evidence plus the immutable ready `freeze_release_v1` transition receipt and final revalidation digest | non-null |
| `writer_bind_v1` | `backup_worker_v1` | legal idle committed phase predecessor | one exact `ledger_import_v1|backup_plan_v1|backup_scrub_v1|backup_attestation_v1|immutable_verify_v1` | exact role-specific entry evidence | non-null |
| `writer_bind_v1` | `publication_worker_v1` | legal idle finalizing predecessor | `backup_publication_v1` | detached/HMAC/publication-input evidence | non-null |
| `writer_unbind_v1` | `backup_worker_v1` | active role owned by that authenticated peer | exact current writer role | exact role-specific successful-exit evidence | non-null |
| `phase_handoff_v1` | `backup_worker_v1` | active `backup_scrub_v1`, sanitizing, nonempty root set | `backup_scrub_v1` | exact scrub/ledger/checkpoint evidence | non-null |
| `phase_finalizing_v1` | `backup_worker_v1` | active scrub in legal sanitizing-empty or handoff-nonempty predecessor | `backup_scrub_v1` | exact branch-specific revalidation evidence | non-null |
| `fence_v1` | one actor/reason pair below | active or fencing-eligible predecessor | null | exact reason/record/kernel/capability evidence | null |
| `destroy_v1` | `cleanup_worker_v1|supervisor_recovery_v1` | exact fencing predecessor | null | exact broker/closure evidence | null |
| `publish_v1` | `publication_worker_v1` | active finalizing publication binding | `backup_publication_v1` | exact atomic-publication/HMAC/parent-fsync evidence | non-null |
| `prune_v1` | `supervisor_recovery_v1` | verified published or destroyed terminal predecessor; exact source registry unfrozen with no matching postterminal reference | null | exact reconciliation/empty-rescan plus source-registry revision/hash/no-reference evidence | null |

For `copy_commit_v1`, `predecessor_evidence_sha256` is specifically the §6.12 digest under domain `backup-copy-commit-predecessor-evidence-v1` over `reservation_id,expected_provisional_record_revision,expected_provisional_record_sha256,writer_generation,copy_vfs_audit_sha256,terminal_copy_snapshot_sha256,copy_freeze_release_receipt_sha256,copy_final_revalidation_sha256,lock_proof_capability_sha256`. The supervisor independently reselects the exact source-registry receipt by its digest and requires the release request to have consumed that same final-revalidation digest before constructing the committed successor. A fenced-release receipt, mismatched digest, later writer event substituted as release evidence, or regenerated final-revalidation value fails before sibling creation.

The fence-reason matrix is also closed:

| Actor | Permitted `fence_reason` |
|---|---|
| `producer_v1` | `writer_failure|writer_exit|copy_deadline|capability_drift|identity_drift` for its own reservation |
| `backup_worker_v1` | `writer_failure|writer_exit|sanitize_deadline|capability_drift|identity_drift` for its own binding |
| `publication_worker_v1` | `writer_failure|writer_exit|sanitize_deadline|capability_drift|identity_drift` for its own binding |
| `source_admission_v1` | `raw_destroy_deadline|clock_untrusted|capability_drift|identity_drift|registry_invariant_failure` |
| `readiness_v1` | `raw_destroy_deadline|clock_untrusted|capability_drift|identity_drift|registry_invariant_failure` |
| `supervisor_timer_v1` | `copy_deadline|sanitize_deadline|raw_destroy_deadline` |
| `supervisor_recovery_v1` | `writer_exit|clock_untrusted|capability_drift|identity_drift|registry_invariant_failure` |
| `operator_control_v1` | `operator_abort` only |
| `cleanup_worker_v1` | none; it may request only `destroy_v1` |

Initial creation alone has null expected revision/hash; every other edge has both non-null. `predecessor_evidence_sha256` is never null. Lock proof is null exactly for `fence_v1|destroy_v1|prune_v1` and non-null otherwise. `fence_v1` has one mutating form only: exact active predecessor to exact fencing successor with the selected immutable reason/evidence. A retry whose transition ID/request digest already appears in the non-prunable receipt prefix—or is the exact prune pair in a pruned tombstone—returns that immutable evidence plus the current canonical record/tombstone without mutation; if a detailed record's receipt is current, that record is also the immediate successor. Every attempt to refence an existing fencing row, alter its reason/evidence, install another fencing revision, or reuse any retained transition ID with another request preimage rejects. The supervisor derives every peer field and `requested_at_boottime_ms` after authenticated receipt; timer/recovery requests contain the supervisor's own kernel identity and capability rather than an invented worker peer. `last_transition_request_sha256=SHA-256(UTF8("brain-backup-maintenance-reservation-transition-v1") || 0x00 || u64be(byte_length(U)) || U)`, and `last_transition_id` is the exact ID framed in that request. Any actor/edge/reason/role/null-shape combination absent from these tables, caller-supplied peer fact, missing transition ID, or reused ID with another preimage rejects before sibling creation.

One independently supervised registry service is the sole process permitted to create, replace, rename, or unlink reservation records. Its registry directory is mode 0700 under a distinct supervisor identity. Workers receive neither a registry-directory FD nor direct read/write/traverse permission and cannot select a registry filename. Source admission, readiness, backup workers, cleanup workers, and publication obtain snapshots and request every create, bind, unbind, phase, fence, terminal, and prune transition only through authenticated local IPC. Each request binds the reservation ID, fresh random transition ID, expected `record_revision`, expected `record_sha256`, requested edge, exact caller role, predecessor evidence, and lock-proof/capability digest. The supervisor derives peer UID, PID/start ticks, PGID/leader-start ticks, session, cgroup, and command identity from the kernel; request-supplied identity is never authority.

Sole-supervisor authority is enforced by a nontransferable kernel OFD lock on the exact supervisor-control inode opened through the private registry-root dirfd. The FD is `CLOEXEC`, is never inherited or sent, and the supervisor never forks. A replacement may enter mutation, source-writer-registry, or cleanup code only after the external manager verifies the old PID/start ticks through pidfd, sends `SIGKILL`, reaps it, proves the OFD lock released, acquires that lock, and reconciles the complete registry. It may never overlap or resume a stopped predecessor. Every transition sibling is created only while the current lease is held. Watchdog kill/reap, lease acquisition, reconciliation, and resulting fencing are included in the destruction margin; an unkillable or unbounded old instance fails target capability.

The supervisor serializes mutations, rejects stale expected revision/hash, constructs the complete successor itself, increments `record_revision`, stores the typed request ID/digest, appends the exact receipt after computing the non-self-referential successor-core digest, and computes the new record hash. Before evaluating stale CAS it first searches the complete receipt prefix by transition ID: an exact digest match follows the idempotent response path, while a different digest rejects. It writes an exclusive mode-0600 sibling in the already-open same registry directory, verifies regular-file type, link count one, mount/device, owner, mode, exact length, and hash, fsyncs that sibling, then revalidates the unchanged canonical predecessor, request authority, capability, both headrooms, and applicable strict deadline. One same-directory atomic rename over the canonical basename is the sole state linearization point. No fallible operation follows it. The pre-rename headroom tests plus enforced sole-supervisor/watchdog bounds establish timeliness; a post-rename sample, reopen, or directory fsync is neither required nor permitted to revoke success. Same-boot tmpfs makes the successor authoritative until reboot, while reboot removes the entire protocol namespace. Direct readers are forbidden and a response reports the immutable transition receipt plus the canonical successor/descendant. A fence event already due at the final pre-rename sample outranks and rejects any ordinary queued transition.

Crash and response-loss behavior is exact. Before sibling fsync or rename, the complete canonical predecessor remains authoritative. A fully fsynced but unrenamed sibling is never auto-promoted and is deleted during supervisor reconciliation. After rename, the complete successor and its newly appended receipt are authoritative even if the supervisor crashes or the response is lost. While the detailed record exists, retrying the same transition ID returns the retained receipt and current canonical successor/descendant without another mutation; the receipt proves the exact earlier request, predecessor, successor revision, and successor-core digest even after later detailed-record transitions. After `prune_v1`, only the tombstone's exact prune ID/preimage has a reproducible idempotent response; every pre-prune transition ID returns `terminal-pruned` without a receipt or mutation, exactly as the tombstone contract above specifies. A competing ID against the same predecessor that lost the CAS has no receipt and remains stale; it can never be mistaken for the winning edge. Fencing/destroyed never replays or revives an earlier state. A complete valid record with a state/namespace disagreement enters normal `registry_invariant_failure` fencing. A malformed canonical record, duplicate canonical name, sequence/revision/receipt gap, receipt-prefix mutation, wrong hash, or unresolved sibling ambiguity authorizes **no** guessed successor: the supervisor latches namespace-wide registry-invariant containment, revokes every raw-stage capability, privately scans/destroys every raw namespace entry without canonical locks, and keeps readiness/admission down until the empty mount is reinitialized under a fresh proof. It never emits per-reservation destroyed evidence from untrusted fields. On supervisor restart every still-active valid writer binding is conservatively fenced. A valid idle committed reservation may resume only after full registry/staging/deadline/receipt reconciliation.

Every opener follows the state machine below. A worker first holds the stated canonical lock subset, obtains a current supervisor snapshot, and sends the exact bind/phase/unbind request with that lock proof; the supervisor itself never acquires or waits for a canonical lock. A clean close unbinds only after the role's required exit facts exist. A long scrub may bind under the transition subset, release it, and separately acquire `global_maintenance -> backup_publication -> retired_ledger` for clone/ledger work, but it must release that entire subset before reacquiring the transition subset for a phase request. The reacquired worker revalidates its checkpoint and, when it needs ledger facts, acquires the full canonical subset with source admission before retired ledger. Any open/transaction/check/close/unbind failure requests fencing and never advances a success phase. The one `backup_scrub_v1` binding remains active through the lock-free handoff.

| Operation / exact writer role | Required entry and predecessor evidence | Required successful exit |
|---|---|---|
| Raw copy / `backup_copy_source_v1` | active `reserved_unbound`, `uncommitted`, idle generation 0, exact source/destination capability | active `sanitizing`, `committed`, same positive generation idle, exact v3 audit, terminal snapshot token, quiescent physical-attribution cuts, and closed copy evidence |
| Optional base synchronization / `ledger_import_v1` | active `sanitizing`, `committed`, idle, no scrub application, clone head behind the frozen plan base head | same phase/marker, idle at the incremented generation, exact contiguous base suffix committed through that frozen head |
| Plan / `backup_plan_v1` | active `sanitizing`, `committed`, idle, exact frozen base head, no scrub application | same phase/marker, idle at the incremented generation, exact immutable application and children committed |
| Scrub with nonempty included-root set / `backup_scrub_v1` | active `sanitizing`, `committed`, idle, exact application present, no attestation | while the same writer remains active: `sanitizing -> handoff` only after scrub checks, ledger replication, and checkpoint; `handoff -> finalizing` only after live purge, canonical reacquisition, and complete revalidation; close to idle only after exact pre-finalization evidence exists |
| Scrub with empty included-root set / `backup_scrub_v1` | active `sanitizing`, `committed`, idle, exact empty application present, no attestation | while the same writer remains active: direct `sanitizing -> finalizing` only after scrub checks; close to idle only after exact pre-finalization evidence exists |
| Final attestation / `backup_attestation_v1` | active `finalizing`, `committed`, idle, exact pre-finalization evidence, no attestation | `finalizing`, `committed`, idle at the incremented generation, exact resulting ledger suffix plus in-database attestation committed and connection closed |
| Immutable verification / `immutable_verify_v1` | active `finalizing`, `committed`, idle, attestation present and every mutable SQLite sidecar absent | `finalizing`, `committed`, idle at the incremented generation, immutable post-finalization and detached/HMAC evidence complete |
| Publication / `backup_publication_v1` | active `finalizing`, `committed`, idle, complete detached/HMAC evidence and unchanged publication locks/deadlines | terminal `published`, `committed`, idle at the incremented generation only after atomic publication and publication-parent fsync |

A nonempty scrub may not skip `handoff`; an empty scrub may not enter it. A wrong/missing/null role, role/command mismatch, phase, marker, predecessor, revision, generation, writer, suffix, duplicate/reordered opener, destination/supervisor/janitor-role injection, or premature publication fails before a database/file mutation. A later raw-stage role may bind only after the preceding generation is durably idle or the reservation has entered fencing and the prior writer is reaped with zero descriptors.

The initial complete reservation is installed before destination creation. The sole supervisor retains the only long-lived staging-root management dirfd. The dedicated descriptor broker—not the producer—consumes one short-lived, nonexportable, fixed-leaf directory capability derived from that root, creates the destination with `O_RDWR|O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC` and mode 0600, and requires a regular file with link count one. It issues only the sealed preopened destination descriptor/capability to the reservation-scoped native worker; no worker receives a traversable staging path or parent dirfd, and journal operations are fixed-leaf broker calls from the private destination VFS. After authenticated delivery acknowledgement the broker closes its leaf-FD duplicate and the short-lived directory capability; each later fixed-leaf call likewise closes every broker-side leaf/directory duplicate before replying. The broker never holds a staging-root, database, WAL, SHM, journal, or temp FD across IPC requests or through a scan. The worker retains its exact owned destination FD through copy closure. The producer then asks the supervisor to bind `backup_copy_source_v1`, add the observed device/inode, and advance to `copying`/`uncommitted` before the first destination byte. The producer may invoke only the §6.12.1 native `backupToVolatileClone` operation, and only when `raw_destroy_by` is strictly less than every included root's earliest cleanup start. Terminal accepted read-epoch closure, the later quiescent physical-attribution cut, semantic replay, file/directory fsync, the active provisional record, and the final committed `copying -> sanitizing` transition must all satisfy the headroom-aware copy deadline below; an ordinary failure requests synchronous fencing/destruction and the supervisor fences a crash-left active writer. This replaces the contradictory fixed 240-second assumption and accounts for the online-backup interval itself.

The reservation remains active after terminal accepted read-epoch closure, after the distinct quiescent physical-attribution cut, and throughout the lock-free live-purge handoff. Under `global_maintenance -> source_admission_artifact_namespace`, each source admission asks the supervisor for one fully reconciled, typed-hash-bound registry/staging snapshot and validates it before opening its writer transaction; it never reads the registry directly. An orphan stage, malformed/duplicate record or tombstone, active or fencing reservation at/past `raw_destroy_by`, registry/mount mismatch, supervisor/reconciliation failure, or unreadable namespace denies admission and readiness and triggers priority fencing where applicable. Otherwise the new root's `retention_cleanup_start_by` must be strictly greater than the maximum `raw_destroy_by` of every active or fencing reservation; equality fails. A verified published/destroyed detailed terminal record or valid pruned tombstone imposes no scheduling bound. Source admission never acquires `backup_publication` and never waits for the backup worker: it either rejects or proves the later cleanup start. The held source-admission lock prevents a new restricted root or earlier reservation from entering the original snapshot comparison; the supervisor-owned same-boot reservation/tombstone preserves that fence after the locks release.

Raw staging and its reservation registry occupy one dedicated canonical tmpfs mount; checking only a directory name or `/run` prefix is insufficient. Before any reservation or destination is created, and continuously while one exists, the capability proves the exact mount ID/device/root inode, `FSTYPE=tmpfs`, no bind or persistent backing, mode/owner, zero enabled swap devices, disabled hibernation, disabled core dumps for every raw-stage process, and `SQLITE_TMPDIR|TMPDIR` confined to that mount. Its checked capacity floor is the sum of the maximum simultaneous raw-stage allocation, each of the exact `SOURCE_WRITER_REGISTRY_MAX_SOURCE_COUNT_V1` simultaneously supported source registries' independently generated terminal-closure byte/inode envelopes, both canonical-and-sibling transition files, and fixed supervisor metadata; the supervisor/stage-broker admission ledger reserves those terms so one class cannot consume another's terminal headroom. A new source identity beyond that first-layer-attested count is denied before registry initialization or writable descriptor delivery until a new boot rather than weakening the floor. It also proves that the supervisor has a distinct UID where supported, session, cgroup, and PGID; is outside every writer group; receives no database/stage-file FD; runs no worker code; and shares no worker mutex.

The staging namespace is mode 0700 and owned by a dedicated descriptor-broker identity, not by an application, backup/publication worker, or shared raw-stage UID. Workers have no mount-namespace path, parent dirfd, traverse permission, `/proc/.../fd` reopen authority, `pidfd_getfd` authority, or unmediated `open|openat|openat2` route to staging. The broker issues an exact preopened descriptor only after consuming a supervisor capability bound to reservation, generation, role, inode, flags, recipient pidfd/start/session/cgroup, and one nonexportable issuance nonce. Kernel LSM/sandbox policy labels every raw-stage descriptor with its reservation and permits inheritance, duplication, and `SCM_RIGHTS` transfer only within that reservation's tracked cgroup. Process creation, cgroup migration, `pidfd_getfd`, `/proc` FD reopening, and transfer outside the cgroup are denied. Every recipient is registered by pidfd before delivery. Inability to enforce this continuously is a pre-creation capability failure. Mount, broker, sandbox, or identity drift denies creation, publication, admission, and readiness and requests fencing before further bytes are written.

The record's same-boot deadline conversion is independent of SQLite and canonical locks. The supervisor verifies `boot_id_hash` and `clock_anchor_proof_sha256`, samples `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` as `B`, and computes checked `E=MAX(T0,T0+(B-T0_boottime_ms))`; a boot mismatch, negative delta, overflow, missing proof, or trusted-clock latch requests immediate `clock_untrusted` fencing. Let `W=reservation_watchdog_takeover_headroom_ms`, `R=reservation_rename_headroom_ms`, `C=max_raw_destroy_physical_closure_ms`, and `Q=raw_destroy_composed_bound_ms=2*W+C+2*R` from the exact immutable profile selected by the record's `retention_budget_profile_hash`; missing lookup, hash mismatch, or disagreement between the record's W/R values and that profile is capability drift and cannot enter a mutation. Before entering a deadline-governed mutation, an ordinary success transition is admitted only when checked `E+W+R < D`. A destruction-start `fence_v1` request instead requires checked `E+Q <= raw_destroy_by`; after authoritative fencing and physical closure, `destroy_v1` requires checked `E+W+R <= raw_destroy_by`. Each admitted mutation independently causes the external manager to arm one absolute `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` **takeover-completion** guard at its own `B+W`. That instant is a completion deadline, never the time at which killing first begins: progress deadlines cause early intervention so that, unless the expected successor is already installed, successor-missing detection, old-supervisor kill/reap, OFD acquisition, canonical reconciliation, successor reconstruction/write/fsync/revalidation, and replacement readiness at the final deadline sample have all completed no later than that mutation's `B+W`. The old instance cannot resume. No one mutation recursively starts a second `W`; the separately admitted fence and destroy mutations deliberately reserve two independent `W` intervals through `Q`.

Every actual mutator—original or replacement—then samples effective time immediately before its rename syscall. An ordinary success transition requires checked `E+R < D`; `fence_v1` requires checked `E+R+C+W+R <= raw_destroy_by`; and closure-complete `destroy_v1` requires checked `E+R <= raw_destroy_by`. The mutator must complete its rename inside `R`. A replacement additionally verifies the original authenticated watchdog-admission evidence and its unexpired mutation-specific `B+W` completion bound before using the remaining `R`. A nonpaused original normally installs the successor before `B+W`; a paused original leaves enough separately reserved time for bounded takeover and bounded rename. After the authoritative fence rename, physical closure must finish inside `C`, and the complete evidence must reach `destroy_v1` admission while its separate `W+R` still fits. Failure to complete either takeover by its own `B+W`, physical closure inside `C`, any checked remaining-sequence sum, or either rename inside `R` is a supported-target capability failure and cannot produce success. Thus no component borrows another's reserve, and both renames—not merely earlier samples—are inside the composed destruction bound.

Fence events outrank ordinary IPC work. `fence_v1` is never withheld merely because an ordinary success deadline has arrived; it uses the separate checked `Q` reservation against `raw_destroy_by`, installs denial under the watchdog-bounded serializer, records its exact sample, and begins closure. Equality loses for copy, sanitize, finalization, and publication. Every bind, phase, unbind, and publication request after committed copy, including terminal `published`, reserves `W+R` against `sanitize_finish_by`; copy success reserves `W+R` against `copy_finish_by`, and every actual rename repeats its applicable final predicate. A still-nonterminal raw stage at its ordinary deadline cannot succeed. `destroy_v1` additionally requires the already-complete broker-revocation/kill/reap/scan/unlink/parent-fsync evidence; neither headroom nor `C` can excuse late physical deletion. `sanitize_finish_by` is the destruction-start deadline, and the derived margin covers the independently takeover-bounded `fence_v1`, measured physical closure, and independently takeover-bounded `destroy_v1`. Reaching `raw_destroy_by` without physical absence and the authoritative destroyed rename is a supported-target capability failure but never relaxes fencing or fabricates destroyed evidence. No deadline, measurement, or headroom can extend or recompute from a faster/later profile.

Deadline/failure fencing is a supervisor-private control-plane transition that acquires no canonical retention, publication, admission, artifact, ledger, SQLite, or worker mutex. The supervisor first installs `state=fencing`, thereby revoking every outstanding unconsumed raw-stage capability and prohibiting every new bind, phase, publication, or upload. It then atomically revokes the reservation at the descriptor broker. A successful revocation acknowledgement proves every in-flight broker request is cancelled/returned, every broker-side staging leaf/directory FD is closed, and no future issue, open, duplication into a new process, cgroup admission, or FD transfer can occur. The supervisor freezes the reservation cgroup, verifies the registered pidfd set and cgroup membership are complete, kills/reaps every member—not only the current writer—and proves the cgroup empty. Its own PID/PGID/session/cgroup and the descriptor broker are disjoint from that set; an idle reservation invents no writer but still performs broker revocation and empty-cgroup proof. The denial remains permanent through terminal pruning. Kill/reap, enumeration, and cleanup execute outside the bounded mutation serializer and report typed evidence for the terminal transition; the serializer never waits for a victim or worker mutex.

Only after broker revocation and empty-cgroup proof does the cleanup lane scan all processes.
