Because no process can gain path authority or a labeled descriptor and no reservation task can be born, the zero-opener fact is stable rather than observational. The lane uses only the supervisor's private retained staging-root management dirfd and acquires **zero** canonical locks before `destroyed`. It enumerates every `/proc/[0-9]*/fd` entry and identifies holders by both `fstat` device/inode and canonical staging-prefix target, including ` (deleted)` descriptors, directory FDs, database/WAL/SHM/journal/temp/sidecar entries, inherited/duplicated/SCM_RIGHTS FDs, and PID/PGID reuse. Any descriptor outside the tracked cgroup is a capability breach and forbids a terminal claim. The pre-scan may exclude only the exact attested root-management dirfd plus explicitly enumerated cleanup FDs opened by that scan. After a zero pre-scan it revalidates type/device/inode/link-count-one, unlinks every reservation leaf fd-relatively, fsyncs the staging parent, closes every leaf/short-lived cleanup FD, and performs the complete zero post-scan; the post-scan excludes only the one management root dirfd. Broker revocation remains in force after the scan and terminal rename. An unrelated process may hold any canonical lock indefinitely without delaying this lane. Content-free live-database/ledger/handoff reconciliation and terminal pruning occur later under ordinary canonical order and are not part of `raw_destroy_by`.

Path disappearance is never destruction evidence. Unavailable/incomplete procfs, failed broker revocation, incomplete pidfd/cgroup membership, a possible new opener, an unauthenticated or unkillable holder, a zombie-owning/inherited descriptor, remaining deleted inode, hardlink, supervisor/broker identity overlap, or either nonzero scan leaves the record in fencing and can never emit `destroyed`, readiness, admission, publication, or upload evidence. The hard `raw_destroy_by` guarantee is supported only where every raw-stage task is killable userspace, broker/LSM/cgroup exclusion is continuous, and the external manager can replace a failed or stopped supervisor within the frozen margin. An indefinitely uninterruptible task, incomplete holder enumeration, unbounded reap, watchdog failure, or opener-control gap fails capability before destination creation. The margin includes supervisor detection/restart, OFD-lease transfer, broker revocation, cgroup freeze/kill/reap, both scans, unlink, and parent fsync.

A producer-process crash or SIGKILL does not remove tmpfs, so the canonical reservation remains visible to the independently managed supervisor in the same boot. Suspend does not clear it and `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` deadlines continue to advance. On kernel panic, power loss, or reboot, both raw bytes and the detailed reservation disappear with the tmpfs instance. Absence after a boot change is not accepted merely as a missing lease: before any listener, backup, source admission, or readiness, the supervisor obtains the current-boot trusted-time proof, verifies a newly mounted compliant tmpfs instance, opens the complete root by directory FD, and proves registry/staging namespaces empty. A missing mount, persistent fallback directory, enabled swap, hibernation capability, stale bind mount, nonempty unleased namespace, or inability to prove a fresh current-boot mount keeps readiness and source admission down. No persistent recovery marker is required because no raw surface may survive this proven power-loss boundary; content-free published backup attestations remain publication evidence, while an aborted raw attempt leaves no persistent per-attempt metadata.

`published` requires the exact final sanitized database, detached HMAC, atomic publication, publication-parent fsync, and the legal publication-role terminal transition. `destroyed` requires the complete closure proof above. A terminal reservation may be pruned only after exact content-free canonical reconciliation, a fresh registry/staging/descriptor rescan proves no matching stage, raw/partial path, sidecar, temp inode, or open descriptor, and the exact canonical source registry is unfrozen with no postterminal reference to that reservation. Raw destruction never waits for this content-free release/prune interlock: a crash may leave a detailed destroyed record plus an installed source freeze, and recovery releases that freeze from the retained exact fence/destroy lineage before pruning. Only the supervisor may replace the detailed record with the exact same-boot pruned tombstone above. A crash before that rename leaves the detailed terminal record; a crash after it but before response leaves the verified tombstone. Power loss removes either volatile form. The raw copy can never enter a persistent publication directory or upload stream. Fault, power loss, SIGTERM, SIGKILL, a slow native `backupToVolatileClone`, scrub/VACUUM, hardlink/copy, uploader, writer-response loss, supervisor restart, and handoff barriers prove the raw sentinel disappears by its derived bound and before cleanup start on every supported target. If insufficient margin remains, the producer performs/awaits ordinary live purge first or creates no backup. Only the closed post-VACUUM database plus exact fsynced attestation may be atomically published or uploaded; remote retention cannot substitute because restricted roots are absent before upload.

Restore runs in offline quarantine with listeners, schedulers, workers, timers, boot backup, and readiness disabled. It has two closed evidence modes. `normal_restore_v1` applies when a local data root exists: before trusting a candidate it obtains the latest contiguous HMAC-valid `youtube-retired-item-ledger-v1` head from both the local protected ledger and its independently authenticated recovery replica and rejects disagreement, fork, or a head behind the current/prior-live database. It ordinarily verifies the candidate's historical backup key through both local and recovery roles. A historical local verification slot may be absent only when the recovery control plane's complete append-only provisioning lineage proves that exact version predates a completed declared-root-loss operation, binds the lost-root input evidence and successor key, and shows no later fork; then the recovery verify-only slot may verify that historical **input** alone while the current local/recovery active pair remains exact. The historical key is never reinstalled as a signer. Every transformed output uses the current active local key and its exact receipt.

`declared_data_root_loss_restore_v1` applies only when the local root, signer key, and local ledger are attested absent. It requires the independent recovery store's authenticated append-only monotonic head manifest, complete contiguous HMAC-valid ledger replica, matching historical backup-verification slot, and permission-restricted operator artifact; it never invents a requirement for the lost local copies. The historical recovery slot verifies only the input. Before any transformed-candidate plan row, the restore must complete §3.3 `declared_root_loss_signer_reprovision_v1`, append/fsync its recovery-control-plane receipt, activate the new local signer, and bind that fresh key/receipt to the output plan. If both local and recovery evidence are present at entry, the operation is normal mode and disagreement still blocks.

The **input backup and sidecars** must already be exact S26/S27 under the existing NotebookLM latch or carry a verified v2 sanitized-S28 in-database row plus detached attestation; an unsanitized/historical S28 input is rejected until a separately authorized offline source-sanitization operation replaces it. Restore independently hashes the closed effective main, rejects any SQLite/WAL/SHM/journal/temp input sidecar, requires exactly the one detached v2 attestation when applicable, resolves `backup_key_id_hash` and `backup_key_provisioning_receipt_sha256` against the independent recovery history, verifies the HMAC, requires the exact in-database row to equal the detached prefix, and reconstructs `pre_finalization_check_evidence_sha256` from the persisted historical pre-finalization fields. It does not compare that historical file or scan hash to the now-final file. Under the attested runtime and descriptor it independently reruns final integrity/quick/FK/page/freelist/head checks and the post-finalization scan against the supplied closed database, matching only the detached `database_sha256` and `post_finalization_*` facts; pre/post phase substitution, either nonzero violation count, or a missing/extra/reordered surface rejects.

Restore then reclassifies schema, recomputes the retained retired-ID set, and zero-scans restricted roots, nonterminal obligations, unbound applications/receipts, cache, client-contract content, and content sentinels rather than trusting either historical count. Retained complete histories must pass their exact tombstone/root-seal/ledger/receipt graph. On its fresh volatile candidate, before any target publication, it deletes all upload grants, deletes all capture intents, proves both mutable-authority surfaces zero, and rotates `youtube_browser_authority_epoch` from the candidate's exact epoch to the operation-journaled fresh `restore_invalidate_v1` epoch; permanent inspect issuances and immutable receipts remain byte-identical. A data-root-loss fixture restores from only the sanitized database, detached attestation, independent historical verifier slot, independent retired-ledger replica/head manifest, operator artifact, and newly completed provisioning receipt/current signer pair; missing/wrong lineage, receipt, key, or ledger evidence fails closed.

The candidate never becomes the target merely by passing its own older attestation. Restore clones it into a fresh volatile inode, applies any required S26/S27-to-S28 migration under the prepositioned containment package, and computes the monotonic union of (a) the latest external ledger and (b) every independently verified prior-live/current-target retired proof already corroborated by that ledger. The candidate's retired rows are untrusted evidence inputs, not an independent union source: each may preserve its stronger exact root-seal/backup-attestation proof only after its complete retirement tuple is found in an HMAC-valid external entry; otherwise restore fails with `candidate_retired_tuple_unproven` and neither appends nor imports that candidate-only item ID. This prevents a validly HMAC-attested but stale/forged candidate database from poisoning the authoritative retired-ID namespace. Before displacement, a prior-live/current target may append a locally proven not-yet-mirrored retirement only through the separate original-surface procedure below; that privilege never applies to an input backup candidate.

Every database retired tuple must therefore occur as an exact verified ledger entry. The ledger may contain later corroborating entries for one item; the distinct item-ID set is authoritative, and the earliest valid sequence supplies the canonical tuple when a database has no stronger ledger-corroborated local root-seal/backup-attestation row. For each ledger item ID that exists as an item root in the older candidate, the offline scrubber computes copy survivor actions, deletes vectors first, removes that resurrected root/content/cache/client graph, and installs the earliest ledger-bound `external_ledger_import` tombstone; it never exposes or reuses the root. For each distinct ledger ID absent from both the candidate item and retired tables, it inserts that same canonical import; an existing exact local live/backup proof is preserved only when its tuple also occurs in the ledger. S26/S27 candidates with a nonempty ledger are therefore upgraded and union-scrubbed, not published as a schema that cannot enforce retirement. The resulting database records the exact latest ledger-state head and all ledger-entry projections, proves its retired item-ID set equals the ledger's distinct item-ID set, is freshly v2-scrub-attested, and only then is eligible for publication. A restored database may contain a rootless schedule reservation only when it matches a retired row; the reciprocal is not required for a ledger-imported historical tombstone.

Before any S28 prior-live, `.pre-restore`, sideline, target, or other original is displaced, restore takes the exclusive artifact lock, verifies/appends any valid tombstone not yet mirrored to the external ledger, and computes the union of exact restricted artifact manifests while both path columns still exist, then performs the fd-safe deletion proof. Each original database/sidecar set has only two allowed outcomes: securely destroy and fsync it after classification/evidence, or clone it into a new volatile staging inode, run the guard-bypass scrub only on that clone, publish the sanitized/attested/ledger-unioned clone, then securely destroy/fsync the original. No original is scrubbed in place and no unsanitized original is retained as a resurrection channel. A missing database needed to classify an orphan artifact path, invalid manifest, or path outside reviewed roots blocks activation. Pre-026, partial, and unknown schemas fail. Fresh trusted-time proofs immediately before candidate publication and activation own equality/crossing containment; either clock-direction latch restarts the gate rather than exposing the database. Fault barriers before/after every ledger append, union row, resurrected-root scrub, database rename, and original destruction prove that an older backup can never regress the retired set.

Migration 028 cannot begin until an S27/S28-aware standalone containment package is already installed outside the replaceable application binary. Under the global migration lock, the runner verifies the exact v2 scrubber, budget profile, schema/guard registry, SQLite/sqlite-vec/scalar hashes, artifact locks, clock-high-water service, synchronous retention unit, and separately supervised one-minute fallback unit; both units must execute a signed S27 dry probe and arm their S28 descriptor **before** DDL. The runner writes/validates a content-free capability latch whose hash is part of the migration ledger authority. A kill, crash, or failure after any S28 DDL leaves the ordinary app/readiness down while these prepositioned units recognize S28 and continue containment; rollback never starts B27 against S28 and never removes/stops the units.

Application startup then runs the synchronous fatal retention sweep immediately after migrations and before any other scheduler, claimant import, backup, listener, or health/readiness success. One shared activation predicate obtains a current-boot trusted proof, advances the high-water only from effective time, and blocks any pending obligation at/after its stored cleanup start, any `artifacts_purging|artifacts_purged|db_root_purged` phase, any retention error/either-direction clock latch, a non-mirrored retired-ledger head, or a missing attested profile. A pre-start pending obligation is allowed only when both retention units, exact registry/capability, artifact/metadata locks, trusted-time proof, retired ledger, and timers are execution-proven. Activation, switch, deployment, and automatic restoration may stop an ordinary unit only under a bounded maintenance lock whose worst-case duration remains strictly before every stored cleanup start; the independently supervised fallback never stops. They run the sweep, require the shared predicate immediately before restart, restart and execution-prove both units before releasing the lock. B27 against S28 and any runtime lacking v2 fail closed. Migration 028 is never added to the audited-additive rollback allowlist; merge resolution must preserve Stage 1 D-019 runtime-capability/opaque-reservation checks alongside PR #55's NotebookLM-027 compatibility support. Production/live application remains denied by §1 even after these local contracts pass.

### 6.4 Rebuilt `transcript_segments`

Preserve every row and field. Replace independent ownership with a composite FK:

- `(transcript_source_id,item_id)` → `transcript_sources(id,item_id)` using the exact action in §6.11;
- retain `UNIQUE(transcript_source_id,idx)` and time-order checks;
- add no independent path that permits the segment item to differ from the source item.

For every post-028 segment and every active legacy row accepted by preflight, time fields have no independent duration degree of freedom: `paragraph_only` requires `start_ms=duration_ms=end_ms=NULL`; otherwise `start_ms` is non-null and, when `end_ms` is null, `duration_ms` is also null, while a non-null end requires `end_ms >= start_ms` and `duration_ms=end_ms-start_ms`. The named guard rejects every other combination. Therefore the governing `transcript-integrity-v1` tuple intentionally hashes `(idx,start_ms,end_ms|null,text)` without omitting mutable information; duration is deterministically recoverable, and all segment fields become immutable after insert.

Browser inserts require contiguous indexes starting at zero, exact `segment_count`, per-segment text hash, and server-recomputed source hash/normalization proof. Every browser cue has a safe-integer `start_ms` in `0..86400000`, starts are nondecreasing in stored order, normalized text is nonempty and at most 2,000 Unicode scalar values, and both `duration_ms` and `end_ms` are null. The client cannot submit either derived time. Named segment-insert and terminal-receipt guards enforce the row shape, contiguous/count proof, and exact source aggregate before commit. Repeated text, equal starts, and repeated equal cues remain separate ordered rows; storage never sorts, truncates, or globally deduplicates them.

Every segment column is immutable and every direct update/delete while its item exists aborts. A named `BEFORE INSERT` guard also rejects a segment whenever an exact manual evidence seal already exists for its source/item; all manual segments must therefore precede the terminal seal, and neither a sealed zero-segment paste nor a sealed segmented source can acquire an extra row later. Item-root cascade deletion remains allowed. Source-only tombstoning is not a retention substitute because the canonical transcript also lives in `items.body` and derived rows.

#### 6.4.1 `transcript_manual_source_evidence_seals`

The four post-028 non-browser manual writers require a physical terminal seal because the immediate segment-to-source FK necessarily inserts the source before its segments. A service convention or job transition cannot prove that a source-first transaction actually completed when the job is absent or already done. Create an immutable seal table with this exact physical column order:

```text
seal_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL, exactly the nonempty bounded transcript source ID
transcript_source_id TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE
body_transition_id TEXT NOT NULL
item_id TEXT NOT NULL
accepted_content_revision INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK > 0
policy_decision_id TEXT NOT NULL
source_kind TEXT NOT NULL, exact user_paste|uploaded_file|youtube_official_caption|owned_media_stt
renderer_version TEXT NOT NULL, exact transcript-source-body-v1
body_sha256 TEXT NOT NULL, lowercase 64-hex
text_sha256 TEXT NOT NULL, lowercase 64-hex
integrity_sha256 TEXT NOT NULL, lowercase 64-hex
segment_count INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
sealed_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
```

The body transition is inserted first with its preselected source/seal ID and exact reset counts. The transaction then supersedes the exact current unheld source when one exists, performs the complete §6.1.2 reset and one body/revision/generation update, inserts the new active source with `manual_evidence_seal_id=id` at that stored revision, inserts all required segments, performs the exact source-backed job resolution when applicable, and finally inserts the seal as the terminal evidence/reset write. A deferred composite source-to-seal FK requires `(manual_evidence_seal_id,id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,policy_decision_id,source_kind,text_sha256,integrity_sha256,segment_count)` to match the seal's unique exact tuple at commit. Add `UNIQUE(seal_id,body_transition_id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,body_sha256)` for the body-transition reverse FK. The seal has the inverse source-evidence FK, exact body-transition FK, and item-root FK in §6.11. Thus an active post-028 manual source cannot commit without its exact seal, transition, reset, and optional exact job disposition, while copied legacy, browser, and recovery sources remain governed by their distinct legacy or terminal-receipt contracts.

A named seal-insert trigger runs after all child inserts and validates only stored database facts under the writer lock. Every seal field is non-null before any equality/FK test, so SQLite `MATCH SIMPLE` cannot turn a partial-null child tuple into authority; IDs are nonempty/bounded, integers use their declared storage class and bounds, and hashes pass exact lowercase-64-hex checks. The trigger requires `seal_id=transcript_source_id=source.id=source.manual_evidence_seal_id`, the exact `manual_source` body transition and its selected seal ID/item/applied revision/body hash/clock, the same current item/revision, active source, exact allowed policy with `blocked_reason IS NULL`, exact kind/provenance/retention shape, `sealed_at=source.created_at=transition.created_at` at the sampled transaction time, and `body_sha256=text_sha256=source.text_sha256=transition.body_sha256=sha256_utf8(items.body)`. For `user_paste`, it requires zero segments and recomputes the zero-segment integrity value. For the three segmented kinds, it requires a positive exact count, contiguous ascending indexes, every `text_sha256=sha256_utf8(text)`, the exact time/duration shape, the two-LF ordered text assembly byte-equal to `items.body`, and exact `transcript_integrity_v1_json` equality. Missing/extra/reordered segments, wrong separator/body/hash/integrity, cross-item substitution, early seal insertion, or a seal after source/transition drift aborts the complete transaction.

Seal columns are immutable. Its `BEFORE INSERT` guard aborts when either `seal_id` or `transcript_source_id` already exists before any conflict algorithm runs, so `INSERT OR REPLACE` cannot remove/rewrite a seal or cascade-delete its graph. Direct seal deletion is denied while the item exists; item-root cascade removes source, segments, and seal coherently. A seal does not authorize processing by itself: source status/current revision, body hold, and queue authority are still rechecked. Before the seal exists, the source-resolution job guard must join the fresh transition/application, its absent preselected seal ID, the complete source/segment tuple, and the transition clock; the terminal seal inserted next revalidates the final job and closes the deferred cycle. Thus a fabricated job-only completion cannot rely on transaction time or revision alone.

### 6.5 Rebuilt queue and attempt tables

Rebuild `transcript_jobs`, `enrichment_jobs`, and `embedding_jobs`, preserving IDs, states, attempts, timestamps, errors, and uniqueness. Each gains:

| Column | Contract |
|---|---|
| `expected_content_revision` | `INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(expected_content_revision > 0)` |
| `claim_token_hash` | nullable lowercase 64-hex |
| `lease_expires_at` | nullable nonnegative epoch |

`enrichment_jobs` additionally gains `generation INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(generation > 0)`. `embedding_jobs` additionally gains nullable `expected_enrichment_generation`; it is required and positive for every post-028 enqueue, while preserved legacy terminal rows may remain null and are ineligible for the exact current-generation contract.

Every post-028 transcript, enrichment, and embedding claim uses the same frozen constant `schema_028_claim_lease_ms=600000`. A claim requires exact SQLite `INTEGER` storage for `claimed_at` and `lease_expires_at`, `0 <= claimed_at <= 9223372036854175807`, and writes `lease_expires_at-claimed_at=600000` under checked signed-int64 arithmetic. No shorter, longer, clipped, caller-supplied, profile-derived, or renewable lease is valid. A transcript claim additionally requires `lease_expires_at <= policy_expires_at <= retention_cleanup_start_by < source_delete_by`. An enrichment or embedding claim, when any retained same-item cleanup start exists, requires `lease_expires_at <= MIN(retained retention_cleanup_start_by)`. Insufficient remaining runway makes the row ineligible and never creates a shortened lease. Result ownership remains exactly `now < lease_expires_at`; equality and later time belong exclusively to the stale sweep. A post-claim policy, cleanup, or capability change follows the existing finalizer precedence and never rewrites the frozen lease.

`transcript_jobs` additionally gains nullable `policy_decision_id`. Copied rows retain null; no policy is inferred during migration. Every post-028 reauthorization/claim/dispatch requires a current immutable policy decision for the same item with `blocked_reason IS NULL` **and** that item's exact `no_durable_client_copy_v1` row/hash. Under the same writer transaction but before claim, a dedicated `pending|retryable_error -> same-state` reauthorization CAS may fill/change that policy and current expected revision, set a copied legacy null schedule to `now`, and clear `last_attempt_id` only when its immutable attempt has null or different policy; it preserves state, attempts, ceiling, a non-null existing schedule, prior provider/error display fields, creation/completion shape, and every identity field. No other pending reauthorization mutation is allowed. The subsequent claim requires that policy/revision and v1 client contract already be exact, `next_run_at IS NOT NULL AND next_run_at <= now`, the global provider cooldown is null or `now >= cooldown_until`, and every candidate exclusion still passes. The only permitted transition into `running` is that atomic `pending|retryable_error -> running` claim: it increments `attempts` exactly once, stamps `claimed_at=updated_at=now`, clears `next_run_at`, writes fresh `claim_token_hash` and finite `lease_expires_at`, and freezes the exact current policy, content revision, item/source platform/video identity, priority, retry ceiling, now-compatible prior attempt/provider/error pointers, creation time, and completion-null shape. A nonterminal job whose item is `legacy_unproven` is inert and cannot enter this reauthorization edge; v1 jobs remain inert until the explicit reauthorization/rearm succeeds.

Every `running -> running` update is forbidden, even when it appears to preserve authority or merely extends a lease. After claim, **every column** of the issued snapshot—including `attempts`, `max_attempts`, `claimed_at`, `updated_at`, token hash, lease, policy, expected revision, item/platform/video identity, priority, schedule-nullness, completion-nullness, last-attempt/provider/error fields, and creation time—remains byte-identical until one exact source-resolution, user-ignore, stale-sweep, pre-dispatch authority disposition, retry, manual, or success transition exits `running`. Dispatch itself never contacts the provider until one immediate no-contact gate revalidates that complete snapshot, database time, policy/deadline/runtime/item/generic-receipt predicates, and cooldown. A failed gate exits through the exact sealed transition below rather than leaving the row running. Terminal/retry transitions may change only the columns their closed row shape enumerates; a no-contact or throttle finalization may extend `max_attempts` only while exiting `running`, never while the claim remains live.

Add `UNIQUE(item_id,generation,expected_content_revision)` on enrichment jobs for exact lookup. No historical revision or generation field references mutable `items.content_revision` or the mutable singleton enrichment-job generation through an FK: those references would become invalid as the item/job advances. Named enqueue/claim/apply triggers and service CAS instead require current item revision and exact current enrichment generation at each authority transition. Legacy nullable embedding generation rows remain outside the post-028 current-generation contract until explicitly rearmed.

The rebuilt enrichment singleton does not rely on a delete trigger to survive conflict algorithms. Its named `BEFORE INSERT` pre-conflict guard aborts if the supplied primary-key ID already exists, if any row already has `NEW.item_id`, or if the complete `(item_id,generation,expected_content_revision)` lookup tuple already exists, before SQLite resolves ordinary uniqueness, UPSERT, `INSERT OR REPLACE`, or `recursive_triggers`. The corresponding `BEFORE UPDATE` collision guard excludes `OLD.id` and repeats the complete key set before any permitted non-held generation/revision edge; the primary ID and item identity themselves are immutable. Enqueue and common-reset code select-and-exact-reconcile the singleton, use an ordinary insert only for proven absence, and use a guarded ordinary update that preserves its ID otherwise. Adding a later unique index to `enrichment_jobs` is forbidden unless this guard and the schema manifest add its collision predicate in the same reviewed migration.

Held-generation denial is physical in 028. A named `BEFORE UPDATE` guard on `enrichment_jobs` rejects **every actual column change** to the exact row selected by a live hold's `(item_id,held_enrichment_generation,expected_content_revision)` while its parent item exists; a named delete guard rejects direct deletion of that job and permits only the tested item-root cascade. Independent named guards reject any change to the held item's `enrichment_state,summary,quotes,category,enriched_at,batch_id,current_applied_enrichment_generation`, any `embedding_jobs` insert/update for that item, and any enrichment batch reservation/submission/result application while the hold exists. The only state allowed for the exact held singleton is the complete pending/unclaimed shape created by §6.1.2; `running|batched|done|error`, attempts, claim/batch fields, completion, result, and applied-generation changes all abort even if a caller omits the hold check. The synchronous worker claimant, batch planner/submitter/poller, direct enrich route, enrichment result applier, embedding-enqueue trigger, standalone backfills, and repair/rearm helpers each repeat the exact hold/generation/revision exclusion under their writer lock. A late result issued before attachment loses its revision/generation/hold CAS and is no-effect. Migration 029 may replace these guards only in the same reviewed DDL that installs the exact immutable hold-release receipt; schema 028 has no release or bypass token.

All legacy rows seed `expected_content_revision` from their current item only after stopped-writer preflight. Enrichment jobs receive deterministic positive generations and the item allocator is seeded above them; the migration does not claim that a legacy summary is a proven current applied generation. A running row requires `claimed_at`, token hash, and `lease_expires_at > claimed_at`; non-running rows carry no live token/lease. Claims set a fresh hash and finite lease atomically. Apply, retry, stale sweep, success, and terminal transitions require the exact hash, expected revision, and applicable generation and clear only their own token/lease.

Rebuild `transcript_attempts` with base `(job_id,item_id)` ownership plus its own immutable `(policy_decision_id,item_id)` policy reference, and add nullable `policy_decision_id`, nullable `expected_content_revision`, nullable `claim_token_hash`, nullable `observed_content_revision`, nullable `result_code`, nullable `provider_result_state`, and nullable `finalization_seal_id`. Copied historical attempts retain null for all seven because 028 cannot truthfully reconstruct their policy/claim/revision/result/finalization authority. `observed_content_revision` is the positive immutable item revision observed by the finalizer's authority classification before any permitted success apply; it is not an FK to the mutable item. It is required whenever an actual provider result is finalized and for the generic-user-text sweep disposition below: ordinary results equal the expected revision, automatic success records that pre-apply value even though the same transaction then advances the item once, and a skipped stale result records the newer current value. `result_code` is non-null only for post-028 `skipped` and its closed allowlist is exactly `stale_item_revision|policy_expired_before_finalize|source_deadline_elapsed_before_finalize|runtime_authority_revoked_before_finalize`. `provider_result_state` is null when no provider result was accepted and otherwise is exactly `success|retryable_error|manual_needed`; it records the actual normalized timed-text result independently of the job/attempt disposition. Migration data copy completes before a named insert guard is installed; every post-028 attempt insert requires a fresh nonempty bounded finalization-seal ID plus a same-item policy, positive expected revision, and policy/revision/claim hash exactly equal to the then-running job snapshot. The historical snapshot does **not** retain a policy-bearing FK back to the mutable singleton job: after an attempt exists the job may be explicitly reauthorized to a later policy while the immutable attempt truthfully keeps the old one. Add `UNIQUE(id,job_id,item_id)`, `UNIQUE(id,job_id,item_id,policy_decision_id)`, `UNIQUE(id,job_id,item_id,policy_decision_id,expected_content_revision,claim_token_hash)`, and `UNIQUE(finalization_seal_id,id,job_id,item_id,policy_decision_id,expected_content_revision,claim_token_hash)` as exact FK parents, plus `UNIQUE(job_id,expected_content_revision,claim_token_hash)` so one post-028 claim can create at most one terminal fact while historical null-authority rows naturally remain outside that uniqueness relation. The attempt has the deferred reverse finalization-seal FK in §6.5.2. The job’s nullable `last_attempt_id` has both a base deferred composite FK back to the same attempt/job/item and a supplemental exact policy FK using §6.11; reauthorization/rearm to a new policy clears the old pointer before changing policy. Attempt number/count/duration fields are nonnegative, Boolean flags are constrained to 0/1 when non-null, hashes are validated, and an attempt can never name a job or policy for another item.

Every post-028 attempt is a closed terminal shape validated by a named insert guard:

- all states require a positive attempt number equal to the running job count, a post-028 provider exactly `youtube_innertube_timedtext|transcript_worker`, nonnegative `started_at`, non-null `finished_at >= started_at`, `duration_ms=finished_at-started_at`, and `created_at` equal to the transaction time. Every non-null `provider_result_state` requires `provider='youtube_innertube_timedtext'`; every null-provider-result pre-dispatch/sweep attempt requires `provider='transcript_worker'`. Copied historical `transcript_provider` rows remain display-only, but no new attempt may store that fallback;
- `success` requires `retryable=0`, `result_code IS NULL`, null error/status fields, provider exactly `youtube_innertube_timedtext`, positive bounded transcript characters, bounded/null language, Boolean/null generated/translated facts, and `artifact_ids_json IS NULL`; an actual timed-text result additionally requires `provider_result_state='success'` and `observed_content_revision=expected_content_revision`;
- `retryable_error` requires `retryable=1`, `result_code IS NULL`, an allowlisted normalized error code and its exact packaged stable message, optional status code in `100..599`, null transcript facts, and null artifact JSON; an actual timed-text result requires `provider_result_state='retryable_error'` and `observed_content_revision=expected_content_revision`, while a `transcript_worker` stale-claim/worker failure has both provider-result fields null;
- `terminal_error` requires the same error shape with `retryable=0`, `result_code IS NULL`, and null transcript/artifact facts; an actual timed-text result requires `provider_result_state='manual_needed'` and `observed_content_revision=expected_content_revision`, while a proven pre-dispatch outcome such as `missing_video_id` has both provider-result fields null;
- `skipped` is a reviewed content-authority disposition, always with `retryable=0` and null transcript/artifact facts, while `provider_result_state` independently preserves any actual result. `stale_item_revision` requires positive `observed_content_revision > expected_content_revision`; a live-lease returned result uses the attempted provider and actual normalized result/error shape, while pre-dispatch or lease-expiry no-contact cleanup uses provider `transcript_worker` and null provider-result/error/status. Its job becomes `ignored` with stable message `Transcript result was ignored because the item content revision changed.`. The other three result codes require provider exactly `youtube_innertube_timedtext`, `observed_content_revision=expected_content_revision`, and non-null actual `provider_result_state`: success has null error/status, while either error state has the matching allowlisted normalized error code, exact stable message, and optional bounded status. They transition the job to `manual_needed` with the disposition code and its exact stable message: `policy_expired_before_finalize` → `Transcript result was not applied because recovery policy authority expired.`; `source_deadline_elapsed_before_finalize` → `Transcript result was not applied because the source retention deadline elapsed.`; `runtime_authority_revoked_before_finalize` → `Transcript result was not applied because runtime recovery authority was revoked.`. Actual provider errors remain separately auditable on the attempt and cannot replace the job disposition reason.

Copied historical rows are preserved byte-for-byte and may use their legacy nullable shapes, but their null authority tuple prevents them from satisfying a current claim, reverse policy pointer, or recovery receipt. Every `transcript_attempts` update is forbidden after insert. Direct deletion of a transcript job or attempt while its item remains is forbidden; only the tested item-root cascade may remove them.

Transcript attempts remain terminal facts; 028 does not create a synthetic `running` attempt. A provider claim exists only on `transcript_jobs`. Successful recovery inserts its `success` attempt inside the atomic apply transaction before the receipt/job pointer finalization. Retryable, terminal, and skipped results likewise insert one terminal attempt in the same transaction as their exact job transition. Historical null-authority attempts are display/audit-only and are never eligible as current apply authority.

Named transcript-job shape guards make claim and completion state physical rather than conventional:

- every row requires `attempts >= 0` and `max_attempts > 0`; `running` requires `1 <= attempts <= max_attempts`, non-null `claimed_at`, `claim_token_hash`, and `lease_expires_at`, requires `lease_expires_at > claimed_at`, and requires `completed_at IS NULL`; every non-running state requires all three live-claim fields null;
- `pending` and `retryable_error` require `attempts < max_attempts` and `completed_at IS NULL`; every post-028 transition into either state has a non-null `next_run_at`, only `next_run_at <= now` is claimable, and copied legacy pending rows may retain their existing nullable schedule but remain inert until the explicit reauthorization/rearm CAS fills it;
- `done`, `manual_needed`, and `ignored` require non-null `completed_at` and null `next_run_at`;
- a post-028 provider transition to `retryable_error`, attempt-backed `manual_needed`, or provider-skipped `ignored` requires the exact terminal attempt pointer and normalized provider/error/result/observed-revision facts described above. The automatic-recovery `done` statement requires its exact success attempt plus the fresh automatic transition's absent preselected recovery-receipt ID and complete application/source tuple at `now=transition.created_at`; the recovery receipt is inserted next, revalidates that final job, and closes the deferred transition cycle;
- a source attachment outside the transcript worker is not a provider attempt. If an existing non-done singleton job is resolved by a post-028 `user_paste`, `uploaded_file`, `youtube_official_caption`, `owned_media_stt`, or `browser_visible_transcript` source, the same body/source transaction sets `state='done'`, `completed_at=updated_at` to its database transaction time, `next_run_at=NULL`, `last_provider` to that exact source kind, and both error fields null. It clears `claimed_at`, `claim_token_hash`, and `lease_expires_at`, but preserves `attempts`, `max_attempts`, `last_attempt_id`, `policy_decision_id`, and the pre-replacement `expected_content_revision`; an already-done job is unchanged and absence of a job does not create one. To avoid an impossible insert-order cycle, the named job transition guard requires the exact current source/segments plus the body transition's preselected terminal-evidence ID and complete tuple, proves that evidence row is still absent, and requires `now=transition.created_at`; the terminal manual seal or attached browser receipt is inserted **after** the job transition and revalidates both that source graph and the final job shape. For each manual kind, the preseal guard recomputes the exact kind-specific provenance/count/body facts for `user_paste` and the two-LF ordered segment assembly, count, per-segment hashes, and integrity for `uploaded_file`, `youtube_official_caption`, and `owned_media_stt`; the final seal makes those facts durable and closes the deferred cycle. Browser resolution analogously uses the absent preselected attached-receipt ID and exact source/hold graph, then the final attached receipt verifies the job. Wrong-kind, stale-revision, wrong-body, wrong-hash, wrong-segment, missing/wrong eventual evidence, missing source, or job-only completion aborts at the statement or deferred commit. No synthetic success/skipped attempt is inserted. A running provider result that loses this writer-lock transition cannot later satisfy its state/token/revision CAS. A preserved `last_attempt_id` continues to identify the prior provider fact; `last_provider` identifies the later non-provider resolution mechanism.
- generic YouTube user-text capture is explicitly not that attachment path. The only source-less body replacement permitted for an existing YouTube item is the dedicated server-only generic-user-text service used by `/api/capture/url`, the item upgrade action, and Telegram capture. That service accepts the current item identity plus analyzed raw user text, derives and validates the canonical video/platform/URL and metadata server-side while preserving the immutable stored identity bytes, calls the pinned `buildYoutubeUserTextCapture`, and emits **both** `extraction_method='youtube_user_provided_text'` and `capture_quality='user_provided_full_text'` with null warning plus the fresh transition-bound immutable generic body-update receipt in §§6.1.1/6.5.3; a caller-supplied `CapturedContent`, transition/receipt ID, Boolean escape hatch, either literal alone, or any other literal pair cannot select this branch. The private transaction requires the §5.2.1 view to return exactly `weak_metadata_capture` under its writer lock, performs the exact common reset, and advances `content_revision` once, but creates no transcript policy, source, seal, segment, attempt, or new transcript job and calls no source-resolution helper. Job handling is closed: absence remains absence; `done|manual_needed|ignored` remains byte-identical; `running` remains byte-identical so its already-issued claim is owned only by the stale-result/lease paths below; and `pending|retryable_error` performs one evidence-backed no-attempt transition to `ignored` in this same transaction. That transition sets `completed_at=updated_at=transition.created_at`, `next_run_at=NULL`, `last_provider='youtube_generic_user_text'`, `last_error_code='generic_user_text_superseded_recovery'`, and `last_error_message='Transcript recovery was closed because user-provided item text replaced the weak capture.'`; its live-claim fields were already null, and it preserves attempts, max attempts, last-attempt pointer, policy, expected revision, item/platform/video identity, priority, and creation time exactly. The job transition guard requires the fresh generic transition's absent preselected receipt ID, exact application/applied revision/body tuple, and `now=transition.created_at`; the receipt inserted last revalidates the final job and closes the deferred cycle, so an attempt-less fabricated ignore cannot commit. Immediately after commit, the classifier returns `current_generic_user_text`; that class plus the current generic receipt independently makes claim, dispatch, apply, explicit retry, enqueue, and backfill ineligible. If a provider result was already in flight, the only accepted result path before lease expiry is the exact attempt-backed `stale_item_revision` finalization below: it has zero content/derived/usage effect, changes the running job only to its frozen `ignored` shape, and cannot apply or retry returned content. Every other classifier result, an active source, or any hold rejects the generic body update. A user who explicitly chooses the dedicated transcript-paste flow instead enters the reviewed atomic `user_paste` policy/body/source/seal/job transaction above. The exact marker tuple plus transition/receipt identifies generic non-transcript state only inside this closed service; it never authorizes source inference, source-backed resolution, or automatic recovery.
- the explicit user review action may transition an existing non-done job to non-provider `ignored` under the same writer lock without inserting an attempt. It stamps `completed_at=updated_at` to the transaction time, clears schedule/live-claim fields, preserves attempts, prior attempt/policy/revision audit fields, and causes any preempted running result to lose its CAS. The provider-skipped path and the evidence-backed generic pending/retryable close above remain distinct. Generic attempt-less provider completion through `markTranscriptJobDone` is unsupported after 028.

Stale-claim sweep is attempt-backed, not an exception to these shapes. At database time `now >= lease_expires_at`, one immediate transaction revalidates the exact running policy/revision/claim tuple. If the item still has the expected revision and remains a recovery candidate, it inserts one `retryable_error` attempt with a fresh finalization-seal ID, provider `transcript_worker`, code/message exactly `stale_claim`, `attempt_number=job.attempts`, `started_at=claimed_at`, `finished_at=created_at=now`, and deterministic duration. It points the job at that attempt, copies the normalized provider/error facts, clears the exact live claim, and inserts the §6.5.2 seal last. If `attempts < max_attempts`, it schedules `retryable_error`; the final allowed claim instead becomes `manual_needed` with no schedule. If instead the exact source-less/unheld generic-user-text shape with its current transition-bound body-update receipt has advanced the current revision beyond the claim, the sweep inserts the `skipped`/`stale_item_revision` attempt with its fresh seal ID, `observed_content_revision=items.content_revision`, `provider='transcript_worker'`, and null provider-result/error/status facts, then performs the same exact `ignored` job transition and terminal seal as the returned-result path with no provider-health effect. Every other revision/item/source/hold/transition/receipt mismatch is a no-effect containment failure for a separate reviewed reconciler, never a fabricated retry. The lease boundary is exclusive for every provider-result path: only `now < lease_expires_at` may finalize a returned result, while equality belongs to this sweep. Attempt-claim uniqueness makes a duplicate sweeper no-effect, and a concurrent late provider or source attachment must lose one of the serialized state/token/source CAS checks.

Explicit retry/rearm is one closed non-provider transaction, not a generic state rewrite. A missing job, `running`, `done`, or `ignored` job is byte-identical no-effect. An eligible source-less/unheld/non-generic `pending|retryable_error` job may become `pending` at database `now` with attempts and ceiling unchanged, `next_run_at=updated_at=now`, null completion/live-claim fields, cleared display error code/message, and every identity/priority/creation/provider/history field preserved. An eligible `manual_needed` job may take the same edge only with `max_attempts=MAX(OLD.max_attempts,OLD.attempts+1)`—exactly one available claim when exhausted—while attempts remain unchanged and its prior terminal attempt/seal stays immutable. Both edges rebind the current item revision and one current allowed policy; an incompatible `last_attempt_id` is cleared before a policy change, while a same-policy pointer is preserved. Active source/hold, current generic receipt, ineligible item, denied/expired policy, retention/runtime denial, or any unsupported source state is no-effect. Direct `retryTranscriptJobNow`/stale-rearm writes that do not match this complete shape are retired and rejected. Claim still waits for the ordinary schedule/cooldown gates.

Raw tokens are forbidden. An unaware binary that omits these fields is unsupported on schema 028 and must be rejected before startup.

#### Closed normalized provider/error registry

The provider adapter and sole finalizer share one exported, hash-attested registry; SQLite attempt/job guards encode the same rows. No implementation layer may invent a retry class, stable message, status shape, throttle behavior, provider-result state, or job effect. Provider-controlled messages and unknown raw codes are never persisted. Reduction is explicitly two-phase. Phase A validates only the provider-owned envelope/code/status and yields either one error row below or a tagged `phase_a_success_envelope` with null code/error/status; it does not inspect returned transcript content. Stale-revision and policy/deadline/runtime dispositions may persist that tag only as a `skipped` attempt with `provider_result_state='success'`; they never claim the content was complete. Only when content authority remains current does Phase B validate the strict `youtube-recovery-result-v1`; a malformed Phase-A success becomes `recovery_contract_incomplete` before an attempt, health update, or apply, while a strict Phase-B success alone may create a `success` attempt or recovery receipt. Thus exact guards distinguish preliminary-envelope success from applied strict success by attempt/disposition shape even though both have null normalized error code. Every path selects exactly one of these frozen facts before its attempt insert:

| Normalized code | Accepted origin/result | Attempt state / `retryable` / `provider_result_state` | Status shape | Throttle | Exact stable message |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| *(null Phase-A success)* | provider-owned success envelope only; content deliberately unchecked | only an authority/stale `skipped` disposition / `0` / `success` | null | no | null |
| *(null Phase-B success)* | strict complete `youtube-recovery-result-v1` | `success` / `0` / `success` | null | no | null |
| `video_unavailable` | normalized timed-text result | `terminal_error` / `0` / `manual_needed` | null | no | `The YouTube video is unavailable.` |
| `invalid_youtube_url` | normalized timed-text result | `terminal_error` / `0` / `manual_needed` | null | no | `The item does not have a valid YouTube URL.` |
| `captions_unavailable` | normalized timed-text result | `terminal_error` / `0` / `manual_needed` | null | no | `No transcript captions were available.` |
| `metadata_only` | normalized timed-text result | `terminal_error` / `0` / `manual_needed` | null | no | `Transcript recovery returned metadata only.` |
| `transcript_manual_needed` | unknown/manual adapter code after closed fallback | `terminal_error` / `0` / `manual_needed` | null | no | `Transcript recovery needs manual help.` |
| `live_stream_captions_pending` | normalized timed-text result | `retryable_error` / `1` / `retryable_error` | null | no | `Live-stream captions are not ready yet.` |
| `innertube_fetch_failed` | normalized timed-text result | `retryable_error` / `1` / `retryable_error` | null | no | `The transcript provider could not be reached.` |
| `provider_exception` | normalized adapter exception | `retryable_error` / `1` / `retryable_error` | null | no | `The transcript provider failed unexpectedly.` |
| `timedtext_fetch_failed` | normalized timed-text result with no HTTP status | `retryable_error` / `1` / `retryable_error` | null | no | `The transcript provider request failed.` |
| `recovery_contract_incomplete` | returned success that fails the strict server-owned recovery-result contract before content apply | `retryable_error` / `1` / `retryable_error` | null | no | `The transcript provider returned an incomplete recovery contract.` |
| `transcript_retryable_error` | unknown/retryable adapter code after closed fallback | `retryable_error` / `1` / `retryable_error` | null | no | `Transcript recovery hit a retryable error.` |
| `timedtext_http_429` | raw `timedtext_http_429` only | `retryable_error` / `1` / `retryable_error` | exactly 429 | yes | `The transcript provider rate limited recovery.` |
| `timedtext_http_5xx` | raw `timedtext_http_NNN`, `500 <= NNN <= 599` | `retryable_error` / `1` / `retryable_error` | exact NNN | no | `The transcript provider is temporarily unavailable.` |
| `youtube_antibot_metadata_only` | normalized anti-bot metadata-only result | `retryable_error` / `1` / `retryable_error` | null | yes | `The transcript provider required an unsupported challenge.` |
| `timedtext_http_error` | raw `timedtext_http_NNN`, `100 <= NNN <= 499`, `NNN != 429` | `terminal_error` / `0` / `manual_needed` | exact NNN | no | `The transcript provider rejected the request.` |

An unknown raw code maps only to `transcript_retryable_error` when the adapter's separately validated state is retryable or to `transcript_manual_needed` when it is manual; it can never become a new stored literal. A raw `timedtext_http_NNN` outside `100..599`, a code/status disagreement, a status on a null-status row, a raw message selected as the stable message, or success with any error/status field rejects the result without consuming the live claim and raises the content-free containment code `provider_contract_invalid` for operator reconciliation. Every Phase-A row is server-bound to provider `youtube_innertube_timedtext`; `transcript_provider` is a copied historical display literal only and is retired from new attempts/jobs. The registry module's exact source hash is part of `PACKAGED_YOUTUBE_BROWSER_SCHEMA_CONTRACT` before migration implementation GO.

Ordinary job behavior is also closed. A non-throttled retryable row exits to `retryable_error` with the §8.2 base schedule when `attempts < max_attempts`, otherwise to `manual_needed`; its attempt remains `retryable_error` in both cases. Either throttled row always exits to `retryable_error`, extends `max_attempts` to `MAX(old.max_attempts,attempts+3)`, and uses the exact cooldown rule in §8.2. Every manual row exits to `manual_needed`. Only a committed unique actual-provider attempt changes provider health, using its persisted `provider_result_state` and normalized code/status; pre-dispatch/lease-sweep rows below have no health effect.

Claim and dispatch each re-read the global cooldown at their own database clock and allow equality (`now >= cooldown_until`). Immediately before contact, dispatch first applies this no-contact precedence under one writer transaction: missing/cascaded job is no-effect; exact `current_generic_user_text` revision drift becomes the sealed `transcript_worker`/`skipped`/`stale_item_revision` ignored shape with no provider result or health effect; a missing/mismatched/non-v1 client-storage contract makes the job ineligible before claim and, if authority drifted after claim, selects `runtime_authority_revoked_before_dispatch` with zero provider contact; otherwise `now >= source_delete_by` selects `source_deadline_elapsed_before_dispatch`, else `now >= policy_expires_at` selects `policy_expired_before_dispatch`, else any fresh deployment/external-lab/schema-ready/feature/worker/kill-switch/retention denial or classifier result other than `weak_metadata_capture` selects `runtime_authority_revoked_before_dispatch`; only then is cooldown tested. Each of those three authority codes writes one sealed `transcript_worker` `terminal_error` attempt with null provider-result/observed-revision/status/transcript facts, raises `max_attempts` only to `MAX(old.max_attempts,attempts+1)`, exits the job to `manual_needed` with the exact code/message and `completed_at=updated_at=now`, and clears the claim/schedule without provider health. If authority remains live but `now < cooldown_until`, the sole finalizer writes and seals the exact null-provider-result `provider_cooldown_before_dispatch` attempt below, returns the job to `retryable_error`, sets `next_run_at=cooldown_until`, and raises `max_attempts` only to `MAX(old.max_attempts,attempts+1)`. Thus no no-contact branch consumes the final permitted provider try or survives as a misleading stale claim.

Pre-dispatch and worker-owned failures use this separate exact null-provider-result registry:

| Code | Provider / attempt shape | Job effect | Exact stable message |
|---|---|---|---|
| `missing_video_id` | `transcript_worker`, `terminal_error`, retryable 0, null `provider_result_state`/status | `manual_needed` | `The item does not have a valid YouTube video identifier.` |
| `stale_claim` | `transcript_worker`, `retryable_error`, retryable 1, null `provider_result_state`/status | ordinary retry/exhaustion split | `The transcript worker claim expired before completion.` |
| `worker_exception` | `transcript_worker`, `retryable_error`, retryable 1, null `provider_result_state`/status | ordinary retry/exhaustion split | `The transcript worker failed unexpectedly.` |
| `provider_cooldown_before_dispatch` | `transcript_worker`, `retryable_error`, retryable 1, null `provider_result_state`/status | exact no-contact deferral above | `Transcript recovery waited for the provider cooldown.` |
| `source_deadline_elapsed_before_dispatch` | `transcript_worker`, `terminal_error`, retryable 0, null `provider_result_state`/status | exact no-contact manual disposition above | `Transcript recovery was not dispatched because the source retention deadline elapsed.` |
| `policy_expired_before_dispatch` | `transcript_worker`, `terminal_error`, retryable 0, null `provider_result_state`/status | exact no-contact manual disposition above | `Transcript recovery was not dispatched because policy authority expired.` |
| `runtime_authority_revoked_before_dispatch` | `transcript_worker`, `terminal_error`, retryable 0, null `provider_result_state`/status | exact no-contact manual disposition above | `Transcript recovery was not dispatched because runtime authority was revoked.` |

`item_missing` is also retired as a post-028 attempt/job disposition: item-root deletion cascades the job before a retained worker can finalize, and that worker's missing-claim reconciliation is content-free no-effect. `item_upgrade_failed` is retired because the R-032 transaction either commits its complete success graph or rolls back. The worker-only fallback `transcript_unavailable` is retired in favor of the closed rows above: an accepted actual provider result uses `captions_unavailable`, while an absent/unknown raw code must take the validated manual/retryable fallback and can never invent this legacy literal. Thus no post-028 attempt/job may store `item_missing|item_upgrade_failed|transcript_unavailable`; a browser popup's separately packaged local UI state with the same English token is not an attempt/job error and supplies no database authority. Legacy attempts and legacy job messages remain byte-stable display evidence but are never accepted by a new attempt, claim, retry, or health event. `generic_user_text_superseded_recovery` is the receipt-backed no-attempt job-only disposition defined above and is intentionally absent from both attempt registries.

#### 6.5.1 Automatic-recovery source contract

An automatic recovery success must no longer pass a body-only `CapturedContent` into a generic item upgrade. The provider adapter returns a strict `youtube-recovery-result-v1` value containing the canonical body plus an ordered source payload:

```text
provider exact youtube_innertube_timedtext
source_kind exact lab_public_caption
policy_decision_id exact running-job policy
language_code bounded BCP-47 token or null
caption_source_class standard|asr|forced|unknown
timestamp_mode timestamped|paragraph_only
extractor_version and parser_version reviewed tokens
segments 1..7200 ordered tuples (idx,start_ms,duration_ms|null,end_ms|null,text)
```

`source_kind` and `policy_decision_id` are attached by the server from the immutable claim/policy snapshot after the provider adapter returns; they are never accepted from a provider response or caller. The result's provider/video identity and cue payload are revalidated against that snapshot before apply.

InnerTube timed-text is public extraction, not the OAuth/owned-video YouTube Captions API. Its policy/source kind is therefore the existing restricted `lab_public_caption`, never `youtube_official_caption`. Before claim/dispatch, the current policy service must produce a same-item decision with `method='lab_public_caption'`, `environment='lab'`, `rights_basis='public_lab_only'`, `production_allowed=0`, full-text retention backed by the required legal approval, and current non-production deployment authority. Production, test/development live access, missing/expired approval, or unclassified/conflicting deployment denies before provider contact. This physical contract confers no live-lab authority.

The success adapter must expose real parsed cue structure. It may use one paragraph-only segment only when the upstream result itself is explicitly paragraph-only; it may not synthesize timestamps, globally deduplicate/sort cues, or derive source truth from the generic item body. A legacy body-only provider result is `recovery_contract_incomplete`, performs no apply, and finalizes through the retryable/manual policy rather than fabricating segments.

The service validates contiguous ordinals, nonempty normalized cue text, source limits, and exact video/policy/provider contract. For `timestamped`, every cue has a nonnegative safe-integer start, starts are nondecreasing, text is at most 2,000 Unicode scalar values, and duration/end are either both null or nonnegative safe integers satisfying `end_ms=start_ms+duration_ms`; no missing value is synthesized. `paragraph_only` is allowed only as one index-zero segment with all three time fields null when the upstream result explicitly declares that mode. Named source/segment/receipt guards enforce the same shape before commit. The service preserves two distinct representations and hashes:

- canonical source text uses `youtube-timedtext-render-v1`: timestamped input uses the current `formatTranscriptBody` rendering over ordered parsed cues from `youtube.ts` SHA-256 `cd68a29fb0f060202b2ab4b48b5622d3302593fff5dd73cf4a4e7a96c5c0aa84`, while an explicitly paragraph-only result uses its one normalized segment text with no timestamp prefix; `transcript_sources.text_sha256` hashes those exact UTF-8 bytes;
- the item body uses `youtube-body-v1`, preserving the current deterministic Title/Channel/Published/Duration/URL/Capture-quality, optional Description/Chapters, and `Transcript:\n<canonical source text>` format from `youtube-body.ts` SHA-256 `b68ab865d208abe50a35c103cbb8d449a23f44c38450199ed807ce9aa78984bc`; the recovery receipt separately stores `applied_item_body_sha256`.

This is an explicit no-format-migration contract: source transcript text is not conflated with the metadata-bearing item body, and B27/B28 byte equality for the same fixture is mandatory. The inserted source has `provenance_schema='youtube-recovery-provenance-v1'`. Server code computes every segment hash, source `text_sha256`, item-body hash, `transcript-integrity-v1`, and exact segment count. It inserts an active `lab_public_caption` source and its segments in the R-032 transaction with canonical UTF-8 JSON, no duplicate keys, and exactly this provenance object with no additional keys:

```text
schema_version exact youtube-recovery-provenance-v1
observation_kind exact youtube_lab_public_caption_recovery
provider exact youtube_innertube_timedtext
extractor_version 1..128 reviewed ASCII token
parser_version 1..128 reviewed ASCII token
server_normalization_version exact youtube-transcript-normalization-v1
source_text_contract_version exact youtube-timedtext-render-v1
item_body_contract_version exact youtube-body-v1
language_code bounded BCP-47 token or null
caption_source_class standard|asr|forced|unknown
timestamp_mode timestamped|paragraph_only
transcript_is_generated boolean or null
transcript_is_translated boolean or null
cue_count 1..7200
total_chars 1..500000, exact Unicode-scalar count of canonical source text
received_at nonnegative epoch equal to source.created_at and the transaction time
```

The object excludes raw provider response/error/request IDs, URLs, titles, track labels, cookies, authorization, signed resources, and arbitrary fields. A named `youtube-recovery-provenance-v1` insert trigger validates `json_valid`, duplicate-key absence, exact key membership/count, JSON types, token/language bounds, fixed-key canonical serialization, `received_at` equality, and equality to separately bound provider/source/segment facts. Its `total_chars`, the success attempt's `transcript_chars`, and the recovery receipt's character equality all use the exact Unicode-scalar count of the canonical **source transcript text**, never JavaScript UTF-16 length and never the metadata-bearing item body; `items.total_chars` remains the deliberately separate UTF-16 count from §6.1.2. The source policy ID, item, accepted revision, hashes/counts/delete-by, and receipt must agree by the exact FKs/guards in §6.11. Its `source_delete_by` is copied from the restricted lab policy and invokes the same item-root cleanup contract as a browser source. Any active transcript source of **any** kind blocks automatic apply; “browser source” is not a narrower substitute.

#### 6.5.2 Immutable `transcript_attempt_finalization_seals`

An attempt insert cannot be allowed to consume one-attempt-per-claim uniqueness while leaving its job running. Create a terminal finalization-seal table with this exact all-non-null physical column order:

```text
seal_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL, fresh bounded opaque ID
attempt_id INTEGER NOT NULL UNIQUE CHECK > 0
job_id INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK > 0
item_id TEXT NOT NULL
policy_decision_id TEXT NOT NULL
expected_content_revision INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK > 0
claim_token_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
terminal_job_state TEXT NOT NULL exact done|retryable_error|manual_needed|ignored
provider_health_effect TEXT NOT NULL exact success|failure|none
sealed_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
```

Add `UNIQUE(seal_id,attempt_id,job_id,item_id,policy_decision_id,expected_content_revision,claim_token_hash)`. Every post-028 attempt is inserted with a preselected `finalization_seal_id`, then the provider-health reducer runs exactly when its persisted actual provider-result fields require it, then the job performs its exact pointer/state transition, and finally the seal is inserted. A deferred reverse FK from the complete attempt/seal tuple requires that final row at commit; the seal has the inverse immediate exact-attempt FK plus the item-root FK in §6.11. Copied historical attempts alone retain a null seal ID and cannot be used as current authority.

The seal-insert trigger requires the attempt's exact non-null authority tuple and `finalization_seal_id=seal_id`, `transcript_jobs.last_attempt_id=attempt_id`, the same job/item/policy, all live-claim fields cleared, `job.updated_at=attempt.created_at=seal.sealed_at`, and the exact terminal job shape implied by the attempt: success plus recovery receipt is `done`; non-exhausted or explicitly throttle-extended retry is `retryable_error`; exhausted retry or terminal/manual result is `manual_needed`; provider/boundary skipped is `manual_needed` except stale revision, which is `ignored`. Its `provider_health_effect` is `success` for actual provider success, `failure` for actual retryable/manual provider result, and `none` only when `provider_result_state IS NULL`; for the first two, the stored provider-health projection at seal time must have matching `updatedAt`, success/failure timestamp, normalized error/status, and reset/cooldown shape. The reducer still derives solely from the newly inserted attempt and is conditional on that unique insert. Missing/wrong job transition, pointer, health effect, claim clearing, clock, or seal aborts the transaction through the trigger or deferred FK.

Every seal field is immutable. Its insert guard rejects an existing seal or attempt ID before conflict handling, direct deletion while the item exists aborts, and item-root cascade removes the attempt/seal cycle coherently. Later reviewed rearm may change the singleton job while the immutable seal remains historical proof of the original terminalization. No production code may insert `transcript_attempts` except the one immediate finalizer that owns attempt, provider health, job transition, and seal; repository scans reject another insert API or raw insert.

#### 6.5.3 Immutable `youtube_generic_body_update_receipts`

The exact generic user-text marker pair is product state, not sufficient physical proof that the closed service owned the body transition. Create a durable non-transcript mutation receipt and bind it to the preinserted transition in §6.1.1. Its exact all-non-null physical column order is:

```text
receipt_id TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL
body_transition_id TEXT NOT NULL
item_id TEXT NOT NULL
previous_content_revision INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK > 0
accepted_content_revision INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK = previous_content_revision + 1
mutation_kind TEXT NOT NULL exact youtube_generic_user_text
caller_surface TEXT NOT NULL exact capture_url|item_upgrade_action|telegram_capture
source_platform TEXT NOT NULL exact youtube|youtube_short
renderer_version TEXT NOT NULL exact youtube-user-provided-body-v1
extraction_method TEXT NOT NULL exact youtube_user_provided_text
capture_quality TEXT NOT NULL exact user_provided_full_text
body_sha256 TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
created_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK >= 0
```

Add `UNIQUE(item_id,accepted_content_revision)`, `UNIQUE(receipt_id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,extraction_method,capture_quality)`, and `UNIQUE(receipt_id,body_transition_id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,body_sha256)`. The receipt has an immediate item-root FK and the exact `generic_user_text` body-transition FK. Historical or newly inserted generic items may have no update receipt; they are readable, but absence cannot serve as authority for an existing-item mutation or stale-result classification.

The dedicated service opens `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, derives the current YouTube identity and canonical user text server-side, freshly requires `youtube_body_authority_v1.authority_class='weak_metadata_capture'`, and captures a byte-stable all-column transcript-job snapshot (or exact absence). It preselects transition and receipt IDs, inserts the exact `generic_user_text` body transition, performs one body update with the exact builder output and complete marker tuple plus the §6.1.2 common reset, applies the one closed job case above using the transition's preselected generic-receipt ID, and inserts the immutable receipt last before commit. The receipt-insert guard requires the exact selected transition, previous/current revisions, platform/marker/renderer/caller allowlist, `body_sha256=transition.body_sha256=sha256_utf8(items.body)`, no active source/hold, null warning, exact extraction version, and `created_at=transition.created_at` at the sampled transaction time. It also reselects the job: absence, running, and terminal snapshots must remain byte-identical; a captured pending/retryable snapshot must differ only by the exact generic evidence-backed ignored shape. The terminal guard requires the postimage classifier to be exactly `current_generic_user_text`. A missing, stale, cross-item, partial-null, wrong-body, wrong-marker, wrong-revision, transition-only, receipt-only, stranded pending/retryable, non-weak preimage, wrong postimage class, or otherwise changed job graph fails, and the transition's deferred FK makes a body update with forged marker literals but no exact receipt fail at commit.

Receipt columns are immutable. Its insert guard rejects an existing receipt, transition, or item/revision before conflict handling; direct deletion while the item exists aborts, and item-root cascade deletes both rows coherently. A later authorized body/revision transition leaves the historical receipt untouched and uses fresh never-reused IDs. It stores no raw text, transcript, URL, title, request payload, token, policy, source, segment, or provider fact and confers no transcript/recovery authority.

### 6.6 `content_processing_holds`

Create `content_processing_holds` as `STRICT` append-preserving history with this exact physical column order:

| Column | Contract |
|---|---|
| `id` | opaque text primary key |
| `item_id` | item FK, cascade |
| `transcript_source_id` | source binding |
| `expected_content_revision` | positive exact revision |
| `policy_decision_id` | exact policy binding |
| `owner_scope_hash` | 64-hex server-derived owner scope |
| `source_delete_by` | nonnegative epoch exactly equal to source/policy deadline |
| `held_enrichment_generation` | positive unique generation allocated by the item |
| `state` | `held|released`, default `held` |
| `reason` | exact `youtube_browser_v0_1` |
| `release_receipt_id` | nullable; must be null in 028 |
| `created_at` | nonnegative epoch |
| `released_at` | nullable; must be null in 028 |

Composite FKs bind source/item/revision/policy/owner and policy/item/owner. The held generation must equal the rearmed enrichment job for the same item/revision. Add:

- one unique active hold per item where `state='held'`;
- one unique row for `(item_id,transcript_source_id,expected_content_revision)` so an exact binding cannot be silently re-held;
- one parent key `UNIQUE(item_id,transcript_source_id,expected_content_revision,held_enrichment_generation,policy_decision_id,owner_scope_hash,source_delete_by)` for exact receipt binding;
- active-claim index on `(item_id,state,expected_content_revision)`.

The named hold `BEFORE INSERT` guard first rejects every collision in the complete uniqueness set: an existing `id`; an existing `(item_id,transcript_source_id,expected_content_revision)`; an existing full seven-column receipt-parent tuple; and, for `NEW.state='held'`, any existing held row for `NEW.item_id`. Those checks run before SQLite resolves the primary key, partial active-item key, either composite key, UPSERT, or `INSERT OR REPLACE`, so an old hold/source/job graph cannot be cascade-deleted when recursive triggers are off. A later unique index is forbidden unless its collision test lands in this guard and the schema manifest in the same reviewed migration.

Migration 028 accepts inserts only in the held shape and forbids all application updates and direct deletes while the parent item remains. Its source must still be the exact active `browser_visible_transcript` row when the hold is inserted; its referenced enrichment singleton must already be the exact pending/unclaimed row and must survive the insert byte-for-byte. A held row thereafter blocks source status transitions and all body updates. Item deletion cascades through its source and hold graph. Migration 029 may replace the guard only after its immutable manual-release receipt exists and a reviewed trigger proves exact item/source/revision authority. No 028 route or service can release a hold. Ordinary insert/update, UPSERT, `INSERT OR REPLACE`, and `UPDATE OR REPLACE` collision tests with `recursive_triggers` off and on prove the old hold, source, transition, receipt, policy, item, and enrichment singleton remain exact.

### 6.7 Browser authority epoch and `youtube_browser_capture_intents`

#### Browser authority epoch singleton

Create `youtube_browser_authority_epoch` as a one-row `STRICT` table with this exact physical column order:

```text
singleton INTEGER PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK(singleton=1)
authority_epoch_id TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE lowercase 64-hex encoding fresh random raw32
created_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(created_at>=0)
creation_reason TEXT NOT NULL exact migration_initialize_v1|backup_sanitize_v1|restore_invalidate_v1
operation_id TEXT NOT NULL bounded opaque ID
previous_epoch_id TEXT nullable lowercase 64-hex
CHECK(
  (creation_reason='migration_initialize_v1' AND previous_epoch_id IS NULL)
  OR
  (creation_reason IN ('backup_sanitize_v1','restore_invalidate_v1')
   AND previous_epoch_id IS NOT NULL
   AND previous_epoch_id<>authority_epoch_id)
)
```

Migration 028 creates exactly one `migration_initialize_v1` row before any browser authority can exist. Every intent, permanent inspect issuance, and upload grant copies the exact current `authority_epoch_id`; insert/transition guards reselect the singleton and reject another epoch. The ID is a nonsecret random epoch discriminator, not a credential. Token envelopes carry the exact version and epoch so a wrong/retired epoch is rejected before secret hashing or authority lookup.

Ordinary insert/update/delete, UPSERT, and `REPLACE` of the singleton abort. The only later update occurs on a fresh verified disposable backup/restore clone under the existing guard-bypass capability: the operation journal fixes the fresh random ID and operation ID; the scrubber deletes every upload grant, deletes every capture intent, asserts both tables are empty, changes the singleton once to the exact next reason/previous/current tuple, recreates the guard from packaged SQL, and verifies the complete descriptor before proceeding. The bypass never drops or weakens permanent issuance or receipt guards. A retry for the same journaled operation must use the same chosen epoch; a distinct operation must choose a different fresh epoch. RNG repetition, a missing delete, or a different prior epoch aborts.

Backup sanitization performs that clone-wide invalidation for every database, even when no restricted item root or active authority exists, and records `backup_sanitize_v1`. Restore performs it again after candidate/prior-live/ledger reconciliation and before target publication, recording `restore_invalidate_v1`. Startup/readiness accepts a live S28 database only with `migration_initialize_v1` or `restore_invalidate_v1`; it rejects a directly launched sanitized backup whose current reason is `backup_sanitize_v1`. Permanent issuance rows and immutable receipts remain byte-identical through both rotations, but no old intent/inspect/upload token can become authority because every mutable row is absent and its envelope epoch differs before lookup.

This is mutable, content-free, hashed authority; it is not a receipt.

The exact physical column order follows; all columns are `NOT NULL` unless explicitly marked nullable:

```text
intent_hash primary key
```
