```text
authority_epoch_id lowercase 64-hex exact current singleton
owner_scope_hash
item_id item FK cascade
expected_content_revision positive
canonical_video_id_hash
page_route_class exact standard_watch_v1|shorts_fixture_v1
policy_decision_id
policy_expires_at
source_delete_by
extension_id_hash
extension_version
api_version
route_contract_version
extractor_contract_version
transfer_contract_version
return_route_kind exact item_detail_v1
panel_binding_hash nullable 64-hex
state created|claimed|inspect_authorized|review_pending|upload_authorized|committed|terminal_conflict|cancelled|revoked|expired
claimed_at nullable
inspect_generation nonnegative default 0
inspect_grant_hash nullable
inspect_issued_at nullable
inspect_expires_at nullable
inspect_consumed_at nullable
inspect_invalidated_at nullable
created_at
expires_at exactly created_at + 1800000
terminal_receipt_id nullable bounded opaque ID
terminal_at nullable
updated_at
```

#### 6.7.1 Permanent inspect-authorization issuance history

Create `youtube_browser_inspect_authorization_issuances` as a permanent content-free `STRICT` table with this exact physical column order:

```text
inspect_grant_hash TEXT PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
authority_epoch_id TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
intent_hash TEXT NOT NULL lowercase 64-hex
inspect_generation INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(inspect_generation > 0)
issued_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(issued_at >= 0)
expires_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(expires_at >= 0)
CHECK(expires_at-issued_at=60000)
UNIQUE(authority_epoch_id,intent_hash,inspect_generation)
```

This table deliberately has no item or intent FK. It is permanent content-free nonauthority within a live epoch and backup snapshot, and survives consumption, invalidation, replacement, terminalization, mutable-intent cleanup, item deletion, clone-wide authority invalidation, backup sanitization, and process restart. The sole inspect-issuance statement inserts this immutable row. Its trigger joins the current intent and singleton, validates every ordinary inspect gate, requires exact epoch equality and the new generation to equal the intent's old generation plus one, CAS-updates that intent to the exact live hash/generation/issued/expiry shape, and aborts unless exactly one intent changed. The intent guard rejects an `inspect_authorized` edge without that exact just-inserted issuance. Update, delete, UPSERT replacement, and `REPLACE` of an issuance always abort; a pre-conflict guard rejects either primary hash or `(authority_epoch_id,intent_hash,inspect_generation)` collision before conflict handling.

The issuance row never grants consumption authority. Consumption comes only from the current live intent's exact epoch/state/hash/generation/bindings. Clearing or deleting current authority therefore cannot make the same `(epoch,raw secret)` reusable: it hashes identically and collides before intent mutation. A restored older snapshot is not falsely claimed monotonic to live issuances that occurred after that snapshot; those absent rows are harmless because restore removes all mutable authority and rotates the epoch before exposure. Backup/restore require issuance rows present in their input snapshot to remain byte-identical.

#### 6.7.2 Intent keys and guards

Required keys and guards:

- unique active intent per item for states before terminal;
- unique non-null live `inspect_grant_hash` as defense in depth, while the permanent issuance primary key and exact epoch tuple are the authoritative never-reuse guarantee across closed/cleaned intents and deleted items within that epoch;
- an item FK plus the base and exact owner/policy-window composite FKs in §6.11; expected revision is enforced by named issuance/transition triggers and service CAS, never by an FK to mutable `items.content_revision`;
- immutable epoch/owner/item/revision/video/page-route/policy-window/extension/version/contract/return-kind/expiry binding. Intent insert and every later edge require exact current-singleton equality; a stale envelope fails before intent lookup. Insert and the `created -> claimed` edge also require the current classifier to be exactly `weak_metadata_capture` and the exact same-item `no_durable_client_copy_v1` row/hash. The insert shape joins the policy and requires `created_at < expires_at=created_at+1800000 <= policy_expires_at <= retention_cleanup_start_by < source_delete_by`. `standard_watch_v1` requires `items.source_platform='youtube'` and a server/worker-parsed active page with exact HTTPS host `www.youtube.com`, path `/watch`, one canonical `v` identity equal to the item/video binding, and no Shorts, embed, mobile, live, playlist-only, or alternate route. `shorts_fixture_v1` requires `items.source_platform='youtube_short'`, environment `test|development`, the packaged fixture server/origin/document hash, and an execution-proven no-network synthetic harness; it is rejected in `lab|production` and can never authorize a live tab. Any other page route or ambiguous/cross-route URL is no authority. `panel_binding_hash` has one explicit pre-child exception to general parent-key immutability: it is null on insert, may change exactly once from null to the validated hash in the same `created -> claimed` worker-CAS statement while no inspect/grant/receipt child exists, and is immutable thereafter. It is non-null in every claimed/inspect/review/upload/receipt-bearing state. A direct `created -> cancelled|revoked|expired` terminal edge retains null because no panel was ever bound; a terminal row reached after claim retains its non-null hash. No other state may have null and no terminal edge may add, clear, or replace it;
- the only nonterminal edges are `created -> claimed`, `claimed|review_pending -> inspect_authorized`, `inspect_authorized -> inspect_authorized` on authorized replacement, `inspect_authorized -> review_pending` on consumption, `inspect_authorized -> claimed` on invalidation, the trigger-owned `review_pending -> upload_authorized` edge caused by exact grant insertion, and the trigger-owned `upload_authorized -> claimed` edge caused by exact nonreceipt grant closure when a fresh sequence still fits. A separate cancel/revoke/expiry transaction may move a state with no issued grant once to its corresponding terminal state; while an issued grant exists, it must instead close that grant through the coupled trigger in §6.8, which atomically selects the exact intent terminal edge. A fully authenticated exact grant may move to `terminal_conflict` only for an allowlisted durable greater-revision current-state conflict or otherwise attachable note conflict. Rejected upload-grant/commit auth, binding, time, pre-body revision, lower-revision, weak-greater-revision, same-revision-nonweak, or invalid-graph gates do not change intent state. No terminal state exits;
- an inspect authorization is issued only when at least 60,000 ms remains on the intent, so `inspect_expires_at=inspect_issued_at+60000` never exceeds intent expiry;
- the never-issued shape has generation 0 and all five inspect hash/time fields null;
- the live shape has positive generation, non-null hash/issued/expiry, null consumed/invalidated, and state `inspect_authorized`;
- the closed shape has positive generation, null hash, retained issued/expiry, and exactly one of consumed/invalidated non-null. Successful consume moves to `review_pending`; inspect-authority expiry, cancellation, replacement, or an inspect-specific revalidation failure sets invalidated and moves to `claimed`. Reissue/replacement is allowed only before upload/terminal state after every inspect gate passes again, inserts the fresh permanent issuance first, increments generation exactly once through that trigger-owned statement, replaces the retained timestamps, writes the fresh hash, and clears both closure timestamps. Every previous raw authorization remains invalid forever through its retained issuance row;
- inspect use is a durable two-step content-free handshake under the later route contract: `authorize-inspect` issues the raw authorization/hash, then immediately before any isolated DOM execution the approved worker calls the exact `consume-inspect` operation with the raw token. Authorization and consumption each run one immediate transaction that CAS-validates intent state, panel binding, inspect generation/hash, database time, kill switch, extension/version, item revision, video, exact current page-route classification, policy, `youtube_body_authority_v1.authority_class='weak_metadata_capture'`, the exact `no_durable_client_copy_v1` row/hash, and absence of an active source/hold; consumption clears the hash and stamps `inspect_consumed_at`. Only a successful consume response permits one isolated execution. A legacy/unproven client contract, non-weak class, Shorts/embed/mobile/live route presented to a standard-watch intent, or a non-fixture Shorts route revokes without inspection. Response loss permits no inspection and requires a fresh authorization generation;
- terminalization invalidates a live inspect authorization, clears its hash, and sets `terminal_at` exactly once. A previously consumed closed shape remains closed;
- `terminal_receipt_id` is null in every nonterminal state and in `cancelled|revoked|expired`; `committed|terminal_conflict` requires the one preselected terminal receipt ID, sets it in the same state transition, and can commit only through the deferred exact receipt relation in §6.11. It is immutable once non-null;
- document/tab/video identity drift, panel remount/reload with a new binding, navigation, or extension identity drift revokes the intent rather than replacing `panel_binding_hash`; the user must obtain a fresh intent and inspect again;
- `return_route_kind='item_detail_v1'` is the complete same-item return binding. After a terminal receipt, server/package code derives the route from the immutable `item_id` and compile-time exact Brain page origin. No caller supplies a path, URL, origin, query, fragment, or destination;
- no transcript, title, URL/path, raw tab/window/document ID, raw binding token, raw request ID, bearer, origin, endpoint, manifest, or arbitrary error field.

Mutable authority does not make destructive conflict handling acceptable. A named `BEFORE INSERT` pre-conflict guard aborts if `intent_hash` already exists, if another active intent exists for `item_id`, or if a non-null inspect-grant or terminal-receipt ID already exists, before SQLite resolves the primary key, active-item partial index, inspect-hash/terminal-receipt index, `OR REPLACE`, or an UPSERT target. A named `BEFORE UPDATE` guard applies the same tests excluding the row itself; immutable bindings and the no-terminal-exit state machine prevent an update from moving onto another natural key. The schema manifest enumerates these as the complete intent uniqueness set, and adding a later unique index requires extending this guard first. Ordinary `INSERT`, UPSERT, `INSERT OR REPLACE`, and `UPDATE OR REPLACE` collision tests with `recursive_triggers` off and on must preserve the old intent, issued grant, terminal receipt, item, and retention graph byte-for-byte.

### 6.8 `youtube_browser_upload_grants`

The exact physical column order follows; all columns are `NOT NULL` unless explicitly marked nullable:

```text
grant_hash primary key
authority_epoch_id lowercase 64-hex exact current singleton and parent intent
intent_hash
grant_generation positive
owner_scope_hash
item_id
expected_content_revision
canonical_video_id_hash
page_route_class exact standard_watch_v1|shorts_fixture_v1
extension_id_hash
extension_version
panel_binding_hash
policy_decision_id
policy_expires_at
source_delete_by
requester_origin_hash
destination_binding_hash
api_version
route_contract_version
extractor_contract_version
transfer_contract_version
extractor_version
inspect_generation positive
return_route_kind exact item_detail_v1
request_id_hash
request_fingerprint 64-hex
body_sha256
body_bytes 1..2097152
cue_count 1..7200
authority_fingerprint 64-hex
state issued|consumed|revoked|expired
terminal_receipt_id nullable bounded opaque ID
nonreceipt_close_reason nullable exact grant_expired|grant_revoked|intent_cancelled|intent_revoked|intent_expired|issuance_response_lost
nonreceipt_intent_state nullable exact claimed|cancelled|revoked|expired
issued_at
not_before exactly issued_at
expires_at exactly issued_at + 120000 and no later than intent expiry, policy expiry, or source delete-by
intent_expires_at exact copied intent expiry
consumed_at nullable
revoked_at nullable
expired_at nullable
created_at
```

The three context/destination values are domain-separated hashes of server/worker-validated canonical values: the tab/document/video binding token, exact approved `chrome-extension://` requester origin, and the full fixed lab destination binding. The destination binding covers exact scheme, host, effective port, fixed grant/commit/reconciliation route discriminators, and transfer-contract version; origin-only equality cannot authorize a path. The request ID is stored only as a domain-separated hash. `request_fingerprint` and `authority_fingerprint` use the exact tuples in §6.12; neither is caller truth. Extension/extractor/contract tokens are nonempty reviewed ASCII version tokens of at most 128 bytes. The server recomputes request fingerprint, body SHA-256, streamed byte length, normalized cue count, and all canonical bindings at commit; client values are binding aids only.

The raw upload-grant envelope must name the current epoch before its v2 hash is computed. The exact intent FK and named insert guards require equality of epoch, owner, item, expected revision, video, page-route class, extension ID/version, panel binding, policy ID whose immutable row has `blocked_reason IS NULL`, policy expiry, source delete-by, API/route/extractor/transfer contracts, return kind, consumed inspect generation, and unexpired intent. Issuance also reselects the singleton and requires its epoch, the current classifier to remain exactly `weak_metadata_capture`, the exact `no_durable_client_copy_v1` row/hash, and no source/hold; it reparses the active page and repeats the exact standard-watch or synthetic-Shorts predicate. Any epoch/client-contract/classifier/source/hold/route drift denies issuance and discards the in-memory candidate without transfer or a grant; route drift is terminal denial, never caller-supplied equality. Inspect-generation equality is an issuance-trigger/CAS check, not part of the persistent FK, because later inspect generations must not invalidate retained terminal grants. Add `UNIQUE(intent_hash,grant_generation)`, `UNIQUE(intent_hash,request_id_hash)`, and `UNIQUE(authority_fingerprint)`; each insertion increments the per-intent generation with no reuse. A grant can be issued only from `review_pending`, after explicit confirmation, when at least 120,000 ms remains before each of intent expiry, policy expiry, and source delete-by; therefore its lifetime is always exactly 120 seconds and `expires_at <= MIN(intent_expires_at,policy_expires_at,source_delete_by)`. Shape rules are:

- `issued`: all three terminal timestamps, `terminal_receipt_id`, and both nonreceipt closure fields are null;
- `consumed`: only `consumed_at` non-null among terminal timestamps, `issued_at <= consumed_at < expires_at`, one non-null preselected `terminal_receipt_id` whose deferred FK must close in the same transaction, and both nonreceipt closure fields null;
- `revoked`: only `revoked_at` non-null, `revoked_at >= issued_at`, null terminal-receipt ID, and one complete nonreceipt reason/intent-state pair;
- `expired`: only `expired_at` non-null, `expired_at >= expires_at`, null terminal-receipt ID, and one complete nonreceipt reason/intent-state pair. `grant_expired|intent_expired` alone may select grant state `expired`; every other nonreceipt reason selects `revoked`. `intent_cancelled|intent_revoked|intent_expired` requires its matching terminal intent state, while ordinary grant expiry/revocation/response-loss selects `claimed` only when the database clock proves at least 180,000 ms remains for a fresh inspect-plus-grant sequence and otherwise selects terminal `revoked` (or `expired` when the intent itself is due).

Every state transition is one-way from `issued` to exactly one terminal state and clears effective authority. A unique partial index on `intent_hash WHERE state='issued'` permits one issued grant, without consulting the clock. An unexpired issued row blocks issuance. Only an independent cleanup or authorized reissue transaction may change a due row to `expired`; a rejected commit never lazily expires it. Explicit revocation likewise uses a separate transaction while the grant is still live.

The nonreceipt grant/intent lifecycle is trigger-owned and statement-atomic. The sole `AFTER INSERT` grant trigger requires exactly one parent in `review_pending` with the complete copied binding, updates it to `upload_authorized` at `updated_at=grant.issued_at`, and aborts the grant statement unless exactly one row changed; the intent guard rejects that edge without the just-inserted exact issued grant/time. The sole `AFTER UPDATE OF state` grant trigger for `issued -> revoked|expired` validates the complete reason/state/clock precedence above, CAS-updates the exact parent from `upload_authorized` to `nonreceipt_intent_state`, stamps/clears its inspect/terminal fields in the closed shape, and aborts unless exactly one row changed. An intent guard denies every independent exit from `upload_authorized` while an issued grant exists and denies any nonreceipt exit not produced by the exact just-closed grant tuple. The consumed edge is excluded from this trigger: it remains coupled to `committed|terminal_conflict` by the two deferred terminal-receipt relations. Trigger bodies use explicit `RAISE(ABORT)` row-count assertions and do not rely on `recursive_triggers`; partial grant insert/update, partial intent update, wrong reason/target, and forced trigger failure roll back the complete statement/transaction.

A named grant `BEFORE INSERT` pre-conflict guard aborts if `grant_hash` already exists, if `(intent_hash,grant_generation)`, `(intent_hash,request_id_hash)`, `authority_fingerprint`, or a non-null terminal-receipt ID already exists, or if another issued grant exists for the intent, before any conflict algorithm can delete the old authority row. A named `BEFORE UPDATE` guard repeats the complete collision test excluding the row itself; all key/binding/fingerprint fields are immutable, `terminal_receipt_id` may change once from null only on the consumed edge, and only the one-way state/timestamp edge may otherwise change. These are the complete grant uniqueness relations in the schema manifest, and a later unique index cannot land without a corresponding pre-conflict branch. Collision tests cover ordinary insert/update, UPSERT, `INSERT OR REPLACE`, and `UPDATE OR REPLACE` under `recursive_triggers` off/on and prove the intent, old grant, receipt/evidence graph, and item root remain byte-identical.

Intent terminal precedence is atomic: if the intent is due, cleanup closes the issued grant with `nonreceipt_close_reason='intent_expired'` and the trigger terminalizes the intent; no path returns it to a nonterminal state. If the intent remains nonterminal with at least 180,000 ms for a fresh inspect-plus-grant sequence, grant expiry/revocation discards the candidate and the same trigger moves the intent to `claimed`. With less remaining time it selects the exact terminal result above instead of creating unusable authority. Any other intent terminal transition closes an issued grant through that one coupled statement. Issuance-response loss cannot recover a raw secret from its stored hash: reconciliation revokes the unreachable issued grant with `issuance_response_lost` and applies the same precedence. Commit CAS-consumes the exact grant and writes the terminal receipt in one transaction. Commit-response loss returns the existing receipt for the same authority/body/request fingerprint without body/grant retransmission and never mints another grant.

### 6.9 Immutable `extension_capture_requests`

This is the terminal route receipt. It intentionally copies inert intent/grant hashes and does not retain an FK to purgeable mutable authority rows; insert guards validate those rows inside the commit transaction.

The exact physical column order follows; all columns are `NOT NULL` unless explicitly marked nullable:

```text
receipt_id primary key
body_transition_id nullable bounded opaque ID
intent_hash unique
upload_grant_hash unique non-null
grant_generation positive
authority_fingerprint 64-hex
commit_fingerprint nullable 64-hex
owner_scope_hash 64-hex
item_id item FK cascade
expected_content_revision positive
accepted_content_revision nullable positive
held_enrichment_generation nullable positive
canonical_video_id_hash 64-hex
page_route_class exact standard_watch_v1|shorts_fixture_v1
authority_policy_decision_id exact intent/grant policy
authority_policy_expires_at nonnegative epoch
authority_source_delete_by nonnegative epoch
authority_retention_cleanup_budget_ms positive
authority_retention_cleanup_start_by nonnegative epoch
authority_retention_budget_profile_hash 64-hex
source_policy_decision_id nullable exact accepted-source policy
source_owner_scope_hash nullable 64-hex
source_delete_by nullable nonnegative epoch
source_retention_cleanup_budget_ms nullable positive
source_retention_cleanup_start_by nullable nonnegative epoch
source_retention_budget_profile_hash nullable 64-hex
panel_binding_hash 64-hex
extension_id_hash 64-hex
extension_version
requester_origin_hash 64-hex
destination_binding_hash 64-hex
api_version
route_contract_version
extractor_contract_version
transfer_contract_version
extractor_version
inspect_generation positive
return_route_kind exact item_detail_v1
request_id_hash 64-hex
request_fingerprint 64-hex
bound_body_sha256 64-hex
bound_body_bytes 1..2097152
bound_cue_count 1..7200
intent_expires_at
grant_issued_at
grant_not_before
grant_expires_at
transcript_source_id nullable source/item/revision FK
outcome attached|conflict
result_code transcript_attached|existing_transcript_conflict|existing_item_text_conflict|legacy_provenance_conflict|ambiguous_provenance_conflict|existing_note_conflict
http_status 200..599
note_action none|created|duplicate
note_epoch nullable positive
note_generation nullable nonnegative
note_sha256 nullable 64-hex
note_include_in_ai nullable 0|1
text_sha256 nullable 64-hex
integrity_sha256 nullable 64-hex
segment_count nullable nonnegative
total_chars nullable nonnegative
created_at
```

The receipt copies every purgeable grant binding and validity window, including `page_route_class`, and has `UNIQUE(owner_scope_hash,request_id_hash)`. Add `UNIQUE(receipt_id,body_transition_id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,text_sha256)` for attached-body transition evidence, `UNIQUE(receipt_id,intent_hash,item_id,expected_content_revision,owner_scope_hash)` for terminal intent closure, and `UNIQUE(receipt_id,upload_grant_hash,intent_hash,grant_generation,item_id,authority_fingerprint)` for terminal grant closure. The authority policy ID/expiry/delete-by/cleanup budget/start/profile always copy the exact intent policy; the latter three are joined from its immutable row even though they are not raw grant fields. The accepted source policy/owner/deadline/cleanup tuple is non-null only for an attached receipt and binds the newly accepted source authority; every conflict leaves it null. Its insert guard requires the intent and grant to carry this preselected `receipt_id`, the grant to be `consumed`, the intent to be `committed` for attached or `terminal_conflict` for conflict, and all copied bindings/clocks to match. It reparses/revalidates the current page immediately before body selection: `standard_watch_v1` may attach or record an allowlisted post-stream conflict only from the exact watch route, while `shorts_fixture_v1` requires the same packaged no-network test/development fixture predicate; lab Shorts and every embed/mobile/live/alternate route abort with zero consumption or receipt. The two deferred reverse references make consumed/terminal authority without this final receipt uncommittable.

`attached` requires a non-null exact `browser_attachment` body transition, `accepted_content_revision=expected_content_revision+1`, non-null source policy/owner/deadline/cleanup tuple, source/text/integrity/count/total-characters evidence, held generation, and commit fingerprint; the transition selects this receipt ID, item, accepted revision, and body/text hash. Its source policy/owner/deadline/cleanup tuple equals authority, `total_chars` equals the Unicode-scalar count of the canonical source text and canonical provenance fact, its result is exactly `transcript_attached`, and HTTP status is 200. It is selectable only when the writer-lock classifier is still `weak_metadata_capture` and `items.content_revision=expected_content_revision`. Schema 028 emits no new `outcome='duplicate'` receipt: one active intent and one issued grant make a competing same-authority handler the same request transaction, so the first idempotency step returns its already-committed attached or conflict receipt. A request beginning after an active source exists is rejected before inspection or body access; supporting a later same-content receipt requires a separately reviewed multi-intent design.

After a valid bounded stream and exact nonrevision revalidation, a greater current revision may select a durable no-content conflict. `active_source_current` at `items.content_revision>expected_content_revision` maps to `existing_transcript_conflict` regardless of source kind or text equality. At a greater revision, §5.2.1 maps `current_generic_user_text` to `existing_item_text_conflict`, `source_less_strong_content` to `legacy_provenance_conflict`, and `source_less_ambiguous` to `ambiguous_provenance_conflict`. A due current or historical source never reaches conflict receipt creation. A lower revision, `weak_metadata_capture` at a greater revision, any nonweak class at the unchanged expected revision, or `invalid_current_authority_graph` is containment failure and cannot create or consume authority. `conflict` retains the grant's original `expected_content_revision`, has null `accepted_content_revision`, null body-transition ID, null commit fingerprint, and leaves every accepted-source/hold field—including cleanup, hashes, counts, generation, and `total_chars`—null. It uses exactly one of `existing_transcript_conflict|existing_item_text_conflict|legacy_provenance_conflict|ambiguous_provenance_conflict|existing_note_conflict` and has HTTP status 409. Source/source-less provenance conflict classification takes precedence over note classification and does not inspect or mutate note state; `existing_note_conflict` is available only after an otherwise attachable unchanged weak classification. Named checks freeze those outcome/result/revision/status/precedence combinations. A conflict receipt is permitted only after an otherwise exact live grant authenticates; it consumes that grant/intent but changes no item/source/note/derived/job content.

Note shapes are exact: `none` has all note fields null and is required for every conflict receipt; `created` stores the new note epoch/generation/hash with `note_include_in_ai=0`; `duplicate` copies the same normalized existing note epoch/generation/hash and its unchanged AI flag. Named insert guards compare accepted note facts to `item_notes` in the commit transaction, but no persistent FK targets the mutable note row. Later note edits cannot rewrite the receipt, and a recovery note never changes a separately authorized note’s AI policy.

Feature/auth/state/time, destination, requester, extension, panel, request-ID, expected item revision, policy, and retention failures are checked before reading the body and cause zero database mutation or receipt. Actual byte length and body SHA require a bounded stream, and normalized cue count requires strict parsing; mismatches found at those later checks likewise cause zero database mutation or receipt. Once that valid bounded stream exists, revision drift alone follows the writer-lock classifier above rather than retroactively becoming a pre-body authentication failure. A rejected expired-grant commit does not perform lazy expiry; only the independent cleanup/reissue transition in §6.8 may do so.

Same-intent/request/body response loss returns the existing row. A changed request/body binding conflicts without inserting a second row. Updates are always forbidden. Direct delete is forbidden while the parent item exists; item deletion cascades. No transcript, segment text, title, URL, labels, raw identifiers/tokens/origins/endpoints, arbitrary errors, or policy documents are stored; origin fields above are hashes only.

### 6.10 Immutable `transcript_recovery_apply_receipts`

The exact all-non-null physical column order is:

```text
receipt_id primary key
body_transition_id bounded opaque ID
job_id
attempt_id unique
item_id item FK cascade
policy_decision_id exact source policy
policy_expires_at nonnegative epoch
source_delete_by nonnegative epoch
retention_cleanup_budget_ms positive
retention_cleanup_start_by nonnegative epoch
retention_budget_profile_hash 64-hex
claim_token_hash 64-hex
expected_content_revision positive
applied_content_revision exactly expected + 1
transcript_source_id exact source/item/revision FK
text_sha256 64-hex
integrity_sha256 64-hex
segment_count positive
applied_item_body_sha256 64-hex
result_code exact recovery_applied
created_at
```

Composite FKs bind the receipt’s attempt/job/item/expected-revision/claim-hash tuple to the exact terminal attempt, its policy/item/expiry/delete-by/cleanup tuple to the immutable restricted policy, and its source/item/applied-revision/policy/delete-by/cleanup/text/integrity/count tuple to the exact immutable source. Add `UNIQUE(receipt_id,body_transition_id,item_id,applied_content_revision,applied_item_body_sha256)` for the exact `automatic_recovery` body-transition FK. Named insert guards additionally require that transition to select this receipt and exact item/revision/body hash, require the exact success attempt's provider/language/generated/translated/Unicode-scalar character facts to equal the canonical source transcript and recovery provenance, require `now < policy_expires_at <= retention_cleanup_start_by < source_delete_by` under the exact policy/profile, and require SHA-256 of the just-applied metadata-bearing `items.body` to equal the receipt/transition facts. There is no separate duplicate result: reconciliation returns the existing `recovery_applied` row and performs no insert or mutation. Updates are forbidden; direct deletion is forbidden while the item exists. This table contains no transcript, URL, title, raw provider response/error, or raw token.

### 6.11 Exact foreign-key and deletion contract

The item is the sole physical deletion root for durable **content/evidence** rows (body transitions and their body-statement applications, policies, sources, segments, manual evidence seals, generic body-update receipts, holds, attempts, attempt-finalization seals, receipts, and its client-storage contract). Content-free retention obligations, schedule reservations, survivor entries, root applications/seals, retired-ID tombstones, ledger state/outbox, purge receipts, clock state, and backup attestations deliberately survive or replace that cascade under §6.3. Narrow lifecycle cleanup may purge terminal mutable intent/grant authority on the fixed schedules in §3.2; it never deletes durable receipts or current content authority. Every FK below uses `MATCH SIMPLE` and `ON UPDATE RESTRICT`. “Immediate cascade” means `ON DELETE CASCADE NOT DEFERRABLE`; “immediate reference” means `ON DELETE NO ACTION NOT DEFERRABLE`; “deferred reference” means `ON DELETE NO ACTION DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED`. Those are the only three classes used in the table. `RESTRICT` is not used for cross-references because SQLite evaluates it immediately and can break a coherent parallel item cascade.

| Child | Child columns → parent columns | Exact delete/deferral |
|---|---|---|
| `youtube_item_body_transitions` | `item_id` → `items(id)` | immediate cascade |
| `youtube_item_body_transitions` | `(transition_id,item_id,expected_content_revision,applied_content_revision,body_sha256,body_utf16_code_units,applied_extraction_method,applied_capture_quality,applied_extraction_version,created_at)` → exact body-statement application tuple | deferred reference; complete for every transition |
| `youtube_item_body_transition_applications` | `(transition_id,item_id,expected_content_revision,applied_content_revision,body_sha256,body_utf16_code_units,extraction_method,capture_quality,extraction_version,observed_at)` → exact transition application-parent tuple | immediate cascade; inserted only by the body trigger |
| `youtube_item_body_transitions` | `(manual_evidence_seal_id,transition_id,item_id,applied_content_revision,body_sha256)` → exact manual-seal body-transition key | deferred reference; complete only for `manual_source` |
| `youtube_item_body_transitions` | `(browser_receipt_id,transition_id,item_id,applied_content_revision,body_sha256)` → exact attached browser-receipt body-transition key | deferred reference; complete only for `browser_attachment` |
| `youtube_item_body_transitions` | `(recovery_receipt_id,transition_id,item_id,applied_content_revision,body_sha256)` → exact recovery-receipt body-transition key | deferred reference; complete only for `automatic_recovery` |
| `youtube_item_body_transitions` | `(generic_receipt_id,transition_id,item_id,applied_content_revision,body_sha256)` → exact generic-receipt body-transition key | deferred reference; complete only for `generic_user_text` |
| `youtube_generic_body_update_receipts` | `item_id` → `items(id)` | immediate cascade |
| `youtube_generic_body_update_receipts` | `(body_transition_id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,body_sha256,receipt_id)` → exact generic body-transition parent key | immediate cascade |
| `capture_policy_decisions` | `item_id` → `items(id)` | immediate cascade; nullable only for preserved unreferenced legacy policies |

The upload-grant intent tuple is exactly `(intent_hash,authority_epoch_id,owner_scope_hash,item_id,expected_content_revision,canonical_video_id_hash,page_route_class,policy_decision_id,policy_expires_at,source_delete_by,extension_id_hash,extension_version,api_version,route_contract_version,extractor_contract_version,transfer_contract_version,return_route_kind,panel_binding_hash,intent_expires_at)`, where child `intent_expires_at` references parent `expires_at`. Required parent keys are:

- policy `UNIQUE(id,item_id)`, the owner/deadline/timing keys enumerated in §6.2, and the exact browser authority-window key;
- body transition `UNIQUE(transition_id,item_id,expected_content_revision,applied_content_revision,body_sha256,body_utf16_code_units,applied_extraction_method,applied_capture_quality,applied_extraction_version,created_at)`, `UNIQUE(transition_id,item_id,applied_content_revision,body_sha256,manual_evidence_seal_id)`, `UNIQUE(transition_id,item_id,applied_content_revision,body_sha256,browser_receipt_id)`, `UNIQUE(transition_id,item_id,applied_content_revision,body_sha256,recovery_receipt_id)`, `UNIQUE(transition_id,item_id,applied_content_revision,body_sha256,generic_receipt_id)`, and `UNIQUE(item_id,applied_content_revision)`;
- body-statement application `UNIQUE(transition_id,item_id,expected_content_revision,applied_content_revision,body_sha256,body_utf16_code_units,extraction_method,capture_quality,extraction_version,observed_at)`;
- source `UNIQUE(id,item_id)`, `UNIQUE(id,item_id,accepted_content_revision)`, and every authority/deadline/evidence key enumerated in §6.3;
- retention obligation `UNIQUE(obligation_id,item_id,source_delete_by)`; reservation `UNIQUE(item_id,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_cleanup_start_by,source_delete_by,retention_budget_profile_hash)`; exact live source/browser-receipt/recovery-receipt admission parent keys; root application `UNIQUE(application_id,item_id)` plus `root_application_exact_key` and `root_seal_exact_key` from §6.3.1; the obligation parent `UNIQUE(seal_id,application_id,item_id,earliest_cleanup_start_by,earliest_source_delete_by,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_budget_profile_hash,obligation_set_hash,obligation_count,artifact_manifest_hash,artifact_manifest_count,survivor_manifest_hash,survivor_manifest_count,metadata_cache_scope_hash,client_storage_contract_hash,db_root_purged_at)`; root-seal retirement parent `UNIQUE(seal_id,item_id,earliest_cleanup_start_by,earliest_source_delete_by,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_budget_profile_hash,obligation_set_hash,obligation_count,client_storage_contract_hash,retirement_authorized_at,db_root_purged_at)`; retired ID `UNIQUE(item_id,retired_tuple_hash)`; both ledger-entry parent keys `UNIQUE(sequence,item_id,retired_tuple_hash,entry_hash)` and `UNIQUE(sequence,item_id,earliest_cleanup_start_by,earliest_source_delete_by,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_budget_profile_hash,obligation_set_hash,obligation_count,client_storage_contract_hash,retired_at,retired_tuple_hash,entry_hash)`; ledger-outbox origin parent `UNIQUE(root_purge_application_id,item_id)` and terminal parent `UNIQUE(ledger_sequence,item_id,retired_tuple_hash,ledger_entry_hash)`; the full ordered scrub-application parent key, planned-item primary/exact stable-tuple parent keys, and copy retired-item exact parent key including `local_root_absent_at` from §6.3.2; and purge receipt `UNIQUE(item_id)` plus `UNIQUE(receipt_id,item_id,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_budget_profile_hash,earliest_cleanup_start_by,earliest_source_delete_by,obligation_set_hash,obligation_count)`;
- manual source seal `UNIQUE(seal_id,transcript_source_id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,policy_decision_id,source_kind,text_sha256,integrity_sha256,segment_count)` plus `UNIQUE(seal_id,body_transition_id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,body_sha256)`;
- generic body-update receipt `UNIQUE(receipt_id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,extraction_method,capture_quality)`, `UNIQUE(receipt_id,body_transition_id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,body_sha256)`, and `UNIQUE(item_id,accepted_content_revision)`;
- attached browser receipt `UNIQUE(receipt_id,body_transition_id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,text_sha256)` plus `UNIQUE(receipt_id,item_id,accepted_content_revision,source_policy_decision_id,source_delete_by,source_retention_cleanup_budget_ms,source_retention_cleanup_start_by,source_retention_budget_profile_hash,transcript_source_id)`, terminal-intent `UNIQUE(receipt_id,intent_hash,item_id,expected_content_revision,owner_scope_hash)`, terminal-grant `UNIQUE(receipt_id,upload_grant_hash,intent_hash,grant_generation,item_id,authority_fingerprint)`, and recovery receipt `UNIQUE(receipt_id,body_transition_id,item_id,applied_content_revision,applied_item_body_sha256)` plus `UNIQUE(receipt_id,item_id,applied_content_revision,policy_decision_id,source_delete_by,retention_cleanup_budget_ms,retention_cleanup_start_by,retention_budget_profile_hash,transcript_source_id)`;
- attempt-finalization seal `UNIQUE(seal_id,attempt_id,job_id,item_id,policy_decision_id,expected_content_revision,claim_token_hash)`;
- transcript job `UNIQUE(id,item_id)`; policy equality is an insertion/transition snapshot guard rather than a historical attempt→mutable-job FK;
- attempt `UNIQUE(id,job_id,item_id)`, `UNIQUE(id,job_id,item_id,policy_decision_id)`, `UNIQUE(id,job_id,item_id,policy_decision_id,expected_content_revision,claim_token_hash)`, and claim-idempotency `UNIQUE(job_id,expected_content_revision,claim_token_hash)`;
- hold `UNIQUE(item_id,transcript_source_id,expected_content_revision,held_enrichment_generation,policy_decision_id,owner_scope_hash,source_delete_by)`;
- authority epoch `PRIMARY KEY(singleton)` plus `UNIQUE(authority_epoch_id)`; permanent inspect issuance `PRIMARY KEY(inspect_grant_hash)` plus `UNIQUE(authority_epoch_id,intent_hash,inspect_generation)`; and intent `UNIQUE` over the exact tuple above, unique active-item partial key, unique non-null live inspect-grant hash, and unique non-null terminal-receipt ID;
- grant `UNIQUE(intent_hash,grant_generation)`, `UNIQUE(intent_hash,request_id_hash)`, `UNIQUE(authority_fingerprint)`, unique non-null terminal-receipt ID, and unique issued-intent partial key.

Every parent-key column is immutable once it can have a child; the sole earlier transition is the intent `panel_binding_hash` null-to-value `created -> claimed` edge in §6.7, which is allowed only while no child exists and is immutable thereafter. Because receipt `item_id` is always non-null and SQLite does not implement `MATCH FULL`, named transition/receipt/retention shape guards supply `MATCH FULL` semantics for every nullable polymorphic tuple: exactly one terminal-evidence family is complete on a body transition and pending obligation; a conflict receipt requires all accepted-source/hold/transition fields null; attached requires every source policy/owner/deadline/evidence/hold/transition field non-null; each retired-ID row selects exactly one live-root, backup-scrub, or external-import proof; and each outbox selects exactly one live-root or backup-scrub origin with the state/nullness frozen above. After those shapes hold, every base, deadline, exact-owner, hold, admission, retention-obligation, root-seal, outbox-origin, and body-transition FK applies. The guards also require every copied value, including nullness, to equal its parent row; no nullable supplemental FK is proof by itself. Restricted item deletion is admitted only by the fresh root application, must remove the complete vector-first content/evidence/cache/client/survivor graph, and atomically moves all obligations from `artifacts_purged` to `db_root_purged`, creates the retired-ID/outbox row, and closes the root seal with an empty `foreign_key_check`. Named delete guards deny direct deletion of a body transition, `transcript_jobs`, policy, source, segment, manual evidence seal, attempt, attempt-finalization seal, hold, or immutable receipt while its item remains yet permit the proven parent-item cascade; permanent inspect issuances deny every update/delete regardless of item state; `embedding_jobs` deletion remains the mandatory §6.1.2 reset operation. Retention obligations, schedule reservations, survivor entries, root applications/seals, retired IDs, ledger state/outbox, purge receipts, clock state, and attestations are never directly deletable on a live database; only the attested disposable-clone scrub procedure in §6.3.2 may temporarily remove and exactly recreate descriptor-listed guards. Root-item, durable-table, chunk/bridge, mutable-intent, mutable-grant, and permanent-inspect-issuance pre-conflict guards reject every primary/natural/partial-unique collision that could otherwise make `INSERT OR REPLACE` or `UPDATE OR REPLACE` delete an existing row before a delete trigger runs. Schema 028 does not depend on `PRAGMA recursive_triggers`, and application writers never use `REPLACE` for authority/evidence. Browser retention uses the durable multiphase contract above.

Revision/generation/claim relations to mutable singleton rows deliberately use named insert/transition triggers plus service CAS, not FKs: queue/intent revision to `items.content_revision`; grant inspect generation to the mutable intent inspect generation; hold/embedding generation to the current enrichment job; attempt revision/claim to a transcript job whose claim is later cleared; and `items.current_applied_enrichment_generation` to the mutable enrichment job. The job/attempt, attempt/finalization-seal, source/manual-seal, and body-transition/terminal-evidence cycles are valid only with the deferred `NO ACTION` reverse FKs above and immediate child-to-parent cascades; composite `SET NULL` is forbidden because it would also try to null non-null ownership/evidence columns.

### 6.12 Canonical hash and fingerprint framing

Schema 028's body/evidence/clock guards use six native deterministic SQLite scalars: `sha256_utf8(value)`, `utf16_code_units(value)`, `transcript_integrity_v1_json(text_sha256,segment_count,ordered_segments_json)`, the exact eleven-argument `youtube_legacy_body_class_v1(...)` from §5.2.1, `trusted_time_proof_v1(proof_kind,reanchor_counter,boot_id_hash,monotonic_anchor_ms,raw_wall_observed_ms,reference_lower_ms,reference_upper_ms,proof_max_error_ms,provider_set_hash,operator_artifact_hash,proof_at)`, and `typed_sha256_v1_json(domain,ordered_values_json)`. Every migration, application, script, backup/restore CLI, maintenance unit, and test connection registers all six as `SQLITE_UTF8|SQLITE_DETERMINISTIC|SQLITE_INNOCUOUS` before schema-028 SQL is prepared. `sha256_utf8` accepts only a non-null SQLite `TEXT` value, hashes its exact UTF-8 bytes with SHA-256, and returns lowercase 64-hex; other storage classes abort. `utf16_code_units` likewise accepts only non-null `TEXT` and returns the exact nonnegative JavaScript `String.length` count, including surrogate-pair and embedded-NUL behavior without normalization. `transcript_integrity_v1_json` accepts a lowercase 64-hex source-text digest, a nonnegative safe integer count, and a JSON array constructed from stored rows in ascending `idx` with exact objects `{idx,start_ms,end_ms,text}`; it rejects extra/missing keys, wrong JSON types, count/index disagreement, and unsafe integers, then returns the exact typed digest from §6.3. The empty array is the zero-segment representation. `trusted_time_proof_v1` enforces the exact storage classes, bounds, automatic/operator null shape, midpoint/error equalities, and typed field order in §6.3.1, then returns lowercase 64-hex.

`typed_sha256_v1_json` is the sole general trigger bridge for §6.12 typed hashes. Its second argument is strict JSON transport only: an array whose entries are exactly one of `["n"]`, `["t",text]`, `["b",lowercase-even-length-hex]`, `["d",lowercase-64-hex]`, `["i",canonical-signed-int64-decimal]`, or `["o",boolean]`. Native code decodes those entries directly into the frame registry below; it never treats arbitrary digest/BLOB bytes as a JavaScript UTF-8 string. Unknown or extra tuple members, invalid UTF-8, noncanonical integer text, signed-int64 overflow, malformed/uppercase hex, a wrong digest length, an unknown domain, an unapproved field count, or an input above that domain's frozen framed-byte maximum aborts. The closed domain/arity/maximum registry and its hash are part of the scalar capability. Connection startup fails closed unless all six functions match the content-hashed conformance vectors. The migration runner registers and probes all six before preflight/DDL, and the schema capability requires those probes plus the exact trigger/view SQL that invokes them. An absent, replaced, wrongly flagged, locale-sensitive, order-insensitive, normalization-performing, or JavaScript-string-mediated scalar is incompatible, never a reason to skip a guard.

#### 6.12.1 Frozen native SQLite authority runtime

The current stock `better-sqlite3@11.10.0` host cannot satisfy this schema: its public API does not expose `SQLITE_INNOCUOUS`, sealed `sqlite3_set_authorizer` roles, or irreversible `SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DEFENSIVE`; `unsafeMode(true)` can disable defensive mode, `loadExtension` can dynamically add code, and JavaScript callbacks are not an acceptable binary transport for arbitrary raw digest bytes. Stock `sqlite-vec@0.1.9` is also incompatible: its `vec0` module never calls `sqlite3_vtab_config(...,SQLITE_VTAB_INNOCUOUS)`, so with `trusted_schema=OFF` the §6.1.2 `BEFORE DELETE` guards cannot read `chunks_vec` and fail with `SQLITE_ERROR: unsafe use of virtual table "chunks_vec"`.

Schema 028 therefore requires two repository-owned source forks that build into **one** native artifact:

- `@ai-brain/better-sqlite3-authority@11.10.0-brain.1`, derived from upstream tag `v11.10.0` commit `335ccad4f0d24d4377eed1118bea5daec0b61647` and npm tarball SHA-256 `71e2d66a44dafc35649ff82742cf4908557fc31841108d19703b74b7065c0293`, carrying better-sqlite3's checked-in, option-generated SQLite 3.49.2 amalgamation—not SQLite.org's stock pre-generated amalgamation. The checked-in source is reproduced byte-for-byte from SQLite.org `sqlite-src-3490200.zip` SHA-256 `c3101978244669a43bc09f44fa21e47a4e25cdf440f1829e9eff176b9a477862`, manifest UUID `17144570b0d96ae63cd6f3edca39e27ebd74925252bbaf6723bcb2f6b4861fb1`, by upstream `deps/download.sh` SHA-256 `5de8af6e9f41fc1b5f6d280cdc55d62c2a210faf722c7e529b6b3427be7c80a8` with its frozen options including `SQLITE_ENABLE_UPDATE_DELETE_LIMIT`; its source ID is `2025-05-07 10:39:52 17144570b0d96ae63cd6f3edca39e27ebd74925252bbaf6723bcb2f6b4861fb1` and its checked-in `sqlite3.c` SHA-256 is `e80215754ac6cfaefe272342efd581b6dbefdbdc21f96bd83fcb698bee9d36a5`. Shipped `deps/patches/1208.patch` SHA-256 `5d1f22d69fee3551b70a7b7b9b14be43fcfd04dbc8683233667abfa75905011a` is already represented in SQLite 3.49.2 and makes no byte change in this generation; and
- source-only `@ai-brain/sqlite-vec-authority@0.1.9-brain.1`, derived from upstream tag `v0.1.9` commit `e9f598abfa0c06b328d8fe5da9c3760cce74be10`, release amalgamation tar.gz SHA-256 `3acd67cb4aff080c7050926fd3cf8227905fe5b7ee3829d8ee5024ab1283cf61`, and upstream `sqlite-vec.c` SHA-256 `ba081a47fa02eadc3cf6b16c314b695b84081269349aac722b4efa338fe8fd85`.

For negative provenance, SQLite.org's separate `sqlite-amalgamation-3490200.zip` SHA-256 `921fc725517a694df7df38a2a3dfede6684024b5788d9de464187c612afb5918` contains stock pre-generated `sqlite3.c` SHA-256 `a891421dc9eb944bfa696458fa55e2676d6187378632563126b0d98c34c7ad07`. It has the same version/source ID but omits the option-regenerated parser capability and is **not** the better-sqlite3 checked-in build input. Substituting either file for the other is an attestation failure.

The sqlite-vec source is compiled with `SQLITE_CORE` and `SQLITE_VEC_OMIT_FS` into the same better-sqlite3-authority `.node` and directly initialized through checked `sqlite3_vec_init` on every owning `sqlite3*`. No sqlite-vec dylib/so/dll, `sqlite3_load_extension`, companion addon recovering a private handle, second SQLite library, mixed stock/fork handle, independently exposed or unsealed handle, or runtime module loader exists. The only deliberate simultaneous-handle case is the exact role-bound online-backup source/destination pair below; both handles come from this same artifact, use distinct fork-private unregistered connection-local VFS wrappers, and neither handle or wrapper is accessible to caller SQL/JavaScript outside its closed role. The repository patch makes both `vec0` `xCreate` and `xConnect` reach one common path that calls `sqlite3_vtab_config(db,SQLITE_VTAB_INNOCUOUS)` **before** `sqlite3_declare_vtab`, checks the return code, and fails closed on any non-`SQLITE_OK`.

The authority build does not mark an unrestricted upstream module innocuous. Its registered `vec0` accepts only the attested main-schema object `main.chunks_vec` with exact constructor `embedding float[768]`; it rejects a temp/attached/other table name, another schema, column, dimension, option, constructor token, rename, or filesystem module. SQLite 3.49.2 has no `SQLITE_PREPARE_FROM_DDL`, so retaining that runtime additionally requires the fixed constructor/name restriction, an audit of every schema-derived internal prepare/shadow statement, and second-layer hashes for the complete exact vec0 shadow schema. A generic innocuous vec0 requires a separately reviewed SQLite upgrade/backport and conversion of every schema-derived internal prepare; it cannot be substituted under this package version.

First-layer vec0 behavior is proven only in a separate packaged native capability-test process against a fresh in-memory main database whose sole virtual object is the exact permitted `main.chunks_vec`. That test exercises both `xCreate` and close/reopen `xConnect` with trusted schema off, and its executable/source/results hash enters the first-layer attestation. It is not a production-connection startup probe, cannot create a temp/attached surrogate, and cannot satisfy any S28 schema gate. Schema-bound vec0/shadow/trigger behavior is proven later on a disposable exact-S28 database and enters the second layer. A production startup verifies those signed build/test artifacts plus its current native and raw-schema hashes; it does not prepare or execute real S28 schema code before the second layer passes and does not mutate a live database merely to self-test.

Connection policy is native, role-bound, and immutable. The only public constructor is `openAuthorityDatabase(role, expectedCapability)` with the closed public role enum `ordinary_v1|migration_v1|maintenance_v1|immutable_verify_v1|ledger_import_v1|backup_copy_source_v1|backup_plan_v1|backup_attestation_v1|backup_scrub_v1`. The native internal registry additionally contains `backup_copy_destination_v1`, but that token is rejected by every JavaScript/public constructor and can be created solely inside the source role's one-shot `backupToVolatileClone(destinationCapability)` operation; neither the destination connection nor any wrapper/pointer for it is ever returned to JavaScript.

The fork adds one internal pointer-bearing primitive, `ai_brain_sqlite3_open_v2_private_vfs_v1(role,connection_open_flags,main_vfs_open_flags,opaque_vfs,opaque_filename_token,&db)`. It never accepts a VFS name, URI, path, or caller-selected configuration. The primitive forks `openDatabase()` before `sqlite3ParseUri()` and is the sole copy-pair open path. `connection_open_flags` and `main_vfs_open_flags` are separate immutable native values, not two aliases for one caller word. For `backup_copy_source_v1` they are exactly `READONLY|NOFOLLOW|PRIVATECACHE|EXRESCODE` and `READONLY|MAIN_DB|NOFOLLOW`; for `backup_copy_destination_v1` they are exactly `READWRITE|NOFOLLOW|PRIVATECACHE|EXRESCODE` and `READWRITE|MAIN_DB|NOFOLLOW`. The primitive rejects every other pair and verifies under an exact constant mask that `main_vfs_open_flags=(connection_open_flags & (READONLY|READWRITE|NOFOLLOW))|MAIN_DB`; `PRIVATECACHE|EXRESCODE` are connection policy only and can never reach Pager or `xOpen`. Each wrapper instance is immutably bound to the reservation ID, writer generation, outer reservation role, internal connection role, exact retained file and supervisor/broker-controlled locked-directory identities, canonical fixed basename set, mount/profile/capability hashes, and its closed surface-operation matrix; a VFS name, URI parameter, alias, caller path, raw FD, directory FD, pointer, or replacement API is never exposed.

After `sqlite3` allocation, stock default-flag initialization, mutex creation, and `aDb` initialization, but before `sqlite3BtreeOpen()`, `sqlite3PagerOpen()`, `sqlite3PagerReadFileheader()`, any compiled-in initializer, any automatic-extension callback, or any caller-visible handle, the primitive calls the attested private helper `ai_brain_apply_pre_btree_role_v1(db,role)`. For both copy roles that helper atomically installs:

- `SQLITE_DBCONFIG_TRUSTED_SCHEMA=0`;
- `SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DEFENSIVE=1`;
- `SQLITE_DBCONFIG_ENABLE_LOAD_EXTENSION=0`;
- `SQLITE_DBCONFIG_NO_CKPT_ON_CLOSE=1`;
- private-cache-only mode, mmap limit zero, a null busy handler, and zero lock timeout; and
- the immutable connection role, opaque `sqlite3_vfs*`, and fixed opaque filename token.

The helper writes one native order marker, `pre_btree_role_configured_v1`, before Btree/Pager construction. The private open bypasses `sqlite3ParseUri()`, `sqlite3_vfs_find()`, named/default VFS selection, and URI parsing; constructs only the fixed opaque SQLite filename object; stores the exact connection-only word in `db->openFlags`; and calls `sqlite3BtreeOpen()` with Btree flags zero and the separately validated exact `main_vfs_open_flags` word. No stock `flags|SQLITE_OPEN_MAIN_DB` expression is used on this branch. The private wrapper records the exact word received by its first main-file `xOpen`, and the primitive rejects unless that requested word equals `main_vfs_open_flags` and the wrapper's returned `pOutFlags` equals the role's exact actual-main word (`READONLY|MAIN_DB` for source, `READWRITE|MAIN_DB` for destination). Failure after allocation closes through the same role-bound VFS while `NO_CKPT_ON_CLOSE` remains set.

The copy-pair primitive never calls `sqlite3AutoLoadExtensions()`, regardless of the process-global automatic-extension registry. `sqlite3_auto_extension()`, `sqlite3_cancel_auto_extension()`, and `sqlite3_reset_auto_extension()` are not exposed by the addon or any role API. A first-layer native canary registers an automatic extension through a test-only C entry point and proves that neither copy-role open invokes it.

After Btree/Pager construction and the initial header read, initializer order is exactly:

1. `sqlite3RegisterPerConnectionBuiltinFunctions`;
2. the frozen ordered `sqlite3BuiltinExtensions` array;
3. direct `sqlite3_vec_init` for the restricted vec0 module;
4. the six deterministic scalars;
5. the two unarmed verifier functions;
6. actual null `db->busyHandler` function/argument, zero busy-invocation count, and zero-lock-timeout readback;
7. authorizer/function/module/VFS seal; and
8. pre-return dbconfig and role readback.

The ordered initializer symbol/hash registry and each return code are first-layer-attested. No initializer may prepare schema SQL, start a transaction, open WAL/SHM/journal/temp, replace the VFS, change a dbconfig, or register an automatic extension. Any failure destroys the unopened role object and returns no handle.

The initial `NO_CKPT_ON_CLOSE` evidence is not a post-open inference. It consists of the `pre_btree_role_configured_v1` order marker plus a true readback after initialization. The second readback occurs after `sqlite3_backup_finish()` and immediately before close. Both markers, both readbacks, the private-open implementation hash, the automatic-extension suppression branch, and the ordered initializer registry enter `native_authority_runtime_attestation_v1`. Non-copy roles use the same immutable policy registry and ordered post-Btree initializer sequence, but only the sealed private copy primitive owns the pre-Btree VFS pointer/token branch and `NO_CKPT_ON_CLOSE` marker.

There is no arbitrary JavaScript authorizer or role change. `unsafeMode`, `loadExtension`, `setDefensive(false)`, `setTrustedSchema(true)`, SQL/host extension loading, runtime module/function/VFS replacement, global or URI-selected VFS registration, `PRAGMA trusted_schema=ON`, `PRAGMA writable_schema`, `ATTACH`, and `DETACH` are removed or hard-fail on ordinary, migration, maintenance, immutable, copy, and scrub roles; readback remains defensive 1/trusted 0/load-extension 0, and both copy handles additionally remain no-checkpoint-on-close 1. `ordinary_v1` and `maintenance_v1` deny schema mutation. For migration 028, `migration_v1` has exactly two non-interchangeable capability branches: the pre-schema `schema_027_to_028_transition_capability_attestation_v1` branch admits only the exact S27 preflight/scalar probes and one ordered 027→028 immediate migration manifest, while the S28 branch admits only the exact second-layer-attested ledger verification/no-op statement. Any later S28-based migration requires its own separately reviewed transition capability; this branch grants no generic schema mutation. Neither branch may prepare a statement from the other, change branch on one handle, or fall back after a mismatch. `backup_scrub_v1` admits only the exact verified-disposable-clone guard/epoch/VACUUM statement manifest. Multi-statement `exec` and automatic reprepare remain inside the same native authorizer; no SQL text path bypasses it.

`backup_copy_source_v1` prepares no caller SQL and exposes only one one-shot `backupToVolatileClone`. Before any SQLite source open, the source descriptor broker's retained locked-parent identity/capability and fixed main basename produce a frozen no-follow companion manifest; the native worker receives only the sealed source leaf descriptor and opaque broker capability, never that parent dirfd. The main file must equal the retained regular read-only inode with link count one and exact owner/mode. **Any** `<main>-journal` directory entry—zero length, nonzero, valid-looking, malformed, regular, directory, symlink, or hardlink—rejects as `source_journal_present` before SQLite source open or backup init; the copy role performs no hotness parse, recovery, open, or delete. This source role is deliberately WAL-only: exact existing regular `<main>-wal` and `<main>-shm` leaves are both mandatory, and their type/device/inode/link count/owner/mode are frozen. Rollback-journal mode, an absent companion, a zero/uninitialized SHM, or an uninitializable read-only WAL/SHM is `online_backup_source_mode_unsupported` and rejects before reservation bind or destination creation rather than falling through to an implicit non-WAL snapshot contract. Neither companion may be created.

The source connection is opened only through its opaque connection-local VFS with exact connection flags `SQLITE_OPEN_READONLY|SQLITE_OPEN_NOFOLLOW|SQLITE_OPEN_PRIVATECACHE|SQLITE_OPEN_EXRESCODE`—never `READWRITE`, `CREATE`, URI interpretation, shared cache, or a named/caller-selected VFS. The separately masked main-file VFS request is exactly `SQLITE_OPEN_READONLY|SQLITE_OPEN_MAIN_DB|SQLITE_OPEN_NOFOLLOW`, and its exact returned flags are `SQLITE_OPEN_READONLY|SQLITE_OPEN_MAIN_DB`; neither `PRIVATECACHE` nor `EXRESCODE` reaches the wrapper. That wrapper recognizes only the fixed main/journal/WAL/SHM tokens and resolves leaves through descriptor-broker operations rooted in locked directories. Main and an existing WAL are read-only/no-create. Pinned SQLite requests the WAL through `xOpen(READWRITE|CREATE|WAL)`, which the source wrapper downgrades without a create/write syscall: it records `forced_readonly_open_v1`, opens only the frozen existing WAL inode with `O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC`, and returns actual `READONLY|WAL` flags. A missing/replaced required WAL propagates exact `SQLITE_CANTOPEN` from that `xOpen`; it is never relabeled as a SHM error. SQLite never calls `xOpen()` for SHM; it reaches SHM only through the main handle's `xShmMap|xShmLock|xShmBarrier|xShmUnmap` methods. A non-null read-only SHM mapping with direct `SQLITE_READONLY` follows the pinned accepted read-only path; inability to establish that mapping returns exact `SQLITE_READONLY_CANTINIT` and is terminal for the private source role. An absent leaf is never created.

A journal was required absent at preflight; if one appears in a race, the wrapper may observe/read only that exact leaf read-only/no-create for drift classification and never recovery. The source allowlist is limited to metadata-only `xAccess` for the fixed tokens and the complete normative method/surface rows below. SHM forbids `fchown`, initialization, extension, and writable mapping. `xFullPathname` accepts only the one opaque fixed-main token and returns the wrapper's non-path internal token; dynamic-library and system-call replacement methods hard-fail. Across main, journal, WAL, SHM, temp, subjournal, transient, and super-journal surfaces the wrapper denies and counts every source-role `xWrite`, `xTruncate`, `xSync`, `xDelete`, non-downgradable read-write/create `xOpen`, writable/extend SHM operation, mutating file control, ownership change, alias, and unlisted token. The pinned core fork checks the private source-role marker immediately after `nPage==0 && !jrnlOpen` becomes true inside `hasHotJournal()` and returns exact `SQLITE_READONLY_ROLLBACK` **before** `pagerLockDb(...,RESERVED_LOCK)` and therefore before `xDelete`; that source hunk/hash is first-layer-attested. The VFS denies remain defense in depth but a raced zero-page journal produces no RESERVED-lock or delete trace entry. If any raced journal reaches hot-journal detection after non-null backup init, the exact first or next unmasked step result is `SQLITE_READONLY_ROLLBACK`; the owner performs the sole finish, closes/audits, and fences/destroys without recovery. Any journal appearance, companion namespace/type/identity/link/owner/mode drift, denied mutation attempt other than the exact recorded WAL downgrade, or close-time side effect is terminal `source_surface_drift`; when detected after non-null init it still owes the one finish.

An ordinary concurrent source write is tolerated only through `source-writer-generation-registry-v1`, a content-free volatile service owned by the same isolated supervisor but serialized independently from reservation mutations. This tolerance additionally requires a first-layer **exclusive-source-access capability**: kernel-enforced identity/sandbox/descriptor-broker policy proves that every process able to open or retain a writable main/WAL/SHM/journal descriptor is the sealed native authority broker, application/worker identities have neither raw parent-directory authority nor an unmediated writable descriptor, and every broker connection is born in the immutable registered-writer wrapper. A registry `begin_v1` successor must be installed and acknowledged before the broker delivers any writable main/WAL/SHM/journal descriptor or directory capability and before SQLite open, Btree/Pager/WAL initialization, recovery, checkpoint, or transaction code can run. Thus even a pre-transaction WAL-index recovery publication belongs to one active generation. Repository discipline, command hashes, advisory locks, and timestamps are not substitutes. If a packaged target cannot prove this boundary continuously, online backup is unsupported and no destination byte is created.

For each source identity the sole supervisor owns one complete canonical registry record with this fixed header order:

```text
contract,boot_id_hash,source_file_identity_sha256,
registry_revision,next_writer_generation,event_count,active_event_count,
writer_begin_freeze_state,writer_begin_freeze_install_transition_id|null,
writer_begin_freeze_reservation_id|null,
writer_begin_freeze_reservation_writer_generation|null,
writer_begin_freeze_terminal_copy_snapshot_sha256|null,
last_transition_id,last_transition_request_sha256,
transition_receipt_count,transition_receipts[transition_receipt_count],
updated_boottime_ms,
events[event_count] in strictly increasing writer_generation,
record_sha256
```

Each event has this fixed order:

```text
event_state,source_file_identity_sha256,native_writer_role,writer_command_hash,
writer_uid,writer_pid,writer_start_ticks,writer_pgid,writer_pgid_start_ticks,
writer_session_id_hash,writer_cgroup_hash,writer_generation,connection_capability_hash,
begin_transition_id,begin_transition_request_sha256,begin_registry_revision,begin_boottime_ms,
end_transition_id,end_transition_request_sha256,end_registry_revision,end_boottime_ms,
writer_vfs_audit_receipt_bytes,writer_vfs_audit_sha256,abort_reason
```

`contract` is exact `source-writer-generation-registry-v1`; `event_state` is exact `active|clean|aborted`; and `native_writer_role` is exact `ordinary_v1|maintenance_v1|migration_v1`. `writer_begin_freeze_state` is exact `unfrozen_v1|postterminal_v1`. The unfrozen shape has all four following freeze fields null. The postterminal shape has all four non-null: the install transition ID, exact backup reservation ID, that reservation's active `backup_copy_source_v1` writer generation, and the already-minted `terminal_copy_snapshot_sha256`. Each role maps injectively to an attested command hash and connection-capability class. Every identity, role, command, generation, capability, begin-transition/request/revision/clock field is non-null in every event and immutable after begin; `begin_registry_revision` equals the revision of the installed begin successor, and `begin_boottime_ms` equals that request's supervisor-derived sample. Active events have every end field, audit-receipt byte string, audit digest, and abort reason null. The only event edge is active to clean or active to aborted. Clean events have end transition/request/revision/clock, a nonempty bounded `writer_vfs_audit_receipt_bytes` raw-bytes frame, and its recomputed audit digest non-null with null abort reason. Aborted events have end transition/request/revision/clock and exact `supervisor_restart|writer_exit|capability_drift|identity_drift`, with both audit fields null. `end_registry_revision` equals the installed end/abort successor revision and its clock equals that transition's request sample. No closed event changes state or field. Transition IDs, including a freeze-install ID, use the same 26-character lowercase Crockford alphabet as reservation transitions and never repeat on the same boot. Every event's source identity equals the header source identity; every hash is raw32-framed; all counters/clocks are nonnegative signed int64; and `active_event_count` equals the exact number of active events while `event_count` equals the encoded array length.

Each source-registry transition receipt has the fixed order:

```text
transition_id,transition_request_sha256,transition_request_bytes,
retained_predecessor_evidence_bytes|null,
predecessor_registry_revision,predecessor_record_sha256,
successor_registry_revision,successor_core_sha256
```

`transition_request_bytes` is the exact bounded canonical request frame stream defined below; reparsing it must reproduce `transition_id|transition_request_sha256` and every request field. `retained_predecessor_evidence_bytes` is non-null exactly for `freeze_install_v1|freeze_release_v1` and is the complete bounded §6.12 frame stream for that request's named install, ready-release, or fenced-release evidence domain; reparsing it must reproduce the request's `predecessor_evidence_sha256`. It is null for every other source-registry edge. `successor_core_sha256` is the §6.12 typed digest under domain `source-writer-generation-registry-successor-core-v1` over the successor's complete header and event fields except `transition_receipt_count`, `transition_receipts[...]`, and `record_sha256`. Each receipt's immutable digest is the §6.12 typed digest under domain `source-writer-generation-transition-receipt-v1` over its eight fields in the listed order; every reference to an install or release receipt SHA-256 means that raw32 digest. The initialize receipt alone has null predecessor revision/hash; every later receipt's predecessor tuple equals its request CAS. The final receipt's `transition_id|transition_request_sha256` equals the header's `last_transition_id|last_transition_request_sha256`, and its successor revision equals `registry_revision`. Initialization has `transition_receipt_count=1`; every installed begin, clean-end, abort, prune, postterminal-freeze install, or postterminal-freeze release copies the entire receipt prefix byte-for-byte and appends exactly one, so `transition_receipt_count=registry_revision+1`. Receipts and their request/evidence bytes are non-prunable for the same-boot registry lifetime even when their closed event prefix is later removed.

The finite capacity is an admission invariant, not a later best effort. `SOURCE_WRITER_REGISTRY_MAX_SOURCE_COUNT_V1`, `SOURCE_WRITER_REGISTRY_MAX_RECORD_BYTES_V1`, every request/evidence/audit maximum, and the exact `source-writer-terminal-closure-envelope-v1` calculator are first-layer-attested. For a proposed canonical record `R`, that calculator enumerates the finite generated state graph over every ordering in which each active event takes exactly one maximum-sized `clean_end_v1` or `abort_v1` successor and, when `R` is postterminal, the installed freeze takes exactly one maximum-sized ready or fenced `freeze_release_v1` successor. It retains every receipt/request/evidence byte, performs no optional event prune, uses the complete maximum writer audit/publication/mutation receipt for the clean branch, and returns the greatest exact `record_bytes` length plus the greatest simultaneous canonical-plus-sibling byte/inode requirement across those mandatory paths. `begin_v1` may rename only when the closure envelope of its complete proposed successor fits the record maximum and the supervisor has atomically charged the matching dedicated tmpfs byte/inode terminal reserve before acknowledgement. `freeze_install_v1` applies the same predicate to its proposed successor, preserving the already charged terminal reserve for every active event and additionally charging the larger ready/fenced release path. The charge is not a mutable field or guessed journal: it is a deterministic function of the complete canonical record and attested maxima, is reproduced after every supervisor takeover before mutation resumes, and must equal the supervisor's content-free mount-capacity ledger. The packaged mount capability reserves a fixed byte/inode floor equal to the sum of every supported source record's closure envelope plus the maximum simultaneous raw-stage allocation; only the supervisor/stage broker can allocate there, and its admission ledger prevents stage-file creation, another source, an optional prune, or later ordinary admission from consuming a terminal reserve.

`clean_end_v1|abort_v1|freeze_release_v1` are reserved terminal/recovery edges. A valid one consumes or transfers the exact charged reserve and can never be rejected for record-length, request/evidence-byte, tmpfs-byte, or inode capacity; its old canonical plus largest sibling was already included. Every non-capacity authorization, evidence, CAS, filesystem, and hash check still applies. The invariant is recomputed after each installed terminal edge for all remaining active events/freeze, and disagreement with the charged ledger latches containment. Eventual receipt growth may therefore deny a new writable connection, freeze installation, or online backup until reboot, but no admitted event or freeze can become unterminable. Initialization and optional pruning must fit their exact successors; a prune that reduces event bytes never spends another edge's reserve. No implementation may truncate a receipt, delete a used-ID witness, count on future pruning, or treat fail-closed same-boot exhaustion as permission to abandon a mandatory terminal edge.

The initial canonical record is installed under the sole-supervisor OFD lease by an exclusive `initialize_v1` sibling/rename before any snapshot or writer open. It has `registry_revision=0`, `next_writer_generation=1`, both event counts zero, `writer_begin_freeze_state=unfrozen_v1`, all four freeze fields null, no events, and non-null `last_transition_id|last_transition_request_sha256` plus the one initialization receipt. `updated_boottime_ms` is the supervisor sample in the initialize request. `registry_revision` then increments exactly once per installed begin, clean end, abort, prune, freeze install, or freeze release. `last_transition_id|last_transition_request_sha256|updated_boottime_ms` always identify the installed successor's exact transition and final receipt and never move backward. Begin allocates the current `next_writer_generation` and increments it in the same successor, but a begin request observed while `writer_begin_freeze_state=postterminal_v1` rejects before generation allocation, sibling creation, or receipt append. Neither counter decreases, wraps, or reuses a value; exhaustion denies a new writable connection and online backup until a new boot creates a fresh empty tmpfs domain. Retained events form a strictly increasing contiguous suffix of allocated generations. Pruning may remove only a closed oldest event prefix, never an interior event and never a transition receipt, so an unexplained interior generation or receipt gap is malformed.

Let `W` be the §6.12 frames, in order, for `contract,boot_id_hash,source_file_identity_sha256,registry_revision,next_writer_generation,event_count,active_event_count,writer_begin_freeze_state,writer_begin_freeze_install_transition_id,writer_begin_freeze_reservation_id,writer_begin_freeze_reservation_writer_generation,writer_begin_freeze_terminal_copy_snapshot_sha256,last_transition_id,last_transition_request_sha256,transition_receipt_count`, followed immediately by every complete transition receipt's eight fields in append order, then `updated_boottime_ms`, followed by every complete event's fields in the fixed order above and increasing generation. Request and retained-evidence byte strings use raw-bytes frames. Neither `transition_receipts[...]` nor `events[...]` is itself a frame; each count is framed exactly once. Encoding is:

```text
record_sha256 = SHA-256(
  UTF8("source-writer-generation-registry-record-v1") || 0x00 ||
  u64be(byte_length(W)) || W
)

record_bytes = u64be(byte_length(W)+32) || W || raw32(record_sha256)
```

The canonical basename is `src-<source_file_identity_sha256>.v1`; an exclusive sibling is `.src-<source_file_identity_sha256>.<transition_id>.next`. The same sole-supervisor OFD lease protects this namespace, but its mutation queue remains independent from reservation transitions and serializes every writer begin with every freeze install/release. Initialization requires null expected revision/hash and exact empty canonical namespace; every `begin_v1|clean_end_v1|abort_v1|prune_v1|freeze_install_v1|freeze_release_v1` request carries non-null expected revision/hash and a fresh transition ID. Before stale-CAS evaluation the supervisor searches the full non-prunable receipt set: the same ID and request digest returns that receipt plus the current canonical successor/descendant without mutation, while the same ID with another digest rejects. Otherwise it constructs the complete successor, computes its successor-core digest, appends one receipt, writes/fsyncs its mode-0600 sibling, revalidates predecessor/request/capability, and performs one same-directory rename, using no-replace semantics for initialization. Before rename the predecessor or empty namespace is authoritative; afterward the successor and appended receipt are authoritative. Unrenamed siblings are deleted and never promoted, and no fallible success gate follows rename.

Let `V` be the concatenated §6.12 frames in this fixed order:

```text
request_contract,source_file_identity_sha256,transition_id,
expected_registry_revision,expected_record_sha256,requested_edge,request_actor_role,
peer_uid,peer_pid,peer_start_ticks,peer_pgid,peer_pgid_start_ticks,
peer_session_id_hash,peer_cgroup_hash,peer_command_hash,
connection_capability_hash,event_generation,predecessor_evidence_sha256,
requested_at_boottime_ms
```

`transition_request_bytes=V`, stored as one raw-bytes frame in the receipt, and `last_transition_request_sha256=SHA-256(UTF8("source-writer-generation-transition-request-v1") || 0x00 || V)`. `request_contract` is exact `source-writer-generation-transition-v1`; `request_actor_role` is exact `native_writer_v1|copy_producer_v1|supervisor_control_v1|supervisor_recovery_v1`. `initialize_v1` uses `supervisor_control_v1`, alone has null expected revision/hash and event generation, and carries the supervisor peer, exclusive-source broker capability, and initialization evidence. `begin_v1|clean_end_v1` use `native_writer_v1`; begin carries the authenticated writer peer, its non-null capability, the exact next generation, and a before-manifest taken before any writable descriptor/capability delivery, while clean end carries the exact same peer/capability/generation and non-null predecessor evidence equal to the typed digest of the complete `writer_vfs_audit_receipt_bytes` delivered over that authenticated IPC.
