Acknowledgement is the broker's sole prerequisite for descriptor delivery, and the audit receipt bytes become part of the clean successor and record hash. Normal `abort_v1` uses `supervisor_control_v1`; restart abort with exact `supervisor_restart` uses `supervisor_recovery_v1`; both carry the current supervisor peer, victim generation/capability, and kill/reap/reason evidence. `prune_v1` uses `supervisor_control_v1`, carries the supervisor peer, null connection capability, the highest removed oldest-prefix generation, and no-reference evidence. `freeze_install_v1` and ready `freeze_release_v1` use `copy_producer_v1`; fenced `freeze_release_v1` uses `supervisor_recovery_v1`. No other actor/edge/reason combination exists. Every installed edge stores this exact `V`; a digest without the matching canonical bytes rejects before sibling creation.

`freeze_install_v1` is requested by the authenticated `copy_producer_v1` only after one terminal token was returned with `SQLITE_DONE` and the same executor's immediately following public remaining/pagecount reads exactly matched that token. Its non-null connection capability is the reservation-bound copy capability; `event_generation` is the reservation's active `backup_copy_source_v1` writer generation, not a source-writer event generation; and its predecessor evidence is the §6.12 digest under domain `source-writer-postterminal-freeze-install-evidence-v1` over `source_file_identity_sha256,reservation_id,reservation_record_revision,reservation_record_sha256,event_generation,terminal_copy_snapshot_sha256,immediate_counter_verification_sha256,canonical_opener_lock_proof_sha256,copy_finish_by`. The receipt retains that complete domain-specific evidence frame stream. It requires the exact active uncommitted copying reservation, unfrozen source registry, matching source/capability/generation, held canonical opener locks, strict deadline, and the complete terminal-closure capacity charge described above. The installed successor changes only the five freeze-state/header fields, registry revision, last-transition fields, clock, receipt prefix, and record hash; the deterministic external capacity ledger changes atomically under the same mutation serializer and is immediately reproducible from that successor. It allocates no writer generation and changes no event.

Ready `freeze_release_v1` uses `copy_producer_v1` with the same authenticated producer, reservation-bound copy capability, reservation generation, and exact installed freeze transition ID. Fenced `freeze_release_v1` instead uses `supervisor_recovery_v1`: its peer tuple is the current replacement/current sole supervisor derived from the kernel after OFD-lease acquisition, its non-null `connection_capability_hash` is the attested source-registry recovery capability, and `event_generation` is copied from the installed freeze. The original producer identity/capability/generation remain correlation evidence inside the immutable install receipt and reservation lineage; they are not live authority for a recovery release.

The only successful evidence domains are `source-writer-postterminal-freeze-release-ready-evidence-v1`, over the install transition receipt, zero-active post-close cut snapshot digest, complete provisional reservation revision/hash and v3 digest, final worker revalidation digest, canonical opener-lock proof, and strict two-headroom result; or `source-writer-postterminal-freeze-release-fenced-evidence-v1`, over the install transition receipt and its frozen reservation/generation/token tuple, the authoritative reservation fence record revision/hash and exact fence receipt, the current still-detailed reservation revision/hash/state, nullable exact destroy receipt, and source-registry recovery-capability hash. For the fenced shape, current state is exact `fencing` with current record equal to the fence successor and null destroy receipt, or exact `destroyed` whose retained receipt prefix proves that fence predecessor and whose destroy receipt is non-null. The receipt retains the complete selected domain-specific evidence frame stream, so restart can recompute the evidence digest and distinguish ready from fenced without mutable state or inference. The ready edge is legal only after the supervisor installed provisional and the worker re-read that exact canonical successor and completed every final precommit check; the fenced edge is legal only after fencing is authoritative and never refences an existing fencing/destroyed record. Either release successor sets `writer_begin_freeze_state=unfrozen_v1` and all four freeze fields null without changing a writer event or generation. An early, wrong-actor, wrong-capability, wrong-reservation, wrong-generation, wrong-token, wrong-cut, wrong-provisional, wrong-fence/destroy lineage, wrong-lock, stale, or ambiguous release rejects. The immutable ready release transition receipt—not the mutable last-transition fields—is the evidence later bound by `copy_commit_v1`, so a post-release registered writer begin may legitimately append a later source-registry successor. Every peer field and request boottime is kernel-derived by the supervisor; every combination not described here rejects before sibling creation.

Begin acknowledgement is emitted only after its successor rename, and before that acknowledgement the broker cannot deliver a writable descriptor or directory capability, open SQLite, initialize/recover/checkpoint WAL, or enter a write transaction. A freeze-install acknowledgement is likewise emitted only after its successor rename; from that rename until the exact release-successor rename, every begin request rejects before allocation and acknowledgement. Clean-end acknowledgement is emitted only after its successor rename, and the wrapper cannot report clean close earlier. A clean event's `writer_vfs_audit_sha256` is the §6.12 typed digest under domain `source-writer-vfs-audit-v2` over exact order `source_file_identity_sha256,native_writer_role,writer_generation,connection_capability_hash,literal_writer_surface_matrix_sha256,surface_manifest_before_sha256,surface_manifest_after_sha256,operation_trace_sha256,operation_count,denied_operation_count,wal_header_publish_chain_sha256,wal_header_publish_count,transaction_begin_count,commit_count,rollback_count,close_result`. Counts/results are signed-int64; all digests are raw32; success requires zero denied operations, exact transaction outcome accounting, exact `SQLITE_OK` close, and the same fixed manifest encoder used by the copy.

Every completed pinned `walIndexWriteHdr()` invocation in a registered writer contributes one `writer_wal_header_publish_v1` entry. Its §6.12 typed digest uses domain `source-writer-wal-header-publish-entry-v1` and exact order:

```text
writer_generation,publish_sequence,writer_transaction_sequence|null,
publish_reason,published_header_raw48,
copy_to_header_2_sequence,barrier_trace_sequence,copy_to_header_1_sequence,
publish_result
```

`publish_reason` is exact `recovery_v1|wal_restart_v1|commit_v1`; `writer_transaction_sequence` is null only for pre-transaction recovery and otherwise identifies the wrapper's current transaction; `publish_result` is exact `complete_v1`. The two copy sequences are strictly increasing source-core site sequences and the referenced writer-VFS `xShmBarrier` trace lies strictly between them. The fork uses the same final 48-byte local `pWal->hdr` value for the checksum, both mapped copies, and `published_header_raw48`; it records copy 2 only after the actual `header[1]` copy, the barrier only through the real wrapper trace, and copy 1 only after the actual `header[0]` copy. It appends the completed entry only after copy 1. A crash, exception, missing site, reordered copy, extra barrier, different local, or incomplete publication leaves the writer active/aborted and can never yield a clean receipt.

`wal_header_publish_chain_sha256` uses §6.12 domain `source-writer-wal-header-publish-chain-v1`, then `wal_header_publish_count` and each raw32 entry digest in strictly increasing `publish_sequence`; the exact zero-count chain is legal only when that clean writer lifecycle invoked no header publication. The first-layer source-site registry freezes all three current call reasons and rejects an additional, missing, duplicated, or conditionally compiled `walIndexWriteHdr()` call site. A reader-accepted header change can be attributed only to one unique completed publish in the final clean event set as specified below.

`writer_vfs_audit_receipt_bytes` is the byte-exact, content-free preimage needed to reverify that digest after writer exit or supervisor restart. It is a §6.12 frame stream under contract `source-writer-vfs-audit-receipt-v2` containing, in order: every scalar in the audit-digest preimage; the literal fixed surface count and every complete before-manifest entry; the same count and every complete after-manifest entry; the trace-entry count and every v2 trace entry together with its full argument/result payload preimages; the header-publish count and every complete `writer_wal_header_publish_v1` preimage; and a verified-mutation count followed by each normalized allowed mutation in trace/core-site order. Each manifest entry contains only the closed surface token, presence/type/identity/link/owner/mode, size, `mtime`, `ctime`, and whole-surface byte digest where readable. Each normalized VFS mutation contains `mutation_sequence,surface_token,file_identity_sha256,operation_token,offset|null,length|null,input_buffer_sha256|null,pre_size,post_size,pre_mtime,post_mtime,pre_ctime,post_ctime`; a core-observed WAL-index mutation additionally contains its closed store-site token, mapped region index, region-relative byte offset, exact width, before-value digest, after-value digest, and core publication sequence. It contains no raw database byte, path, SQL, pointer, error text, or wall-clock label; the 48-byte WAL header and integer WAL-index values are content-free control metadata. The supervisor parses the complete receipt, independently reproduces every manifest/payload/trace/publish/mutation digest and matrix decision, requires the derived audit hash to equal `writer_vfs_audit_sha256`, and only then installs clean end. An opaque audit hash without this receipt, an over-limit receipt, a missing trace or publish preimage, a noncanonical/overlapping mutation summary, or a manifest transition not exactly explained by the allowed trace/core-store registry rejects before rename.

The receipt byte maximum and complete encoder are first-layer-attested and include the maximum supported count and bytes for complete `writer_wal_header_publish_v1` preimages and their core-observed mapped-store mutations. The exact bytes remain inside the volatile canonical event while any reservation or snapshot can reference its revision interval; event pruning therefore cannot remove attribution evidence early. Retry searches the complete transition-receipt set, not the prunable event array or only the last-transition fields. Same ID/same digest returns its immutable transition receipt plus the current canonical record; same ID/another preimage rejects. The registry stores no path/content and disappears with the same tmpfs/boot boundary.

On supervisor restart, snapshots and new writer begins are unavailable until reconciliation finishes. After old-supervisor kill/reap and OFD-lease acquisition, the replacement validates every canonical record, every transition-receipt prefix, every retained clean-event audit receipt, and removes unrenamed siblings. It freezes the exclusive-source broker, kills/reaps every still-active event using the persisted PID/start/PGID/session/cgroup tuple, and installs one `aborted/supervisor_restart` successor per active event. Every online-backup reservation whose copy interval could include an aborted event is fenced and destroyed; an aborted event can never explain mutation or permit provisional success.

The same reconciliation owns postterminal freeze recovery. A valid `postterminal_v1` header must name one exact same-boot still-detailed reservation with the same source, uncommitted/provisional copy marker, generation, and terminal token through one of three closed pairings. An `active` pairing first installs authoritative fencing. A `fencing` pairing must preserve the frozen copy tuple and expose the exact fence record/receipt and is not refenced. A `destroyed` pairing must preserve that copy marker and fence facts, retain the exact fence/destroy receipt lineage, and likewise is not refenced. The current/replacement sole supervisor then uses the exact `supervisor_recovery_v1` request shape and recovery capability to install the fenced `freeze_release_v1` successor. It never releases an active reservation before fencing, never depends on the dead producer, and never delays physical destruction merely to release the source gate. Pruning is forbidden until this successor is authoritative.

A valid unfrozen registry paired with an active/fencing/destroyed uncommitted/provisional crash-left reservation fences or completes destruction as applicable but never treats that failed clone as sanitizable. An unfrozen committed reservation is accepted only when its stored `copy_freeze_release_receipt_sha256|copy_final_revalidation_sha256` are both non-null, the release digest selects one exact retained receipt, that receipt's canonical request bytes rehash to its request digest and decode ready `freeze_release_v1` by `copy_producer_v1` for the same source/reservation/generation, its retained evidence bytes rehash under the ready-release—not fenced-release—domain and bind the stored final-revalidation digest plus exact provisional/v3/cut/lock/headroom facts, and its `copy_commit_v1` transition receipt/request digest binds both stored values. A committed or published reservation with an installed freeze; a freeze naming a missing, pruned, wrong-state, wrong-source, wrong reservation/generation/token, or malformed fence/destroy lineage; a ready release without provisional/final-revalidation evidence; missing/malformed request or evidence bytes; a missing/mismatched committed digest; or a commit without the release receipt is registry/capability inconsistency. Recovery fences the still-active raw reservation when its identity is authoritative and otherwise latches containment; it never guesses a release from mutable last-transition fields. A response lost after ready-release but before commit therefore leaves an unfrozen provisional that is fenced, whereas response loss after the committed rename reconciles the committed record without another release or commit.

Snapshots and writer begins may resume only after no active event remains, every affected copy is fenced or exactly committed, `writer_begin_freeze_state=unfrozen_v1`, and the exclusive-source boundary is revalidated. A malformed record, missing/duplicate generation, revision or transition-receipt gap, audit-receipt/digest mismatch, wrong hash, or ambiguous namespace latches source-writer-registry containment: source writes, online backup, source admission, and readiness remain denied until reboot establishes a fresh compliant empty tmpfs. Same-boot reinitialization is forbidden because it could reuse a generation or transition ID. An event may be pruned only when clean/aborted, no reservation or snapshot can reference its revision interval, and the successor preserves `next_writer_generation` plus the entire transition-receipt prefix; reaching the first-layer capacity ceiling denies begin rather than dropping evidence.

The copy captures canonical registry snapshots before source open and immediately before and after every init, step, finish, close, semantic replay, and provisional boundary. Each `event_sha256` is the §6.12 typed digest with domain `source-writer-generation-event-v1` over every event field in the fixed order above, including its state and exact nulls. A snapshot digest uses §6.12 domain `source-writer-generation-snapshot-v1` and exact order `source_file_identity_sha256,snapshot_sequence,snapshot_revision,sampled_boottime_ms,canonical_record_sha256,event_set_hash,event_count,active_event_count`; `snapshot_revision` is the canonical `registry_revision`, local sequence is contiguous from zero, revisions/samples never decrease, and counts are nonnegative. `event_set_hash` uses domain `source-writer-generation-set-v1`, then event count and each raw32 `event_sha256` in generation order. `source_writer_snapshot_chain_sha256` uses domain `source-writer-generation-snapshot-chain-v1`, then snapshot count and each raw32 snapshot digest in sequence order.

The physical-attribution interval has two authoritative quiescent cuts. Before source open, the supervisor freezes new writer begins, waits under the immutable copy deadline until `active_event_count=0`, takes the exact pre-open registry snapshot and source manifest, and only then permits source open and releases ordinary begins. After the final `SQLITE_DONE` step has minted its terminal snapshot token and the same executor's immediate public counters have matched it, the controller installs the canonical `freeze_install_v1` successor, waits for every event that began before that successor to become clean, rejects/fences on an abort or bounded-wait failure, closes the source, and takes the post-close zero-active registry snapshot and source manifest while `writer_begin_freeze_state=postterminal_v1`. The ready release is legal only after independently replayed v3 audit bytes are accepted into the provisional successor and the worker has re-read and fully revalidated that exact successor; a failure release is legal only after fencing is authoritative. Consequently no writer event straddles either attribution cut, and a whole-connection receipt cannot smuggle operations from before the open cut or after the close cut into the copy interval. A registered writer admitted after the ready-release successor begins strictly after the closed physical-attribution interval, may change the later source, and neither invalidates nor widens the already-closed clone; only loss of the exclusive-source-access capability itself remains fatal.

The interval is bounded by those two quiescent revisions. Its final event set contains exactly every retained generation whose begin revision is greater than the pre-open cut and no greater than the post-close cut; every included event is clean with a byte-present, independently reparsed v2 audit receipt and completed header-publication chain. Intermediate active events may explain SQLite's documented restart while transfer continues, but provisional success waits for their exact completed forms. The copy orders those receipts by generation/trace/core sequence, independently chains their complete before/after manifests, expands their normalized VFS and mapped-store mutation sets, and requires the exact union to explain every observed main/WAL/SHM byte-digest, size, `mtime`, or `ctime` change—never a copy-role mutation, journal appearance, companion identity/namespace/mode change, overlap ambiguity, or mutation outside that union. An opaque digest is not attribution. Access time is not mutation evidence. `source_surface_manifest_after_sha256` is sampled at the zero-active post-close cut. Any missing receipt, unexplained, aborted, audit-inconsistent, or boundary-straddling change is `unattributed_source_mutation` and fences/destroys the destination. The exclusive-source boundary prevents an unregistered writer from hiding inside a registered interval; contradictory evidence is capability drift. A no-writer copy additionally proves source bytes unchanged through source close.

Physical attribution is not clone inclusion. Clone inclusion is fixed only by `terminal_copy_snapshot_sha256`, produced from the final accepted read epoch of the exact final `SQLITE_DONE` step below. A registered writer that publishes after that epoch begins may be validly included in the physical-attribution interval and absent from the clone. No source-close, post-close manifest, writer clean-end, finish, provisional, or later equality check may widen the terminal epoch or claim that the clone equals the later source.

The copy audit is byte-exact for every byte explicitly passed into or returned from a VFS entry point and is content-free outside volatile memory. A returned SHM mapping is a pointer-bearing exception: the `xShmMap` VFS trace binds only the mapping result, while the separately pinned semantic WAL-read transcript below binds the exact mapped control values that SQLite actually consumes. Unconsumed concurrent SHM changes are not independently fatal. Every VFS call, including a denied call, creates one trace entry before returning. Its digest is:

```text
typed_sha256(
  "ai-brain-backup-copy-vfs-trace-entry-v2",
  trace_sequence,
  connection_role,
  surface_token,
  operation_token,
  file_identity_sha256|null,
  argument_payload_sha256,
  direct_result_code,
  result_payload_sha256,
  denied_boolean
)
```

`trace_sequence` is a contiguous nonnegative signed int64 per wrapper. `connection_role|surface_token|operation_token` are closed first-layer enums. `direct_result_code` is the method's direct result, never `sqlite3_errcode()`, a masked primary code, `errno`, or a synthesized host code. Every void or pointer-returning method uses exact direct-result surrogate signed-int64 zero; its pointer presence or other observable output is represented only in the result payload. `file_identity_sha256` is raw32 exactly when the call addresses an already resolved existing leaf and is null for a missing, unresolved, non-file, or alias attempt. No pointer address, path, SQL, raw database byte, or error text is framed. A SHA-256 digest of an exact read/write buffer is a permitted content-free binding and is never accompanied by those bytes.

`argument_payload_sha256` and `result_payload_sha256` are typed hashes under the closed domains `ai-brain-vfs-call-arguments-v1` and `ai-brain-vfs-call-results-v1`. Their first two frames are `operation_token` and `payload_schema_token`. Every result payload's next frame is exactly one `output_state` token from `no_output_v1|valid_output_v1|short_read_output_v1|initialized_error_output_v1`; the remaining frames are the method-specific values below. `no_output_v1` is legal only for a method with no output value. `valid_output_v1` requires the direct result under that row to define every listed output. `short_read_output_v1` is legal only for exact `xRead -> SQLITE_IOERR_SHORT_READ`. `initialized_error_output_v1` is required whenever a non-success result leaves an output undefined; every listed semantic output is then encoded null except the explicit initialized-buffer facts for `xRead`.

Before delegation the wrapper initializes every SQLite out-parameter deterministically: integer, Boolean, flag, size, lock, and time outputs to zero; pointer outputs to null; and the complete caller-provided output buffer to zero. It never hashes an uninitialized byte. A valid result replaces those sentinels and encodes the actual values. An initialized-error result leaves or restores them but encodes semantic values as null so a sentinel cannot masquerade as a successful zero. For `xRead`, the result always binds the digest of the complete requested-length post-call buffer: `SQLITE_OK` requires `valid_byte_length=requested_byte_length` and zero-filled length zero; `SQLITE_IOERR_SHORT_READ` requires the exact valid prefix plus a zero tail whose lengths sum to the request; every other error requires valid length zero and the complete initialized buffer zero. Any method/result/output-state combination outside this registry is denied. The remaining frames are exactly:

VFS methods:

- `xOpen`: input-token-class and requested flags; result actual flags, opened Boolean, and identity or null.
- `xDelete`: input-token-class and sync-directory Boolean; empty result.
- `xAccess`: input-token-class and access mode; result the exists/readable/writable Boolean selected by that mode.
- `xFullPathname`: input-token-class and output capacity; result fixed-output-token-class and output byte count.
- `xDlOpen`: input-token-class; result handle-present Boolean.
- `xDlError`: output capacity; result fixed-error-token and output byte count.
- `xDlSym`: handle-present Boolean and symbol-token-class; result symbol-present Boolean.
- `xDlClose`: handle-present Boolean; empty result.
- `xRandomness`: requested byte count; result produced byte count and SHA-256 of the output bytes.
- `xSleep`: requested microseconds; result returned microseconds.
- `xCurrentTime`: empty input; result IEEE-754 binary64 bits as raw8.
- `xGetLastError`: requested capacity and buffer-present Boolean; result integer return and output byte count.
- `xCurrentTimeInt64`: empty input; result signed-int64 Julian milliseconds.
- `xSetSystemCall`: syscall-token-class and function-present Boolean; empty result.
- `xGetSystemCall`: syscall-token-class; result function-present Boolean.
- `xNextSystemCall`: prior-token-class; result next-token-class or null.

I/O methods:

- `xClose`: empty input and result.
- `xRead`: nonnegative offset and requested byte length; result requested byte length, valid byte length, zero-filled byte length, and SHA-256 of the complete requested-length post-call buffer.
- `xWrite`: nonnegative offset, byte length, and SHA-256 of the complete input buffer; no output.
- `xTruncate`: nonnegative target size; empty result.
- `xSync`: exact sync flags; empty result.
- `xFileSize`: empty input; result nonnegative signed-int64 size.
- `xLock|xUnlock`: exact lock level; empty result.
- `xCheckReservedLock`: empty input; result Boolean.
- `xFileControl`: numeric opcode, opcode-schema-token, and complete opcode-specific typed input; result complete opcode-specific typed output.
- `xSectorSize`: empty input; result positive signed-int64 sector size.
- `xDeviceCharacteristics`: empty input; result nonnegative signed-int64 capability mask.
- `xShmMap`: nonnegative page index, positive region size, and extend Boolean; result mapping-present and mapping-writable Booleans.
- `xShmLock`: nonnegative offset, positive count, and exact flags; empty result.
- `xShmBarrier`: empty input and result; direct-result surrogate zero.
- `xShmUnmap`: delete Boolean; empty result.
- `xFetch`: nonnegative offset and length; result mapping-present Boolean.
- `xUnfetch`: nonnegative offset and mapping-present Boolean; empty result.

Pointer values are represented only by present/null. `xCurrentTime` uses raw IEEE-754 bits because the §6.12 frame registry has no float. An unknown method, unknown file-control opcode, malformed payload, overflow, missing output state, missing output, extra field, or unclassified result is traced with `denied=true` and is never omitted. The closed malformed/unclassified return registry is: `xOpen|xAccess|xFullPathname -> SQLITE_CANTOPEN`; `xDelete -> SQLITE_READONLY`; `xDlOpen|xDlSym|xGetSystemCall|xNextSystemCall -> null` with surrogate zero; `xDlError|xDlClose|xShmBarrier -> void` with surrogate zero and a sticky denied flag; `xRandomness|xSleep|xGetLastError -> signed-int64 zero`; `xCurrentTime|xCurrentTimeInt64|xSetSystemCall|xClose|xRead|xFileSize|xLock|xUnlock|xCheckReservedLock|xFileControl|xFetch|xUnfetch -> SQLITE_MISUSE`; and `xWrite|xTruncate|xSync|xShmMap|xShmLock|xShmUnmap ->` the exact role-specific `SQLITE_READONLY*` denial from the matrices below. Every malformed/unclassified method with an output uses `initialized_error_output_v1` and its neutral initialized out-parameter shape; output-free methods use `no_output_v1`. `xSectorSize|xDeviceCharacteristics` have no input shape and always return their one frozen scalar; a corrupted wrapper state latches the audit before returning it.

The pinned source-role core uses a semantic WAL-read transcript, not a whole-region stability claim. It observes the exact local values already consumed by SQLite and never adds a mapped reread, lock, barrier, retry, or region-wide hash. The first-layer ABI freezes native endianness and alignment; `sizeof(WalIndexHdr)=48`; field offsets `iVersion=0,unused=4,iChange=8,isInit=12,bigEndCksum=13,szPage=14,mxFrame=16,nPage=20,aFrameCksum=24,aSalt=32,aCksum=40`; `sizeof(WalCkptInfo)=40`; its local offsets `nBackfill=0,aReadMark=4,aLock=24,nBackfillAttempted=32,notUsed0=36`; two header copies at SHM bytes `0..47` and `48..95`; `nBackfill` at `96..99`; `aReadMark[0..4]` at `100..119`; lock bytes at `120..127`; `nBackfillAttempted` at `128..131`; `notUsed0` at `132..135`; `WALINDEX_HDR_SIZE=136`; `WALINDEX_PGSZ=32768`; `SQLITE_SHM_NLOCK=8`; `WAL_NREADER=5`; `WAL_WRITE_LOCK=0`; `WAL_CKPT_LOCK=1`; `WAL_RECOVER_LOCK=2`; `WAL_READ_LOCK(i)=3+i`; corresponding absolute lock bytes `120..127`; `WAL_NORMAL_MODE=0`; `WAL_RDONLY=1`; `WAL_SHM_RDONLY=2`; `READMARK_NOT_USED=0xffffffff`; `WAL_RETRY=-1`; `WAL_RETRY_PROTOCOL_LIMIT=100`; `WAL_FRAME_HDRSIZE=24`; `WAL_HDRSIZE=32`; `HASHTABLE_NPAGE=4096`; `HASHTABLE_NPAGE_ONE=4062`; `HASHTABLE_NSLOT=8192`; and hash multiplier `383`. It also freezes the exact compiler expansion of `AtomicLoad|AtomicStore` and `SQLITE_ATOMIC_INTRINSICS`.

The target compile manifest requires `SQLITE_OMIT_WAL` absent; freezes `SQLITE_DIRECT_OVERFLOW_READ`, the exact `SQLITE_ATOMIC_INTRINSICS` expansion/compiler target, native ABI, and every WAL branch; and requires `SQLITE_USE_SEH`, `SQLITE_ENABLE_SNAPSHOT`, `SQLITE_ENABLE_EXPENSIVE_ASSERT`, `SQLITE_ENABLE_SETLK_TIMEOUT`, and `SQLITE_SAFER_WALINDEX_RECOVERY` absent in this contract. Enabling any one fails first-layer attestation unless its additional loads, locks, stores, exception exits, and call sites receive a separately reviewed registry. At source begin-read entry, `Wal.readOnly` is exact `WAL_RDONLY` or `WAL_RDONLY|WAL_SHM_RDONLY` because the first accepted read-only `xShmMap` sets the second bit; immediately after any successful header-read path and before reader-slot selection it must be exactly `WAL_RDONLY|WAL_SHM_RDONLY`. `exclusiveMode` remains exactly `WAL_NORMAL_MODE`, `bShmUnreliable=0`, and `useWal=0` throughout. Any other value, any unreliable/heap-index path, or entry into the read-mark `AtomicStore` branch is terminal; `source_wal_index_store_attempt_count` is first-layer instrumented and must remain zero.

One `wal_read_attempt_v1` entry is produced for every invocation of pinned `walTryBeginRead()` in one source backup step. Its §6.12 typed digest uses domain `ai-brain-source-wal-read-attempt-v1` and this exact field order:

```text
observation_sequence,read_transaction_sequence,backup_step_sequence,
attempt_ordinal,cnt_before,cnt_after,retry_sleep_trace_sequence|null,
step_pre_registry_snapshot_sequence,
read_only_bits_before,exclusive_mode_before,use_wal,shm_unreliable_before,
p_changed_before,p_changed_after,
page0_mapping_was_preexisting|null,page0_xshmmap_trace_sequence|null,
page0_wal_index_page_result|null,page0_pointer_present|null,
cached_header_before_raw48|null,
h1_raw48|null,header_barrier_trace_sequence|null,h2_raw48|null,
try_hdr_status,header_changed|null,cached_header_after_raw48|null,
shared_write_lock_trace_count,
shared_write_lock_trace_sequences[shared_write_lock_trace_count],
wal_index_read_hdr_result|null,
recover_lock_trace_count,recover_lock_trace_sequences[recover_lock_trace_count],
read_only_bits_after_header|null,exclusive_mode_after_header|null,
shm_unreliable_after_header|null,
selection_nbackfill|null,database_only_candidate|null,
slot0_lock_trace_sequence|null,slot0_barrier_trace_sequence|null,
slot0_live_header_raw48|null,slot0_unlock_trace_sequence|null,slot0_result|null,
read_mark_count,read_marks[read_mark_count] as (slot,value),
selected_slot|null,selected_read_mark|null,selected_lock_trace_sequence|null,
postlock_nbackfill|null,postlock_barrier_trace_sequence|null,
postlock_read_mark|null,postlock_live_header_raw48|null,
validation_unlock_trace_sequence|null,attempt_result
```

Sequences, counters, slots, read marks, flags, internal `WAL_RETRY=-1`, and SQLite results are signed-int64 frames; Booleans are Boolean frames; every header is exactly raw48; and named branch/status values are exact UTF-8 enum tokens. `step_pre_registry_snapshot_sequence` links the one canonical supervisor snapshot taken immediately before the enclosing `sqlite3_backup_step()`; every attempt in that step uses the same link, and the epoch/restart/lookup chain inherits it through `backup_step_sequence`. The before/after read-only/mode/reliability fields are sampled at attempt entry and immediately after `walIndexReadHdr()` respectively; the after fields are null only when the protocol-limit branch returns before that call. `cnt_after=cnt_before+1` under checked arithmetic. With the timeout branch absent, `cnt_after<=5` has no sleep; `6..100` references the exact existing `xSleep` VFS trace and the validator derives its pinned delay; `101` has no sleep, has every nullable page/header/selection field null and every related count zero with `try_hdr_status=not_run_v1`, and terminates exact `SQLITE_PROTOCOL`. A different count, delay, trace order, or later attempt fails.

For every non-protocol attempt, `page0_mapping_was_preexisting` is non-null. True requires exact `SQLITE_OK`, a present pointer, and the earlier `xShmMap` trace that established the still-current mapping. False references the exact current `xShmMap` trace, including direct `SQLITE_READONLY` with a non-null read-only pointer, a null-pointer `SQLITE_OK`, or an error, except that exact pre-map `apWiData` pointer-array allocation failure has a null trace, exact `SQLITE_NOMEM_BKPT`, and no pointer. `page0_wal_index_page_result` and `page0_pointer_present` are the exact local outputs after pinned `walIndexPage()` conversion. Any error or null pointer has `try_hdr_status=not_run_v1`, null cached-header and `h1|barrier|h2` fields, null `header_changed|cached_header_after_raw48`, and no invented mapped observation; a reached mapping call remains cross-linked rather than omitted. The exact private `SQLITE_READONLY_CANTINIT` or null-pointer branch returns before stock unreliable-SHM fallback. A replayed `SQLITE_BUSY` may still take pinned `WAL_RETRY` control flow, but the valid source wrapper never produces it, so its denied VFS entry prevents provisional success.

`try_hdr_status` is exact `not_run_v1|accepted_v1|copies_differ_v1|uninitialized_v1|checksum_mismatch_v1`. When `page0_wal_index_page_result=SQLITE_OK` and a pointer is present, the existing local `h1`, existing barrier, and existing local `h2` are all non-null. The three bad-header statuses have null `cached_header_before_raw48|header_changed|cached_header_after_raw48`, because pinned control flow does not consume or update `pWal->hdr` on those exits. `accepted_v1` alone has all three fields non-null: `cached_header_before_raw48` is the exact cached-header local used by the real comparison, `header_changed` is that attempt's comparison result rather than sticky `pChanged`, and `cached_header_after_raw48` is the resulting cached header. Thus an earlier accepted retry may leave `p_changed_before=true` while a later accepted attempt has `header_changed=false`; `p_changed_after` remains sticky exactly as SQLite requires. The observer independently replays copy equality, `isInit`, checksum over the first 40 native bytes, version, page-size derivation, cached-header comparison, sticky `pChanged`, and `walIndexReadHdr` result. It never trusts the stored status or Boolean without reproducing it.

Only exact `SQLITE_OK` from `walIndexReadHdr()` enters selection and makes `selection_nbackfill|database_only_candidate` non-null. The direct pre-lock `nBackfill` expression is assigned once to `selection_nbackfill`, and that same local drives `database_only_candidate`. The slot-zero fields are null unless that candidate is true. Its path records the shared-lock call, the unconditional existing barrier even when locking fails, the one local live-header copy if the lock succeeded, and its exact unlock on mismatch. `slot0_result` is exact `retained_v1|mismatch_retry_v1|busy_continue_v1|error_v1`; retained keeps the lock for the epoch, mismatch unlocks and returns `WAL_RETRY`, BUSY continues in the same attempt to the read-mark scan, and another error returns it. The scan is either absent with count zero or records exactly four relaxed atomic loads in slot order `1,2,3,4`; the same locals drive selection. When a positive slot is selected, `selected_slot|selected_read_mark|selected_lock_trace_sequence` are non-null even if that shared-lock attempt fails. The post-lock fields are non-null only after that lock succeeds. The post-lock `nBackfill` and selected-mark loads are each assigned once to locals used by both SQLite and the transcript. Because C short-circuiting skips the live-header comparison when the selected mark changed, `postlock_live_header_raw48` is null exactly in that branch. Otherwise the fork copies live header 0 once to a local used by the real comparison and observer. `validation_unlock_trace_sequence` is non-null exactly when post-lock validation abandons the acquired positive-slot lock; successful validation retains it for the epoch. Every other acquired lock that is abandoned has its exact unlock trace.

Bad-header and transient-map control flow remains replayable. `shared_write_lock_trace_sequences` contains the shared `WAL_WRITE_LOCK` probe and, only on success, its unlock; `recover_lock_trace_sequences` independently contains the shared `WAL_RECOVER_LOCK` probe and, only on success, its unlock. Both counts and all results are replayed in exact VFS order. The first attempt of each `read_transaction_sequence` has `cnt_before=0`, `attempt_ordinal=0`, and `p_changed_before=false`; each successor's `cnt_before|attempt_ordinal|p_changed_before` equals the prior attempt's `cnt_after|attempt_ordinal+1|p_changed_after`, and `p_changed_after` can change only false to true. Retry delay state is equally contiguous; every `WAL_RETRY` has exactly one successor, with the closed count-101 protocol branch as the terminal successor after the hundredth retry. The wrapper's final `header_changed` output equals the final attempt's `p_changed_after`. The dedicated source role admits no recovery, blocking-lock, unreliable-SHM, snapshot, or read-mark-store branch.

A `wal_read_epoch_v1` entry is opened only after one attempt passes post-lock validation and the final begin-read wrapper returns `SQLITE_OK`. Its §6.12 typed digest uses domain `ai-brain-source-wal-read-epoch-v1` and exact order:

```text
observation_sequence,epoch_sequence,read_transaction_sequence,
backup_step_sequence,successful_attempt_observation_sequence,
snapshot_header_raw48,read_lock_slot,read_mark|null,min_frame|null,
ignore_wal,lookup_count,terminal_token_sequence|null,
backup_step_result,epoch_end_reason,unlock_trace_sequence
```

The epoch's `observation_sequence` is reserved immediately after the successful begin-read wrapper and before any changed-header restart or lookup; its entry is finalized only at step exit once its lookup count, result, terminal link, and unlock trace are known. `read_lock_slot=0` requires `ignore_wal=true` and null read mark/min frame. Its `sqlite3WalFindFrame()` calls are still recorded and perform no hash-region mapping or probe: C short-circuit precedence requires zero-region `empty_snapshot_v1` whenever `snapshot_i_last=0`, including a retained slot-zero zero-frame snapshot, and zero-region `ignore_wal_v1` only when `snapshot_i_last>0`. A slot in `1..4` requires the exact selected mark, `min_frame=postlock_nbackfill+1`, `ignore_wal=false`, and the snapshot's `mxFrame` decoded from raw48. The epoch authorizes a successful changed-header restart and all lookups only while that exact shared lock is held; an error-result restart has no accepted epoch and occurs after the failed begin-read wrapper. `epoch_end_reason` is exact `backup_step_exit_v1`; the unlock trace must release the same slot after all lookups and, for the final step, after the terminal token. No cached header, lookup, or snapshot authority survives the unlock.

Every pinned `sqlite3WalFindFrame()` call in the source role contributes one `wal_frame_lookup_v1` entry. Its §6.12 typed digest uses domain `ai-brain-source-wal-frame-lookup-v1` and exact order:

```text
observation_sequence,epoch_sequence,lookup_sequence,lookup_call_site,
database_page_number,snapshot_i_last,snapshot_min_frame|null,lookup_branch,
region_count,
for each visited region in actual order:
  region_index,mapping_was_preexisting,
  xShmMap_trace_sequence|null,wal_hash_get_result,region_pointer_present,
  probe_count,
  for each probe in actual order:
    hash_slot_index,iH,aPgno_index|null,aPgno_value|null,
result_code,result_frame|null,
page_read_surface_token|null,page_read_trace_sequence|null
```

The closed source-role call site is `read_db_page_v1`; mmap is sealed to zero and membership-only direct-overflow lookups are unreachable in the generated role call set. `lookup_branch` is exact `empty_snapshot_v1|ignore_wal_v1|hash_probe_v1`: C short-circuit precedence selects `empty_snapshot_v1` whenever `snapshot_i_last=0`, selects `ignore_wal_v1` only when that value is positive and the epoch holds slot zero, and otherwise selects `hash_probe_v1`. The first two branches have zero regions, null `snapshot_min_frame`, exact `SQLITE_OK`, and `result_frame=0`; they remain calls/events and are cross-bound to the ensuing main-file page read. The hash branch requires a positive-lock epoch and its exact non-null min frame. A preexisting region references the earlier VFS entry that established its still-current mapping and requires exact `SQLITE_OK` plus a present pointer. A newly requested region references the exact current `xShmMap` trace whether it yields the accepted read-only pointer, a null pointer, or an error, except that exact pre-map pointer-array allocation failure has a null trace, exact `SQLITE_NOMEM_BKPT`, and no pointer. `wal_hash_get_result|region_pointer_present` are the exact local outputs after pinned `walHashGet()`/`walIndexPage()` conversion. A failed region has its exact error or null-pointer state and zero probes. Each successful region records every relaxed atomic hash-slot load, including the terminating `iH=0`. For nonzero `iH`, the validator derives `iFrame=iH+iZero`; `aPgno_index|value` are non-null only when short-circuit evaluation actually consumes `aPgno[iH-1]`, which requires `snapshot_min_frame <= iFrame <= snapshot_i_last`. It reproduces the page comparison, `iRead` updates, next-key progression, region descent, and the pinned corruption boundary on the 8,193rd nonzero probe. A match does not stop probing until the terminating zero, and the region loop stops only after that completed region when `iRead!=0`.

For `result_code=SQLITE_OK`, `result_frame=0` is cross-bound to the ensuing main-file page-read VFS trace and a positive result is cross-bound to the ensuing WAL `xRead` at the exact frame-derived offset and bounded page length. An error has null result frame and both consumption fields null, even when the caller's initialized local remains zero. A returned frame outside the epoch range, a different consumed page number, an unlinked read, or a mapped value that the independent replay cannot reproduce is fatal. A concurrent writer may legitimately publish `aPgno` and then atomically publish a hash slot after the epoch began; probes may observe those new entries, but entries with `iFrame>snapshot_i_last` are ignored exactly as pinned SQLite requires. Therefore a whole-region change, including a same-region or later-region append, is never independently fatal.

Every source-role entry into the attested `pagerBeginReadTransaction()` reset branch contributes one `backup_restart_v1` entry under §6.12 domain `ai-brain-source-backup-restart-v1`, including the initial/unattached `sqlite3BackupRestart(NULL)` call, with exact order:

```text
observation_sequence,restart_sequence,restart_call_sequence,
read_transaction_sequence,backup_step_sequence,
wal_begin_result,accepted_epoch_sequence|null,cause,
accepted_header_raw48|null,header_changed,
affected_backup_count,affected_backup_ordinal|null,
i_next_before|null,copied_page_count_in_step_before_restart,
pager_reset_sequence,i_next_after|null
```

`cause` is exact `wal_begin_error_v1|wal_header_changed_v1` from the attested `pagerBeginReadTransaction()` branch. This entry's `header_changed` is the wrapper's final sticky output, not one attempt's comparison field. `copied_page_count_in_step_before_restart` is always zero because the reset branch precedes that step's first source-page copy. The closed source role permits `affected_backup_count` only as zero or one. Zero is required while the current backup is not yet attached: `affected_backup_ordinal|i_next_before|i_next_after` are all null, and the event proves the real no-op call rather than inventing an affected object. One requires ordinal zero, positive `i_next_before`, and exact `i_next_after=1`. A successful begin with sticky `pChanged=true` links the accepted epoch/current header; an error has null epoch/header as dictated by the exact branch, even if an earlier accepted retry set the sticky output true. Every other compiled `pager_reset`/`sqlite3BackupRestart` call site is unreachable under this role's generated call set; reaching one, omitting either zero- or one-affected reset, or changing an attached backup's `iNext` without the count-one event latches the audit. Nonmonotonic backup counters are accepted only when that exact count-one event proves the reset before the affected step's first copied page.

`source_wal_read_transcript_sha256` uses §6.12 domain `ai-brain-source-wal-read-transcript-v1`, then total entry count, followed by `(event_kind,raw32 event_digest)` for every event in one contiguous zero-based global `observation_sequence`; `event_kind` is exact `wal_read_attempt_v1|wal_read_epoch_v1|wal_frame_lookup_v1|backup_restart_v1`. `backup_step_sequence` is zero-based over every step call, `read_transaction_sequence` is zero-based over each begin-read wrapper invocation, `attempt_ordinal` resets to zero per read transaction, `epoch_sequence` and `restart_sequence` are zero-based copy-wide, and `lookup_sequence` resets to zero per epoch. The audit separately binds `source_wal_read_attempt_count`, `source_wal_read_epoch_count`, `source_wal_frame_lookup_count`, and `source_backup_restart_count`; their sum equals total transcript count. Attempts for a read transaction are contiguous; every `WAL_RETRY` has exactly one successor; a successful final attempt is followed by exactly one reserved epoch; every successful wrapper with sticky `pChanged=true` has exactly one reset event linked to that open epoch, including the zero-affected initial/unattached shape; an error-result wrapper has exactly one null-epoch reset event; every lookup links its open epoch and falls before its unlock; every slot-zero lookup is zero-region `empty_snapshot_v1` when `snapshot_i_last=0` and otherwise zero-region `ignore_wal_v1`; and every terminal token precedes the exact balancing unlock. A missing, duplicated, reordered, unclosed, post-source-close, or unknown event fails. The producer retains complete transcript preimages only in the same-boot private volatile reservation memory, preallocates the first-layer/profile-bound worst-case capacity, and erases them after accepted audit finalization or fencing. Capacity exhaustion, encoder overflow, or replay budget exhaustion is sticky failure and never truncates or samples.

After the physical writer event set is closed, the separately implemented validator emits `accepted_header_match_v1` entries under domain `ai-brain-source-accepted-header-match-v1` over exact order `match_sequence,successful_attempt_observation_sequence,match_kind,accepted_header_raw48,prior_match_sequence|null,writer_generation|null,writer_publish_sequence|null,writer_publish_entry_sha256|null`. The first accepted header alone uses `initial_baseline_v1`, has `prior_match_sequence=null`, and has all writer fields null. Every later entry has `prior_match_sequence=match_sequence-1`. An unchanged accepted header uses `unchanged_v1` and has writer fields null. Every later changed header uses `writer_publish_v1`, has all three writer fields non-null, and must map uniquely, in generation/publish order, to one completed `writer_wal_header_publish_v1` entry in the v3-bound clean physical event set. Ambiguous, missing, reused-out-of-order, active, aborted, malformed, or pruned publication evidence fails. `accepted_header_publish_match_chain_sha256` uses domain `ai-brain-source-accepted-header-match-chain-v1`, then count and each raw32 entry digest in match order.

The exact final `SQLITE_DONE` step mints one terminal snapshot token only after destination finalization leaves the step's final result `SQLITE_DONE` and before the source read transaction closes or the epoch unlocks. Every copy step requires source transaction state `NONE` at entry and `bCloseTrans=true`; another entry state is unsupported. The token is:

```text
terminal_copy_snapshot_sha256 =
typed_sha256(
  "ai-brain-backup-terminal-wal-snapshot-v1",
  source_main_identity_sha256,
  source_wal_identity_sha256,
  source_shm_identity_sha256,
  terminal_token_sequence,
  done_step_sequence,
  terminal_epoch_sequence,
  snapshot_header_raw48,
  read_lock_slot,
  read_mark|null,
  min_frame|null,
  ignore_wal,
  source_txn_state_at_step_entry,
  b_close_trans,
  final_i_next,
  source_page_count_at_lock,
  finalization_n_src_page,
  backup_step_result,
  remaining,
  backup_pagecount_sample
)
```

The only legal terminal shape has exact `terminal_token_sequence=0`, `source_txn_state_at_step_entry=SQLITE_TXN_NONE`, `b_close_trans=true`, `backup_step_result=SQLITE_DONE`, and `remaining=0`. `source_page_count_at_lock` is the nonnegative `nSrcPage` local obtained after the source read lock is established and immediately before the source-page copy loop; `backup_pagecount_sample` is the in-step `p->nPagecount` value and equals it; and `final_i_next=source_page_count_at_lock+1` under checked arithmetic. `finalization_n_src_page` is the final local used by destination finalization: it equals `source_page_count_at_lock` when positive and exact `1` only for SQLite's `source_page_count_at_lock=0` new-database branch. Any other empty-source rewrite, counter sample, or relation fails. The token's epoch is the clone-inclusion authority. It is minted inside the final step exactly once, linked from that epoch by the same sequence before source transaction close and unlock, and returned as already-closed producer bytes with that step's `SQLITE_DONE`. The immediately following public `sqlite3_backup_remaining()` and `sqlite3_backup_pagecount()` reads must equal the token's `remaining|backup_pagecount_sample`; they verify but never mint or widen it. A writer publication, source close, finish, audit, provisional transition, or later sample cannot change the token.

The semantic validator is a separately implemented supervisor component with its own attested source and binary hashes. It does not call producer encoders, trust producer-computed event digests/counts/status fields, or link SQLite internals; it parses the complete canonical preimages, recomputes all framing and digests, and independently replays every header attempt, mapping failure/null result, retry sleep, lock branch, read-mark selection, post-lock validation, epoch, hash-map result, probe, downstream page consumption, pager reset, accepted-header publication match, and terminal token from VFS/writer-receipt cross-links. Only the frozen format registry and reviewed cryptographic primitive are common inputs. It also requires zero copy-source-role mapped-store attempts, zero unreliable/recovery paths, exact compile/ABI facts, and exact event/site counts. Unexplained physical mutation, a writer mutation of a snapshot-range hash/page-number entry, an unregistered writable descriptor, or a missing/mismatched clean writer receipt remains fatal; unrelated unconsumed SHM movement is not.

Each wrapper trace digest still uses domain `ai-brain-backup-copy-vfs-trace-v2`, followed by role, entry count, and raw32 entry digests in sequence order. Both operation-trace hashes and their payload schemas remain v2. The enclosing audit advances to `ai-brain-backup-copy-vfs-audit-v3` because it binds semantic transcript, header-publication matches, terminal clone authority, and physical writer attribution. Count equality is checked against the actual trace, transcript, match, and writer-receipt arrays. Success requires every VFS call to match exactly one matrix row, zero denied source calls, zero denied destination calls, byte-exact argument/result payload reproduction, complete registered semantic-site coverage, successful independent replay, and the one valid terminal token.

The complete non-file `sqlite3_vfs` matrix is:

| Method | Both copy wrappers |
|---|---|
| `xOpen` | Only the file-matrix rows below. |
| `xDelete` | Only the destination rollback-journal deletion row below. |
| `xAccess` | Only fixed main/journal/WAL/SHM tokens and modes `EXISTS|READ|READWRITE`; descriptor-relative metadata only. Unknown token/mode returns `SQLITE_CANTOPEN`, denied. |
| `xFullPathname` | Accept only the opaque fixed-main input token and return the exact non-path internal token; otherwise `SQLITE_CANTOPEN`, denied. |
| `xDlOpen` | Return null, denied. |
| `xDlError` | Return fixed ASCII token `extension_loading_disabled_v1`, never a path/system error. |
| `xDlSym` | Return null, denied. |
| `xDlClose` | No-op, denied if invoked. |
| `xRandomness` | OS CSPRNG only; return exact produced count and trace the output digest. |
| `xSleep` | Monotonic interruptible sleep; return actual microseconds. The copy loop itself never invokes it. |
| `xCurrentTime` | Read-only system clock; return `SQLITE_OK` and raw binary64 output. |
| `xGetLastError` | Return zero and write zero bytes. |
| `xCurrentTimeInt64` | Read-only system clock; return `SQLITE_OK` and signed-int64 output. |
| `xSetSystemCall` | Return `SQLITE_NOTFOUND`, denied. |
| `xGetSystemCall` | Return null, denied. |
| `xNextSystemCall` | Return null, denied. |

The complete `xOpen|xDelete` file/surface matrix is:

| Role/surface | Exact behavior |
|---|---|
| Source main | Exact requested `READONLY|MAIN_DB|NOFOLLOW`; the broker opens the frozen retained inode with `O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC`; returned actual flags are exactly `READONLY|MAIN_DB`, with no extra or missing bit. |
| Source WAL | Stock request is exactly `READWRITE|CREATE|WAL`; perform no create/write syscall, open only the frozen existing WAL inode `O_RDONLY|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC`, return actual `READONLY|WAL`, and increment `forced_readonly_open_count`. Missing/replaced WAL returns `SQLITE_CANTOPEN`. |
| Source journal race | Only exact `READONLY|MAIN_JOURNAL` on the fixed journal leaf; open an existing raced leaf read-only solely for hotness/drift classification and latch `source_surface_drift`; never recover or delete. |
| Source SHM | No `xOpen` row exists. SHM is accessed only through main-handle `xShmMap|xShmLock|xShmBarrier|xShmUnmap`. |
| Source temp/subjournal/super-journal/alias | Return `SQLITE_CANTOPEN`, denied and drift-latched. |
| Destination main | Exact precreated inode with requested `READWRITE|MAIN_DB|NOFOLLOW`, never `CREATE`; broker opens `O_RDWR|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC`; returned actual flags are exactly `READWRITE|MAIN_DB`, with no extra or missing bit. |
| Destination rollback journal, first open | Exact fixed same-directory basename and `READWRITE|CREATE|MAIN_JOURNAL`; broker opens `O_RDWR|O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC`, mode 0600, and binds regular-file/link-count-one/owner/device/inode. |
| Destination rollback journal, reopen | Exact recorded inode, read-write/no-create; an absent/replaced inode returns `SQLITE_CANTOPEN`. |
| Destination journal delete | Only after the recorded journal handle closed, that inode still names the exact leaf, and the Pager transaction is ending; fd-relative unlink returns `SQLITE_OK`. |
| Destination WAL/SHM/temp/subjournal/super-journal/alias/other journal | Return the matrix-specific `SQLITE_CANTOPEN|SQLITE_READONLY`, denied. |
| Main deletion, either role | Return `SQLITE_READONLY`, denied. |

The complete `sqlite3_io_methods` matrix is:

| Method | Source wrapper | Destination wrapper |
|---|---|---|
| `xClose` | Delegate exactly once for an opened main/WAL/journal handle; no unlink/chown/truncate. | Delegate exactly once; journal lifecycle advances to closed, with no implicit unlink. |
| `xRead` | Main/WAL/raced journal only; prezero the complete requested buffer and bind its digest plus valid/zero-filled lengths under exact normal/short-read/error semantics. SHM has no separate I/O handle. | Main or recorded rollback journal only, with the same initialized-buffer/result binding. |
| `xWrite` | Bind the complete input-buffer digest, then return `SQLITE_READONLY`, denied. | Main or open recorded rollback journal only; exact offset/length/input-buffer digest traced. |
| `xTruncate` | `SQLITE_READONLY`, denied. | Main only during the active backup destination transaction; journal truncate denied because journal mode is fixed `DELETE`. |
| `xSync` | `SQLITE_READONLY`, denied. | Main or open recorded rollback journal; only `NORMAL|FULL|DATAONLY` bit combinations in the pinned call-set. |
| `xFileSize` | Main/WAL/raced journal; return exact nonnegative size. | Main or recorded journal; return exact nonnegative size. |
| `xLock` | Main only: `NONE -> SHARED`; any `RESERVED|PENDING|EXCLUSIVE` request returns `SQLITE_BUSY`, denied. | Main only; exact SQLite graph `NONE -> SHARED -> RESERVED -> PENDING -> EXCLUSIVE`. |
| `xUnlock` | Main only: `SHARED -> NONE`. | Main only: `EXCLUSIVE|PENDING|RESERVED -> SHARED -> NONE`. |
| `xCheckReservedLock` | Main only, read-only Boolean. | Main only, read-only Boolean. |
| `xFileControl` | Only the closed opcode submatrix below. | Only the closed opcode submatrix below. |
| `xSectorSize` | Return fixed attested value 4096. | Return fixed attested value 4096. |
| `xDeviceCharacteristics` | Return zero. | Return zero, forbidding atomic-write, powersafe-overwrite, sequential, and undeletable-open shortcuts. |
| `xShmMap` | Main handle only; exact 32768-byte regions; `bExtend=0`; map the frozen-identity existing SHM leaf read-only. Pinned `walIndexPage` recognizes non-null/nonwritable plus exact `SQLITE_READONLY` as the sealed read-only mapping result, traced `denied=false`. Mapped control bytes may change under registered writers and are governed by the semantic transcript, not a region-freeze claim. Otherwise return null plus `SQLITE_READONLY_CANTINIT`; the private source-role core branch treats that exact result as terminal and forbids the stock unreliable-SHM heap-index fallback. Never initialize or extend. | `SQLITE_READONLY_CANTINIT`, denied. |
| `xShmLock` | Main handle only. Permit exact shared reader-slot lock/unlock plus the pinned read-only bad-header shared `WAL_WRITE_LOCK` probe/unlock and transient-map shared `WAL_RECOVER_LOCK` probe/unlock at their attested sites; every exclusive lock, write, recovery execution, blocking-lock extension, wrong slot/count/flag, or unregistered call returns `SQLITE_READONLY_CANTLOCK`, denied. | `SQLITE_READONLY_CANTLOCK`, denied. |
| `xShmBarrier` | Allow only while a read-only SHM mapping exists. | Set the sticky denied flag; no side effect. |
| `xShmUnmap` | Only `deleteFlag=0`, unmapping without unlink. `deleteFlag=1` returns `SQLITE_READONLY`, denied. | Any invocation denied; `deleteFlag` remains traced. |
| `xFetch` | Return null mapping with `SQLITE_OK`; mmap is sealed zero. | Same. |
| `xUnfetch` | Only null mapping/valid offset, `SQLITE_OK`. | Same. |

The closed `xFileControl` submatrix is:

| Opcode | Source | Destination |
|---|---|---|
| `AI_BRAIN_FCNTL_FILE_IDENTITY_V1` | Return actual opened-main identity tuple. | Same. |
| `SQLITE_FCNTL_HAS_MOVED` | Revalidate retained/path identity and return Boolean. | Same. |
| `SQLITE_FCNTL_LOCKSTATE` | Return wrapper-tracked lock state. | Same. |
| `SQLITE_FCNTL_MMAP_SIZE` | Force input/output zero. | Same. |
| `SQLITE_FCNTL_LOCK_TIMEOUT` | Accept only input zero; return zero with `SQLITE_OK`. | Same. |
| `SQLITE_FCNTL_LAST_ERRNO` | Return content-free stored errno integer, never text. | Same. |
| `SQLITE_FCNTL_BUSYHANDLER|SQLITE_FCNTL_PDB` | Allow only the pinned main-handle calls during the attested `sqlite3BtreeOpen()` interval; require a non-null opaque argument, never dereference/retain/copy/return it, return `SQLITE_NOTFOUND`, and trace `denied=false`. | Same. |
| `SQLITE_FCNTL_SIZE_HINT|SQLITE_FCNTL_CHUNK_SIZE|SQLITE_FCNTL_SYNC|SQLITE_FCNTL_COMMIT_PHASETWO` | `SQLITE_NOTFOUND`, denied. | Allowlisted no-op returning `SQLITE_NOTFOUND`; core falls back to ordinary writes/sync. |
| `SQLITE_FCNTL_BEGIN_ATOMIC_WRITE|SQLITE_FCNTL_COMMIT_ATOMIC_WRITE|SQLITE_FCNTL_ROLLBACK_ATOMIC_WRITE` | `SQLITE_NOTFOUND`, denied. | `SQLITE_NOTFOUND`, denied if invoked because device characteristics are zero. |
| `SQLITE_FCNTL_PERSIST_WAL|SQLITE_FCNTL_CKPT_START|SQLITE_FCNTL_CKPT_DONE|SQLITE_FCNTL_WAL_BLOCK` | `SQLITE_READONLY`, denied and `source_surface_drift`. | `SQLITE_READONLY`, denied. |
| `SQLITE_FCNTL_RESET_CACHE` | `SQLITE_NOTFOUND`, denied. | `SQLITE_NOTFOUND`, denied. |
| Pointer/name/escape opcodes `FILE_POINTER|JOURNAL_POINTER|VFS_POINTER|VFSNAME|TEMPFILENAME|WIN32_GET_HANDLE|WIN32_SET_HANDLE|GET_LOCKPROXYFILE|SET_LOCKPROXYFILE|PRAGMA|ZIPVFS|RBU|CKSM_FILE|NULL_IO` | `SQLITE_NOTFOUND`, denied; return no pointer/name. | Same. |
| Every other numeric opcode | `SQLITE_NOTFOUND`, denied. | `SQLITE_NOTFOUND`, denied. |

Every unprefixed token in the pointer/name/escape row means its exact `SQLITE_FCNTL_` constant. The closed opcode payload-schema registry is:

- `file_identity_v1`: no input; result is exact `adapter_contract,platform_target,filesystem_id,volume_or_device_id,file_id,regular_file_token,link_count,owner_identity,mode_identity`.
- `has_moved_v1`: no input; result Boolean.
- `lockstate_v1`: no input; result exact signed-int64 lock enum.
- `mmap_size_v1`: exact signed-int64 input zero; exact signed-int64 output zero.
- `lock_timeout_v1`: exact signed-int64 input zero; exact signed-int64 output zero.
- `last_errno_v1`: no input; result exact signed-int64 stored errno.
- `btree_internal_pointer_hint_v1`: exact opcode token `busyhandler_v1|pdb_v1`, argument-present true, exact phase `btree_open_v1`, and `pointer_dereferenced=false|pointer_retained=false`; no pointed-to bytes/address and no output.
- `size_hint_v1`: nonnegative signed-int64 input; result the same unchanged value.
- `chunk_size_v1`: nonnegative signed-int64 input; result the same unchanged value.
- `sync_fallback_v1`: exact `null_superjournal|forbidden_nonnull_superjournal` input-token class without reading or framing name bytes; only null is legal; empty result.
- `commit_phase_two_v1|atomic_begin_v1|atomic_commit_v1|atomic_rollback_v1|checkpoint_start_v1|checkpoint_done_v1|wal_block_v1|reset_cache_v1`: exact argument-present Boolean, which must be false; empty result.
- `persist_wal_v1`: signed-int64 input restricted to `-1|0|1`; result the unchanged value.
- `pointer_or_name_escape_v1`: argument-present Boolean plus `null_argument|forbidden_nonnull_argument` token class; result pointer-present false and output-token null. No pointed-to bytes, path, name, or address are read or framed.
- `unknown_file_control_v1`: the unknown numeric opcode plus argument-present Boolean; empty result.

The two internal hints are not pointer authority. On each successfully opened copy main handle the pinned call-set requires exactly one `BUSYHANDLER` hint while `sqlite3PagerSetBusyHandler()` wires its internal Btree trampoline and exactly one `PDB` hint after successful Btree open. The VFS observes only opcode, non-nullness, and the native order marker, returns `SQLITE_NOTFOUND`, and retains zero pointer-derived state. Independently, the pre-Btree role helper and post-Btree/preclose native readbacks require the connection's actual `db->busyHandler.xBusyHandler` and argument to remain null, its busy invocation count zero, and its lock timeout zero; no public or internal later API may replace them. Thus accepting SQLite's opaque Pager/Btree hints cannot install a retry callback, disclose a pointer, or make `SQLITE_BUSY|LOCKED` handling implicit.

An opcode/schema mismatch, out-of-range scalar, mutated output on a fallback/denial, non-null result pointer, additional field, internal hint outside its exact one-call phase/count, or attempt to inspect pointed-to bytes is itself a denied `SQLITE_MISUSE` call and latches the wrapper audit. Thus the later generated call-set checks this already-complete registry; it does not define missing payload behavior.

The pinned call-set generator executes null-init, successful copy, BUSY/LOCKED retry, fatal step, finish rollback, destination commit, and both close paths, and records every method/opcode/flag/result reached. The generated call-set must be an exact subset of the matrices above. Adding a method, opcode, flag, result, side effect, payload field, or surface is a contract change requiring renewed review, not an attestation-only update.

`copy_vfs_audit_sha256` is the typed digest with domain `ai-brain-backup-copy-vfs-audit-v3` over:

```text
reservation_id,copy_entry_record_revision,writer_generation,
source_file_identity_sha256,destination_device,destination_inode,
source_vfs_implementation_sha256,destination_vfs_implementation_sha256,
native_authority_runtime_attestation_sha256,retention_budget_profile_hash,
semantic_schema_registry_sha256,semantic_producer_implementation_sha256,
semantic_validator_implementation_sha256,
source_surface_manifest_before_sha256,source_surface_manifest_after_sha256,
source_writer_event_set_hash,source_writer_event_count,
source_writer_snapshot_chain_sha256,source_writer_snapshot_count,
source_writer_preopen_cut_snapshot_sequence,
source_writer_postclose_cut_snapshot_sequence,
source_wal_read_transcript_sha256,source_wal_read_transcript_count,
source_wal_read_attempt_count,source_wal_read_epoch_count,
source_wal_frame_lookup_count,source_backup_restart_count,
accepted_header_publish_match_chain_sha256,
accepted_header_publish_match_count,
source_wal_index_store_attempt_count,
terminal_copy_snapshot_sha256,
postterminal_freeze_install_transition_receipt_sha256,
source_forced_readonly_open_count,source_denied_operation_count,
source_denied_mutation_attempt_count,
source_operation_trace_sha256,source_operation_count,
destination_operation_trace_sha256,destination_operation_count,
destination_denied_operation_count,destination_final_surface_manifest_sha256,
source_no_ckpt_initial_readback,destination_no_ckpt_initial_readback,
source_no_ckpt_preclose_readback,destination_no_ckpt_preclose_readback,
backup_finish_result,destination_close_result,source_close_result
```

Counts, codes, and cut sequences are signed-int64 frames, readbacks are Boolean frames, and every digest is raw32. Trace, transcript, header-match, and snapshot entries are hashed in their independently contiguous sequences; writer events are sorted by supervisor-assigned generation. The transcript total equals the sum of its four family counts; `accepted_header_publish_match_count=source_wal_read_epoch_count`; every epoch has exactly one match; and exactly one epoch links terminal-token sequence zero. Both named cut snapshots exist in the bound snapshot chain, are ordered, have `active_event_count=0`, and the post-close cut's canonical source-registry record has the exact installed freeze whose immutable transition-receipt digest equals `postterminal_freeze_install_transition_receipt_sha256`; the final event set is the exact clean generation interval between the cuts. `source_denied_mutation_attempt_count` is the exact mutation subset of `source_denied_operation_count`; the WAL downgrade, allowed read-only SHM mappings, and exact shared control-lock probes are not denied calls.

Successful provisional state requires both source denied counts zero, `source_wal_index_store_attempt_count=0`, zero destination denied operations, complete semantic site coverage, successful independent transcript and accepted-header/publication replay, the unique terminal token produced before its epoch unlock and matched by the immediate counters before freeze IPC, the exact installed-freeze receipt, both quiescent attribution cuts, every included writer receipt/publish preimage independently verified, all four temporally distinct no-checkpoint readbacks true, and all three close/finish results `SQLITE_OK`. The manifest encoder freezes per-surface presence/type/identity/link/owner/mode/size/`mtime`/`ctime` plus byte digest where readable, while explicitly omitting access time. A whole SHM-region change alone is not failure; an unknown/missing/extra/reordered trace, semantic event, match, freeze receipt, publication, cut, or manifest surface, counter mismatch, site mismatch, overflow, path/content field, or regenerated expected digest rejects.

`copy_vfs_audit_bytes` is the complete bounded §6.12 frame-stream preimage under contract `ai-brain-backup-copy-vfs-audit-receipt-v3`: it contains every scalar above, every referenced v2 VFS entry/payload preimage, every semantic transcript and accepted-header-match preimage, both cut snapshots, every included writer event and complete v2 writer receipt, the complete postterminal freeze-install transition receipt/request-evidence preimage, and the terminal-token preimage. It remains only in the same-boot private volatile domain. The worker sends those bytes—not an opaque digest—over authenticated IPC with `copy_provisional_v1`; the supervisor invokes the separately implemented validator, reproduces the exact v3 digest, terminal token, and freeze-install receipt, and only then constructs the provisional successor. A validator disagreement, over-limit receipt, stale event set, changed cut or freeze, or missing preimage rejects before sibling creation and requests fencing.

Before opening its destination the sealed native operation consumes through authenticated supervisor IPC one single-use capability for the exact active `copying` reservation, record revision/hash, `backup_copy_source_v1` writer generation/identity, current mount/profile/deadline hashes, retained no-follow source and exclusive destination leaf FDs/device/inodes, the source-broker and staging-supervisor locked-directory identities plus their single-use broker capabilities, and canonical basenames from §6.3.2. The descriptor brokers transfer the two leaf descriptors directly into the role-bound native object; the worker receives no raw FD, parent dirfd, traversable path, or transferable capability. Every later fixed-leaf metadata check, journal create/reopen/delete, and parent sync is a broker operation bound to that same reservation/generation/cgroup and exact basename—not a caller `openat` or directory traversal.

The distinct destination wrapper resolves only its fixed precreated main leaf. Its exact connection flags are `SQLITE_OPEN_READWRITE|SQLITE_OPEN_NOFOLLOW|SQLITE_OPEN_PRIVATECACHE|SQLITE_OPEN_EXRESCODE`, while its separately masked main-file VFS request is exactly `SQLITE_OPEN_READWRITE|SQLITE_OPEN_MAIN_DB|SQLITE_OPEN_NOFOLLOW` and its exact returned flags are `SQLITE_OPEN_READWRITE|SQLITE_OPEN_MAIN_DB`; `CREATE`, `PRIVATECACHE`, `EXRESCODE`, URI interpretation, shared cache, and named/caller-selected VFS bits never reach that main `xOpen`. It permits only the complete matrix above. The broker's first journal create uses `O_RDWR|O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW|O_CLOEXEC`, mode 0600, with regular-file/link-count-one/owner/mount checks; a later reopen must match the recorded inode, and delete is permitted only for that same inode after close under the exact matrix edge. A preexisting leaf fails. The wrapper denies WAL/SHM/temp/alias/other-directory surfaces and every other journal basename, type, operation, or lifecycle. Every permitted destination operation is attributed jointly to internal `backup_copy_destination_v1`, outer `backup_copy_source_v1`, reservation ID, record revision, and writer generation. The retained precreated FD remains independent through copy closure.

Before init the operation requires `sqlite3_db_readonly(source,"main")==1` and `sqlite3_db_readonly(destination,"main")==0`. An attested fork-private `AI_BRAIN_FCNTL_FILE_IDENTITY_V1` operation then obtains type/device/inode or platform file ID/link count/owner/mode from the **actual main-database VFS handle SQLite opened**; it is not SQL, never trusts `sqlite3_db_filename`, reopens a path, or accepts JavaScript identity values, and exposes no descriptor or pointer. The destination identity must equal both the retained FD and the broker's no-follow revalidation of its locked-directory leaf; the source identity must equal its retained read-only FD, frozen source identity, and the broker's corresponding locked-directory leaf. Both must be regular files with exact owner/mode, the destination link count must remain one, and every packaged OS/VFS target must implement and attest both private wrappers, both fixed-leaf broker adapters, and this actual-handle operation or fail closed as unsupported. Source recovery belongs to a separately attested ordinary maintenance edge before a new copy attempt.

The native operation creates exactly one internal `backup_copy_destination_v1` connection through the private primitive. On both copy connections the `pre_btree_role_configured_v1` marker proves that the four dbconfigs, role, private VFS/token, private cache, mmap-zero, null busy handler, and zero lock timeout were installed before Btree/Pager/header work. After the frozen initializer sequence it reads back those values, seals both role registries/authorizers/VFS instances, and records the initial true `NO_CKPT_ON_CLOSE` readback. No API can clear no-checkpoint-on-close or install/replace a busy timeout/handler afterward. The complete source connection, destination connection, private VFS instances, backup object, semantic collector, terminal-token producer, and v3 receipt builder are confined to one native executor: init; every step, semantic event, and any in-step terminal-token mint; the immediately following remaining/pagecount reads and token verification; finish; connection cleanup; transcript/VFS finalization; and error reads are strictly serialized on that executor. No concurrent call, callback, worker, finalizer, cancellation handler, or another thread may invoke either connection, wrapper, collector, or the backup object. The independent validator is different code invoked by the supervisor only after those bounded bytes are closed; it never calls either SQLite handle. The operation initializes its RAII owner as `backup_ptr=NULL,finish_owed=false`, then calls `sqlite3_backup_init(destination,"main",source,"main")`. A null result reads only `sqlite3_extended_errcode(destination)`, makes **zero** step/counter/finish/semantic-epoch/terminal calls, closes destination then source with exact nondeferred `sqlite3_close`, finalizes the VFS traces, and requests fencing/destruction; it never uses `sqlite3_close_v2`. A non-null pointer is the complete init-success test and atomically sets `finish_owed=true`; until the sole finish returns, no API other than the serialized backup-object step/counter/finish interfaces is invoked on the destination connection. Every non-null-init exit, including step error, cancellation, exception, or deadline, calls finish exactly once while `finish_owed=true`, captures its unmasked result, and clears both pointer and flag before any other cleanup path can run. Neither handle may prepare caller/schema SQL, register or replace a function/module/VFS, attach, deserialize, expose a raw pointer, change role, or perform a second backup.

Every transfer call is exactly `sqlite3_backup_step(handle,BACKUP_COPY_STEP_PAGES_V1)` with `BACKUP_COPY_STEP_PAGES_V1=128`; zero, negative, adaptive, or caller-selected values are forbidden. The constant, maximum supported page size, measured maximum single-step latency, copy fixture, and implementation hash enter both the retention profile and first-layer attestation. Each call requires source transaction state `SQLITE_TXN_NONE` at entry and proves the pinned `bCloseTrans=true` branch, so its WAL read epoch is opened and closed within that step. Only exact unmasked `SQLITE_OK` continues and exact `SQLITE_DONE` accompanied by the one valid in-step terminal token completes. Exact `SQLITE_BUSY|SQLITE_LOCKED` alone may retry through this checked domain conversion: sample `B=current_boottime_ms`; compute effective-wall `E=MAX(frozen_max_observed_wall_ms,A+(B-M))`; fail immediately when `E >= copy_finish_by`; otherwise compute `delta=MIN(25,copy_finish_by-E)` and absolute `wake_boottime=B+delta`; then perform the interruptible absolute `CLOCK_BOOTTIME` wait for `wake_boottime`. `copy_finish_by` is never passed to a boottime API. On interruption or wake, the operation resamples `B`, recomputes `E`, and revalidates without extending the original bound. Every addition/subtraction is checked, and the held global-maintenance lock keeps the frozen trusted proof/high-water tuple unchanged through copy closure. No extended BUSY/LOCKED variant and no other primary/extended result is retryable. `SQLITE_OK` must report `0 < remaining <= pagecount`; `SQLITE_DONE` must report `remaining=0` and nonnegative pagecount. Those counters are samples from that most recent step, are read immediately after it and before finish, and are ignored for BUSY/LOCKED. For `SQLITE_DONE`, both samples must byte-for-byte equal the already-minted token fields; mismatch cannot be repaired by finish. A nonmonotonic counter is accepted only when an exact `backup_restart_v1` proves the pinned pager-reset call set reset `iNext=1` before the affected step copied its first page; a registered-writer interval or receipt by itself is insufficient. `T0` is only the retention-admission/deadline anchor, and source close is only the later physical-attribution cut: neither claims clone inclusion. The final accepted read epoch bound by `terminal_copy_snapshot_sha256` is the sole inclusion authority. The copy source role itself has a zero-mutation VFS audit, and no unregistered or companion-identity-changing external activity is tolerated.

Before and after every source/destination open, manifest check, init, step, poll, finish, close, semantic replay, v3 VFS-audit finalization, retained-FD fsync, broker parent sync, and provisional-transition boundary, the operation revalidates cancellation, strict effective-wall `now < copy_finish_by`, both retained descriptors and broker-relative fixed-leaf identities, the semantic collector's sticky-good/capacity state, the complete source companion manifest plus writer physical-attribution evidence through the zero-active post-close cut, thereafter the frozen source identity/namespace/type/link/owner/mode plus registry/access capability without post-close byte/time comparison, destination link count/owner/mode/sidecar inventory, mount/profile/capability hashes, and the exact supervisor snapshot/record revision/writer generation. Equality fails. The pre-open writer-begin freeze, zero-active snapshot, and manifest precede source open; the canonical postterminal freeze, bounded clean-event wait, source close, zero-active snapshot, and manifest establish the later attribution cut. Neither cut moves the terminal clone-inclusion epoch.
